In `--foliage grove` and `--foliage ground` GTAO's darkening follows leaves and blades with no rectangle around a card (by the difference against `--ao capsule`), and a GPU test checks that a card cut out whole leaves the frame exactly as without it while a solid one darkens its foot. Other families' alpha-tested surfaces are still whole in the prepass (crate docs, later milestones).
  - **Foliage** (M1 W6) takes the grid and ambient occlusion as the environment does: `foliage.wgsl` reads the grid's irradiance along its volume normals (per vertex on Mobile-Low, as a varying), then capsule AO and GTAO through the multi-bounce fit by its albedo, times its own canopy and vertex occlusion as W6 built them; translucency, the key light and local lights are unchanged. Its surface pipelines take the same four constants, so a grid arriving or leaving builds them anew with the environment's, and without them it is the shader as W6 left it. A GPU test lays a bed of leaves where the environment's ground was in the grid, capsule and GTAO scenes and finds what the ground shows, each effect included.
  - **Decision: characters are capsule AO's alone.** Both used to darken the ground at a character's feet, since the character and its outline hull are in the scene prepass's depth: GTAO's depth prefilter now gives the sky's distance to every pixel where a character (C5's character prepass depth, equal to the scene prepass's where a character is nearest, as temporal AA's responsive mask reads them) or a hull (C4's outline mask) is the nearest surface, so neither occludes the world around it, and the bodies' capsules darken it instead. Chosen over scaling the capsule down where GTAO runs: the capsule then reads the same on every tier (Mobile-Low has no GTAO), sees the whole body, off screen and behind included, doesn't swim with the silhouette's half-resolution texels, and a hull, pushed a few pixels out, no longer stands beside the character as a thin wall. On the GPU test's outlined ball, GTAO took up to 60% of the ground's light beside it, 30% with the character left out but not its hull; with both, 2%, open ground's. Environment geometry that a character only stands in front of still occludes as before.
  - **Bindings:** frame group 0 gains bindings 11–16, after C5's character prepass targets at 8–10 (the ambient uniforms, the atlas, the brick table, the reflection cube, GTAO's output, a sampler): the anime character family now binds all 16 sampled textures a fragment stage gets (its material's 8, the shadows' 2, the character prepass's 2 and these 4), and the fragment stage still 4 storage buffers, so the next frame-wide texture must share a binding with one of them. The grid's data reaches the relight as one storage buffer of words, half floats decoded in the shader (`unpack2x16float` needs a capability the renderer doesn't assume). Crate docs, "Binding budget".
  - **Stats and overlay:** `RenderStats::ambient` (`AmbientStats`): the grid's probes, bricks of brick positions, GPU bytes, keys and blended keys, bricks relit, capsules, GTAO's size, the reflection cube, and the pass's pipelines built and the last frame's dispatches. The overlay's stats panel shows them on an "Ambient" row, and its GPU table the "Light clustering, AO" row (the cluster pass and the ambient pass) against §3.3's 1.5 ms on Desktop-Low, unbudgeted on Mobile-Low. Probe spheres in the viewport wait for F5 part 2's debug views.
  - **Goldens:** every scene from before pins AO off (`--ao off`, P1's, P3's, W3's, C5's and W6's new scenes included) and loads no grid, so all 54 stay byte-identical; a testkit test checks the pins. Six new scenes (Metal goldens only): `ambient-day`, `ambient-sunset` and `ambient-night` (the arcade under its grid at 10:00, 18:30 and 22:00), `ambient-gtao` (a 12° close-up of the arcade's corners at 16:00), `ambient-capsule` (the character on the stage's stone plinth at night, capsule AO alone) and `ambient-mobile` (Mobile-High).
  - **Tests:** SH projection and evaluation and the sky transfer (`sapphire_math::sh`); bake determinism, brick placement, thin walls' free distances and key bounce (the cooker); the format's round trip, refusals and in-place reload (the assets); and on the GPU (`tests/ambient.rs`): an open grid lighting as the hemisphere does and following the sky the frame it changes, keys blending, no leak through a 10 cm wall (and the leak without the test), characters' ambient from the grid, replacing and removing a grid (the hemisphere's frame again, byte for byte), the fade before an empty brick, Mobile-Low's per-vertex read against the per-pixel one, capsule AO's falloff against its formula, GTAO's creases and stillness, its mirror symmetry (the false horizon the review found fails it), and the characters and hulls it leaves out (each exclusion fails it alone); foliage lit as the environment's ground in the grid, capsule and GTAO scenes, and GTAO seeing cards as the prepass cuts them. Headless: the relight's schedule (all at once, in quarters, or not at all), the constants that are on, the uniform block's layout against the WGSL, and every ambient constant specializing both shaders that read them; in the cooker, cards cut out of the bake (a clear card bakes as none, half a card shades half the sky, its leaves bounce green, two-sided beneath and no wall to the leak test, where a one-sided card's back is), also through the cook from a foliage material's map.
  - **Decision: nothing for work nothing reads.** A renderer without a grid or GTAO records no ambient pass, builds none of its pipelines (nor their shader module) and no reflection cube, and stages its uniforms only when they change (once). Each group of pipelines is built when its work can first run: GTAO's three with a renderer whose tier has it, the grid's light (the projection, the relight and the characters', and the prefilter where there is a reflection cube) with the first grid, beside the environment's rebuild, and kept after it. The first version built all seven with every renderer, and CI's golden runs, a new process per scene, paid for them in every scene, AO off and no grid included: WARP's scenes took 60–75 s instead of about 9, so its job timed out, lavapipe's run went from 8.3 to 20.5 minutes, and Metal's from 77 s for 56 scenes to 113 s for 60. On the M5 (debug build, as the goldens run) the seven cost about 0.75 s a process with Metal's shader cache cold, measured by rebuilding them from sources with a fresh entry-point argument (GTAO's three 370 ms, the light's four 370–500 ms), and 15 ms warm; the software backends have no such cache. With the fix, against 8291e2a (W5's base) in its own target dir, alternating rounds, minima:
    - **Pipelines and dispatches** (`--ao off`, no grid, from wgpu's resource report at the end of a bench): 29 render and 3 compute pipelines on the turntable and 31 and 3 in the courtyard, as on the base, against 10 compute before the fix; no ambient dispatch a frame, before as after (the pass never ran without a grid or GTAO). Textures 39 against 35 and bind groups 38 against 36 on the turntable: the four 1-texel stand-ins for the frame group's ambient bindings, and two groups.
    - **Startup** (`--bench`'s `startup_ms`, the stage and renderer; warm shader cache): turntable 50.2 ms against the base's 49.4 (57.1 before the fix), courtyard 55.7 against 58.5 (64.6); in debug, as the goldens run, 58.6 against 58.0 (64.7) and 64.6 against 63.1 (70.5).
    - **A frame** (`--bench 1000` at 1920×1080, release): CPU p50 137.8 µs against 137.1 on the turntable and 168.4 against 162.8 in the courtyard, inside the rounds' spread (medians 161.6 against 175.6 and 211.1 against 180.7); the timed GPU frame 1,757 µs against 1,784 and 2,582 against 2,617, the main pass 448 against 452 and 1,120 against 1,112. Allocations a frame are the base's, 471.8 and 508.2.
    - **Goldens:** each scene run alone through the testkit, the three builds interleaved scene by scene (warm cache): the 54 scenes from before W5 take 9.74 s against the base's 9.59 (10.33 before the fix), W5's six 1.69 s.
    - **With AO on, unchanged** (before against after the fix, 1080p, release): GTAO in the courtyard 536 µs against 538, its frame 3,512 against 3,447; the grid (`--probes`) 838 µs against 829, its frame 4,368 against 4,382; allocations equal (555.2, 591.9).
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench 3000` and its GPU-timed frames, 1920×1080, Apple M5, release, three alternating rounds against a release build of a560a84, before the rebase onto C5; the machine was shared with other packages' builds, benches and GPU tests, so the fastest of three is quoted with the median in parentheses: the timed GPU frame, then the ambient pass and the main pass by their own timestamps, which overlap the frame's other work):
    - **AO** (the courtyard, `--environment`, no grid): Desktop-High 3,546 µs (4,533) against 2,716 (5,664); the ambient pass, GTAO alone here, 563 µs, and the main pass 1,403 against 1,158 (capsule AO and GTAO's upsample). Mobile-High (1316×740): 2,343 (2,402) against 1,586 (2,011), GTAO 287 µs. Mobile-Low (1049×590, capsule AO alone): 445 (1,177) against 444 (1,372), within the noise.
    - **The grid** (`--probes`: the arcade under its grid from the stage's camera, and from the courtyard's, `--fov 43 --view 20.05,5.73`): Desktop-High 5,428 µs (6,343), the ambient pass 987 µs with GTAO and the main pass 2,214; from the courtyard's camera 5,302 (7,257), 907 and 2,049. Mobile-High 2,020 (4,265), 461 and 1,006. Mobile-Low, per vertex: 652 (979), 95 and 308; from the courtyard's camera 700 (1,192), 67 and 336.
    - **Foliage** (W6's grove, `--foliage grove`, no grid; after the rebase onto W6, three alternating rounds of 2,000 frames against `--ao off`): Desktop-High 4,455 µs (4,854) against 3,556 (3,776): GTAO 535 µs and the main pass 1,877 against 1,557, capsule AO and GTAO's upsample over the plants and the ground. Mobile-High 1,898 (1,934) against 1,244 (1,326): GTAO 214 µs, the scene prepass it makes run 115 µs (foliage's alpha-tested prepass, against 60–70 µs in the courtyard without plants) and the main pass 1,009 against 773. Mobile-Low (capsule AO alone) 726 (772) against 812 (1,743), the main pass 555 against 533: within the noise.
    - **Where it goes,** by switching one part off on alternate frames within a run (the median of odd frames minus even): projecting the whole sky cube every frame cost 0.2–0.45 ms, a face about 50 µs; the relight 10–50 µs; the per-pixel lookup 0.6–1.0 ms on Desktop-High, about a quarter of its main pass, and 185 µs of Mobile-Low's 490 before it moved per vertex (about 10 µs since).
    - **Against §3.3:** GTAO takes about 0.56 ms of "Light clustering, AO"'s 1.5 ms (Desktop-Low, 1080p) on the M5, with the settings both desktop tiers share; the grid's lookup and capsule AO land in "Forward opaque". Mobile-Low has no figure for the row; its capsule AO and grid sit in its 8 ms forward pass. The Desktop-Low reference GPU is slower than the M5 (§3.1), so the per-pixel lookup is the first place to save (later milestones).
    - CPU p50 on Desktop-High 230 µs with AO and 293 with the grid, against 220, within the rounds' spread. Allocations a frame go from 493.2 to 539.2 with GTAO on Desktop-High and to 573.9 with the grid; Mobile-High 374.2, 454.2 and 482.9; Mobile-Low 308.9, 309.4 and 365.3.
- **As built (M1, the WARP ambient-cost fix: `ambient`, `shaders/probes.wgsl`):**
  - **Cause:** on WARP (the Windows CI's CPU rasterizer, 1.0.21) W5's golden scenes with a probe grid took 4–17 minutes each (`ambient-day` 263 s, `-gtao` 261 s, `-mobile` 621 s, `-sunset` 628 s, `-night` 1,045 s in run 40429985109) against 4–12 s on lavapipe. Timed per pipeline and per dispatch (a throwaway CI branch, run 40437773651), it was the sky's projection alone: creating `cs_sky_projection` took 26–48 s and its first dispatch 170–432 s, and later frames under a millisecond. The shader was one workgroup whose threads added up their SH sums in workgroup memory behind `workgroupBarrier`s, in a loop over the faces drawn, then read the totals back for the transfer. Every other ambient pipeline cost WARP well under a second: GTAO's depth prefilter, a reduction behind barriers in a loop of three, took 46–88 ms to create and 53–105 ms to run first, and temporal AA's resolve, with one barrier, 150–300 ms and 177 ms. Lavapipe created the projection in 23 ms.
  - **Fix:** three dispatches in the ambient pass, with no workgroup memory: `cs_sky_projection` (a workgroup a projected face, each thread summing every 64th texel from its index, as before) writes the threads' sums to the pass's own buffer (55 KB, group 3 binding 8 of the projection's layout, made with the grid's other light work); `cs_sky_projection_faces` (a thread per face and coefficient) adds each face's 64 sums in the tree the workgroup's reduction used and keeps them in the sky light's faces; `cs_sky_light` totals the six faces and writes the scale, the radiance and the transfer as before. The additions happen in the same order, so the sky light is the same to the bit: a GPU readback of the sky light, the relit atlas and the reflection cube's mips over twelve frames of a grid with three keys, an animated sky and GTAO matched W5's byte for byte on Metal, and every golden is byte-identical. A frame that projects no face (the authored ambient jumped) runs the sky light's dispatch alone. The grid's light builds five pipelines (six with the prefilter), and a frame that projects makes two more dispatches.
  - **Cost on an Apple M5** (sandbox `--bench 300` at 1920×1080, release, the projection timed as a graph pass of its own in local measurement builds of both, minima of 8 alternating rounds against 439c5fc, the base the fix was made on): Desktop-High in the arcade at 10:00 54.4 µs against 196.8, at 18:30 53.8 against 197.2; Mobile-High 54.7 against 189.2. Both project one face a frame. The relight's code is unchanged, and whole-frame GPU time moved within the rounds' spread (Desktop-High minima 3,687 µs against 3,677).
  - **Kept as they were:** GTAO's depth prefilter and temporal AA's resolve keep their workgroup memory, which WARP compiles quickly. Barrier-free depth prefilters were measured and rejected: a thread per 2 × 2 texels (mips 0 and 1), then a thread per mip-3 texel (mips 2 and 3), cost 47 µs more on Desktop-High and 22 µs more on Mobile-High, and a thread per texel for mip 0, then a thread per mip-3 texel reading mip 0's 8 × 8, 120 and 74 µs more (GTAO's pass 517 µs and 205 µs on base); the first also moved two of mip 1's texels by an ulp on Metal, where fast math divided differently.
- **As built (M1 A7, `sapphire_assets::character_maps`):** the layouts of the authored maps the anime character shader reads, in one module shared with the pipeline's map tools (§5.27), encodings both ways:
  - **ILM maps** (linear RGBA): R specular mask, G shadow bias (0.5 neutral, ≥ 0.95 always lit, ≤ 0.05 always in shadow), B specular threshold, A material ID: the order M0's shader comments already named. There are 8 material IDs (`MATERIAL_IDS`); ID *k* is stored as *k*/7 and decoded by rounding, which tolerates ±1/14, more than BC7 or ASTC 4×4 move a texel. IDs blend under linear filtering and in the ILM class's mips, so the shader reads them from level 0 unfiltered (`textureLoad`), or the map cooks without mips.
  - **Ramps** (RGBA8 sRGB): 16 rows of equal height, the day ramps of IDs 0–7 then their night ramps; `u` runs from the deepest shadow (0) to the lit edge of the terminator (1). The shader samples row centers, `v = (row + 0.5) / 16`, so it needs no row height, and every ID has both rows.
  - **SDF face maps:** each texel holds the light angle at which it turns to shadow, as angle / 180°: R for a light turning from head-forward toward head-right, G (two-channel maps) for one rising toward overhead. A texel is lit while its value exceeds the light's angle in head space / 180°. The encoding is angle-linear, as the research notes recommend: equal steps of light angle are equal steps of value, whatever angles the masks sit at. Light on the head's left samples the map mirrored (`u → 1 − u`), which needs face UVs mirror-symmetric about `u = 0.5`.
- **As built (M1 C1, `shaders/anime_character.wgsl` and `outline.wgsl`, `sapphire_render::pipelines::Permutation`, `sapphire_assets::mesh::VertexFlags`):**
  - **One uber-shader, two diffuse modes,** chosen per material on a branch every pixel of a draw takes alike (the block's ramp flag). The shadow side is albedo × a tint, never a darkened albedo:
    - **Ramp mode:** the region's day row at `u = (x + 1) / (threshold + 1)`, clamped (0 where the split's input `x` is −1, 1 at the terminator), and while the frame's day/night factor (W1's `day_night`) is above 0 its night row too, blended by it; then mixed into the region's subsurface tint by the material's ramp mix (WuWa's `_RampInt`; 1 is the row alone). Rows are read at their centers through the clamped ramp sampler, level 0.
    - **Band mode:** the region's shade color, or with a deep threshold a third band below it in the region's deep shade, on the same biased input. No texture.
  - **The split's input** is N·L scaled in half-Lambert space by the occlusion, `x = N·L + (o − 1)(N·L + 1)` with `o` the vertex AO × the cast shadow, so occluded and shadowed points move into the authored shadow tones instead of darkening, and `x` is exactly N·L when nothing occludes. The cast shadow is one function, `cast_shadow`, returning 1 until R4 fills it (characters receive environment casters only). The control map's shadow bias G then adds the offset `2·G − 1` to it, the sign contract of `character_maps::ilm` (positive lights; MToon's shading shift is extracted into G as `0.5 + offset / 2`), and the sum is compared with the material's threshold; at G ≥ 0.95 or ≤ 0.05 explicit locks hold the side for any light and threshold. A neutral 0.5 adds exactly 0. The transition is the material's softness or half of `x`'s change across the pixel, whichever is wider, and never under 1e-5 (smoothstep is undefined with equal edges). Local lights split the same way, AO, bias and locks included, onto the surface's shadow color.
  - **The control map,** in A7's layout: R, G and B through the trilinear sampler with explicit gradients; A, the material ID, through a third group 3 sampler (binding 10: nearest, repeating, level 0 only), decoded as `round(a × 7)` and clamped below the region count. Without a control map the region is the vertex's material ID and the bias neutral. The specular mask and threshold are read into the surface for C7. The renderer refuses an sRGB texture in a data slot (normal, control, mask, SDF), so data maps stay linear (`RenderError::SrgbData`).
  - **Normal maps,** OpenGL-style as glTF's, turn into world space through a tangent frame built per pixel from the screen derivatives of the position and UV (Schüler 2013, with the determinant's sign restored, since WebGPU's screen y runs down): meshes carry no tangents yet.
  - **The day/night factor** is time of day's (`FramePacket::day_night`, the frame uniforms' `day_night`, W1). The shader samples the night row only while it is above 0, so a frame by day reads one row.
  - **The vertex contract** (`Mesh::VERSION` 4): the surface stream keeps its 8 bytes. Its vertex color is linear data, R ambient occlusion, G outline-width scale and B outline Z-offset scale (all 1 without vertex colors), and its fourth byte is `VertexFlags`: the material ID in bits 0–2, the region of a material without a control map; bits 3–7 are reserved for faces and eyes and must be zero (`MeshError::ReservedFlags`). The vertex shader rounds the unorm8 byte back to its bits and passes them on flat. The glTF importer (version 5) reads `COLOR_0` RGB as that data, as linear as glTF stores it, and a `_MATERIAL_ID` attribute (float holding whole numbers, or unsigned byte or short) into the flags. The sample mannequin's head moved its outline width from alpha to green; nothing else in it changed. The outline hull takes the vertex's region (width scale and color), scales its width by G, and is pushed away from the camera along the view ray by the material's Z offset × B, which hides it behind nearby surfaces without moving it on screen.
  - **Static permutations** are the plan's eight bits, in its order (skinned, alpha test, outline, morph, face, hair, eye, transparent). Two are shader variants today, as WGSL pipeline-overridable constants (`PERMUTATION_ALPHA_TEST`, `PERMUTATION_TRANSPARENT`): alpha-tested surfaces and their hulls discard below the cutoff, and transparent surfaces blend by coverage (keeping scene color's alpha) after the sky, depth-tested without writing, with no hull and in no prepass. Skinned and morphed select the f32-position pipelines that read compute skinning's outputs; outline decides whether a draw is in the outline pass; face, hair and eye wait for C3, C5 and C6. Each variant's pipelines are built for a position format when a mesh in it is uploaded with a material that needs them, or an edit moves a material to the variant (5 for a scene with all three on quantized meshes; `RenderStats::character_pipelines`); that is synchronous, so an edit into a new variant can hitch once until pipelines compile on workers at pack load. The draw list groups draws by format and variant; transparent ones keep the list's order (back-to-front sorting comes with draw sorting), and the prepasses don't cut alpha-tested ones out until something reads them. A material edit that changes the permutation rebuilds the draw list once; other edits still upload one slot and nothing else. `RenderStats` counts surface draws by variant and outline draws, and the overlay shows them.
  - **Golden images:** the seven earlier scenes (W1's `turntable-sunset` included) render pixel-identical captures on Metal (0 of 230,400 pixels differ in each; all at mean, p99 and max 0.0000 FLIP), the mannequin's math being preserved exactly where it has no maps. A new scene, `turntable-swatches` (`--swatches`), puts three spheres beside the turntable: ramp mode over a ramp and control map generated by the pipeline's `samples` module and cooked by A7's tools (four material IDs in latitude bands, each with its own ramp row; a shadow bias striped around the sphere, so the terminator jogs, with a painted shadow on the lit side and an always-lit patch on the shadow side), the same with a 35% subsurface mix, and three bands in per-ID colors from vertex material IDs. The three materials are content as well (material documents the `samples` module writes, naming the maps by ID), and the sandbox loads them by ID from the shared pack, maps included, through the asset server (A4); the spheres are procedural, as the skinned stalk is. Only its Metal golden is committed; lavapipe and WARP come from CI. On the iOS Simulator the sandbox runs it at 60 fps with the mobile cook's ASTC control map.
  - **Tests (Metal):** ramp rows follow the material ID and the night factor (day, night and halfway, per ID within 0.02 in linear color), explicit rows and the subsurface mix; IDs read unfiltered from level 0, magnified and minified, from a hand-made map whose mips hold another ID and from the pipeline's ILM cook (BC7, and ASTC where supported), never showing a blended or mipped ID; two and three bands in each region's colors from vertex IDs; the shadow bias moves the split to where N·L + 2·G − 1 meets the threshold (within 2 pixels), G = 1 lights a point that is in shadow at 0.5 and G = 0 shadows a lit one, and the locks hold past any threshold; normal maps tilt the split the right way in X and Y; alpha-tested surfaces and their hulls cut out, transparent ones blend over the sky; permutations pick pipelines and passes, and a permutation edit rebuilds the draw list once; vertex AO moves the split to where it predicts, G scales the outline (within 0.75 px of the predicted 9.5 and 4.8 px), B pushes the hull behind a wall; sRGB textures are refused in data slots. Breaking the normal frame's orientation, the unfiltered ID read, the night blend, the bias's sign, the bias locks, vertex AO or the region lookup in the shader each fails a test. Headless: the WGSL constants match `sapphire_assets` (vertex flags, bias locks, ramp rows, ID levels), and every permutation of every mesh pass specializes and validates in naga.
  - **Cost** against ef92631, before rebasing onto W1 and A6 (sandbox `--bench 3000`, 1920×1080, 4× MSAA, Apple M5, release, three alternating rounds): CPU p50 93.3–96.8 µs against 93.0–95.2, the GPU wait 500–521 µs against 481–509, the timed main pass 80–100 µs against 75–92 and the timed frame 256–310 µs against 272–278, all within the machine's noise; 305.3 allocations per frame on both (the opaque mannequin binds the same pipelines as before). With `--swatches` (three more instances, 1,764 vertices each, three more materials) the CPU p50 is 91–99 µs, 307.3 allocations a frame and the main pass 130–142 µs. Against d64be43 (A6), on a machine loaded by other builds, the CPU p50 is 95.5–95.9 µs against 94.8–106.5 and allocations are 305.3 on both.
- **As built (M1 C2, the character light rig: `sapphire_world::light_rig`, `sapphire_render::characters`, `shaders/anime_character.wgsl`):**
  - **One rig per character, resolved on the CPU.** A preset (`LightRig`, §5.21) is data: a key light that follows the world's key (the sun, the moon, or both through twilight), or is held to the camera, or a blend; limits on where it may come from (an elevation band, and an azimuth limit around the camera's side, judged around the character's head, past which a key from behind folds back toward the camera); scales on time of day's character key color and brightness; GI settings; a rim scale; and a local-light ceiling. Each frame the world resolves every character's preset for the camera into `CharacterLighting`, which `FramePacket::characters` carries (§4.6). Shipped presets: `gameplay`, the default (the key within 5°–65° of elevation, and folded back past 120° from the camera's side; GI camera bias 0.3, saturation ≤ 0.6, value ≤ 0.5, skin tint (1.12, 0.96, 0.86)), and `portrait` (the key held 35° to the camera's left and 30° up; camera bias 0.6, saturation ≤ 0.5, rim ×0.8, local lights ≤ 1.5).
  - **On the GPU:** a per-frame storage buffer at group 0 binding 3 (fragment), one 80-byte `CharacterRig` per character inside the WGSL `CharacterData`, after entry 0: the world's key light with unstyled GI (no head, so nothing spherizes, whatever a region's weight), which every unlisted instance reads (props drawn with this shader, the swatches, the skinned stalk). An instance finds its entry through `InstanceData::character` (the instance block grows from 176 to 192 bytes), passed on flat by the vertex stage. Entries follow the packet's order, so a character listed in the same place each frame keeps its entry and its instance isn't uploaded again; the buffer is rewritten whole when an entry changes, 80 bytes a character.
  - **Decision: one per-character binding.** It takes the fragment stage's fourth and last storage buffer, because every later per-character need joins `CharacterData` as a struct of its own instead of taking a binding: faces' head basis, expressions and blush (C3), the per-character shadow's matrices (R4), outlines lit by the rig (C4). Per-character fields in the instance buffer were rejected (vertex-only, persistent and uploaded only on change, where rigs change every frame the camera moves), as were flat varyings (bandwidth per vertex on tilers) and a fixed-size uniform array (a hard cap on characters, shared with C3's data).
  - **The key light:** the fragment shader reads the rig once (`key_direction`, `key_radiance`), and the light split, the shadow side's tone and the rim (scaled by the rig) all take the key from there; C3's face shadow reads `key_direction` too. `FrameUniforms::sun_*` remain the world's key light, for the environment and shadows.
  - **Stylized GI (`character_gi`)** replaces `albedo × sky_color(N) × 0.12`. The frame's ambient (time of day's `FramePacket::ambient`, in the appended `FrameUniforms::ambient_sky` and `ambient_ground`) is read through `ambient_light(n) = mix(ground, sky, 0.5 + 0.5 n.y)` in `frame.wgsl`, the irradiance of a sky-and-ground hemisphere, which W4 can share. Per pixel: the normal turns toward a sphere around the head by the region's sphere weight (`RegionGi::sphere`: faces and hair, through A3's region parameters, so it doesn't wait for C3's face permutation) and bends toward the camera by the rig's bias; the hemisphere there, times the rig's intensity; an HSV clamp (saturation held by moving the channels toward the value, then the value capped; hue kept, and a color within both returned exactly); the rig's skin tint by the region's skin weight; and vertex AO. `sky_color` is unchanged (W2's).
  - **Local lights** keep their own directions (decision: the rig's limits are for the key light alone, and a lantern under a face lights it from below on purpose) and take the rig's luminance ceiling, which was the constant 2 (the fallback keeps 2).
  - **The contract for R4's shadows.** The rig keeps C1's `cast_shadow(in)` hook and reads it in one place, the light split's occlusion input. R4 must return how much of the world's key light (the sun or the moon, `FramePacket::sun`, whose shadow maps it renders) reaches the point past **environment casters only**: a character never shadows itself from the sun's maps, so characters stay out of the cascades the character shader samples (or are masked out of them), and the per-character shadow map on desktop and the capsule on mobile shadow only the ground and the environment (optionally other characters). The rig's key direction may differ from the sun's (limits, camera-fixed presets, shot rigs): it shades, it never casts, so a character standing in a building's shadow is in shadow whatever its rig.
  - **The default look:** at the default 10:00 the gameplay preset's key is the sun bit for bit (within its limits, so returned as it is) in time of day's character key, which W1 keyed equal to the sun at 10:00. What changes is the ambient model: the hemisphere irradiance of W1's ambient (the sky's horizon above, its ground below, × 0.12) instead of the sky gradient at the normal × 0.12, plus the camera bias (no measurable effect on the mannequin at 10:00). On `turntable`, about 24,500 of 230,400 pixels move, none by more than 7 of 255 (up-facing normals lose the gradient's zenith blue). Against the C1 goldens on Metal (FLIP): mean 0.0075–0.0081 and p99 0.10–0.11 for the five 10:00 mannequin scenes, mean 0.0101 for `turntable-skinned` and 0.0128 for `turntable-swatches`, and for `turntable-sunset`, where the character now takes time of day's warmer, brighter character key instead of the sun by design, mean 0.038 and p99 0.56. All eight existing Metal goldens are regenerated for that reason; lavapipe and WARP follow from CI.
  - **Golden image:** `turntable-night` (`--time-of-day 22`, halfway from dusk to midnight): the mannequin under the gameplay rig, its key from the moon high on the camera's left in time of day's character key (intensity 0.78, against 0.32 for the moon that lights the world), stylized GI under a deep blue ambient, and two blended LUTs. The skin reads cool pink with a lavender shade, the face lit with its split at the lower right, the outfit navy with its shading visible: not flat, dark or muddy. Only its Metal golden is committed.
  - **Golden coverage (C2 follow-up: `gi-golden-hour`, `gi-clamped`).** Under its key light a character's GI is about a tenth of its light (time of day's ambient is 0.12 of a sky-and-ground hemisphere by day, against a key of 1), and the mannequin's regions take neither the sphere nor the skin tint, so no golden scene saw the GI: with each of its steps taken out of the shader in turn, `faces` moved by a FLIP mean of 0.006 at most (the skin tint) and `turntable-night` by 0.001. Two scenes now show it alone, on the face rig: the face sample is the only content whose regions take the sphere (face, hair and bangs) and the skin tint (face).
    - **The sandbox's GI layouts** (`--faces --face-layout gi|gi-clamped`): eight heads turned and nodding every way, two in gloom, lit by the default preset's GI with the key light off (its intensity 0, so the key's color and brightness, which the night character light fix retunes, never reach the frame) and the GI raised four-fold (`faces::GI_EXPOSURE`), so it reads in the mid-tones: daylight's sky takes it to a value of about 0.46 to 0.48, just under the preset's ceiling of 0.5, and its saturation (0.2 to 0.4) stays under the preset's 0.6. So `gi` shows the GI's shading unclamped, and `gi-clamped` pulls the ceilings into its range (saturation 0.2, value 0.3: `faces::CLAMPED_GI`), greying the hemisphere's colors and flattening the heads' sky sides before the skin tint warms the faces again.
    - **Scenes** (pinned as the face scenes are: plain sky and post, 4× MSAA, no shadows, fog or AO): `gi-golden-hour` (`gi` at 17:30, whose amber sky over a violet ground is the day's strongest contrast between the hemisphere's poles, so the sphere and the camera bias show most) and `gi-clamped` (10:00). Only their Metal goldens are committed.
    - **Sensitivity** (Metal, each step taken out of `character_gi` or `hsv_clamp` in turn; FLIP mean against the gate's 0.01, `gi-golden-hour` / `gi-clamped`): the sphere 0.025 / 0.009, the camera bias 0.038 / 0.013, the whole clamp 0.000 / 0.052, its saturation alone 0.000 / 0.040, its value alone 0.000 / 0.051, the occlusion 0.020 / 0.018 (p99 0.74 / 0.63 against 0.3), the skin tint 0.040 / 0.038, and the intensity 0.234 / 0.210.
    - **Occlusion:** the face head carries no vertex AO (its vertex colors' R is 1), so the scenes see the GI's occlusion through its other inputs, which reach it the same way (`surface.occlusion`): the hair mask's B at the bangs' roots and gloom on the two gloomy heads. The vertex color's path into it is the light split's too, which C1's tests cover.
  - **Tests (Metal):** in `sapphire_render` (`tests/character.rs`), each character takes its own key light and the rest the sun, a rig from behind puts a character in its authored shade, unknown instances are ignored and counted; the rim takes the key's color and the rig's scale; GI normals turn toward the head by the region's sphere weight (0, 0.5, 1), and not at all without a head, and bend toward the camera by the bias (0, 0.5, 1), each pixel as predicted; the HSV clamp holds saturation and value and keeps the hue, and returns a color within both unchanged; skin takes the tint by its weight, and AO and intensity scale the GI; a lamp adds the same light to a rigged character as to an unrigged one, and the rig's ceiling holds it. Removing the sphere weight, the bias, the clamp, the skin tint, the ceiling, the rim scale or the per-character key from the shader each fails a test. End to end in `sapphire_world` (`tests/light_rig_gpu.rs`): presets resolved under time of day and the camera, drawn by the renderer: a backlit character keeps a lit side under the gameplay preset (the neutral rig leaves it all in shade), two characters with two presets are each lit by their own, a shot's rig turns the key along one great circle as its cutscene blends in, and the key takes time of day's character curve at four hours, brighter than the moon at midnight. Headless: the WGSL `FrameUniforms`, `InstanceData`, `CharacterData` and `CharacterRig` match Rust field by field, entries pack and validate, instances point at their entries (moved, joined, dropped, duplicated and ignored), and only changed entries upload.
  - **Cost** against c3ac568 (sandbox `--bench 3000`, 1920×1080, 4× MSAA, Apple M5, release, alternating rounds on a machine shared with four other agents' builds, load average 11–24; fourteen rounds each, of which the four with both p50s under 110 µs are quoted): CPU p50 93.5–94.6 µs against 92.5–104.1, so no measurable change (resolving the character's rig in the sandbox is about 45 ns); 305.3 allocations per frame on both, with 135 more bytes allocated (not traced: the renderer's per-frame character code allocates nothing). The timed GPU frame was 280–325 µs against 259–306 and the main pass 87–121 µs against 84–102, within the machine's noise under other agents' GPU work (one branch round reached 901 and 315 µs, as base rounds under load reached 1,842 and 600).
- **As built (M1, the night character light fix):** the rig and the shader are unchanged; the default day's night character key was retuned to follow the world's key light (§5.21 has the cause, the target and the measurements), so characters at night sit a little above the world, at a third to two thirds of their noon brightness, where they kept 80–100% of it. `turntable-night`'s key is now 0.41 against the moon's 0.32 (it was 0.78); its golden, `sky-night`'s, `environment-night`'s and `fog-night`'s, and W5's `ambient-night` and `ambient-capsule` (the night scenes with a character, Metal pngdiff max 54–104 code values on the character, the mannequin's pedestal, which is part of its mesh, and bloom around them) are regenerated, and the rest stay byte-identical. **Golden image:** `night-character`, the first character at night under the whole look (the courtyard at 23:00, nearer the mannequin, the moon's shadows on Desktop-High, fog, post's layers and TAA; ambient occlusion off and no probe grid, as every scene but W5's): the skin a pale lavender pink a step above the pillars and the wall, the outfit navy, the face's split and rim kept, clearly night.
- **As built (M1 C3, faces: the face permutation of `shaders/anime_character.wgsl`, `CharacterFace` in the per-character entry, `sapphire_assets::character_maps::{face_sdf, face_mask, face_expression}`):**
  - **The face permutation** is a real variant. A static variant is now a coverage (opaque, alpha-tested, transparent) and a part (body, face), six in all (`pipelines::SurfaceVariant`); a face material's pipeline sets `PERMUTATION_FACE` on the shared entry points, and its outline is its coverage's (one outline pipeline per coverage). The body's code is unchanged under the constant: C2's nine golden scenes render pixel-identical captures on Metal (mean, p99 and max 0.0000 FLIP).
  - **The head per character.** The face is C3's struct in the per-character entry, beside C2's rig: WGSL `CharacterFace` in `CharacterData` (`frame.wgsl`), and `GpuFace` in `GpuCharacter`, 48 bytes (unit forward and up in world space, then blush and gloom), packed from `CharacterInstance::face` (§4.6). An entry grows from 80 to 128 bytes, and entry 0, which instances not listed read, holds a head at rest. Only the face permutation reads it, through the `character` index the rig uses: no binding, varying or vertex-stage work is added, and the storage-buffer budget is C2's.
  - **The key light is the rig's.** The fragment shader reads C2's `key_direction` and `key_radiance` once for body and face alike: the face's margin takes `key_direction`, its highlight `key_radiance`, and its diffuse goes through the body's line. Face code reads no light of its own.
  - **The light in head space** (`character_maps`, "Head space", and the reference `face_sdf::HeadLight` and `lit_margin` the GPU tests predict from). With `x = L·R`, `y = L·U`, `z = L·F` and right `R = F × U` (the character's own right, −X at rest): the horizontal angle is the yaw of the light's projection on the head's horizontal plane, `atan2(|x|, z)`, scaled by `|(x, z)| = cos(elevation)`, and the vertical angle the elevation, `asin(y)`, 0 below the horizon. The map is read twice per pixel, at `u` and at `1 − u`; R takes the light's side (`u` for light on the head's right, the mirror for its left) and G is never mirrored, since vertical masks are authored for lights in the symmetric forward–up plane. The two channels intersect: the texel's lit margin is `min(R − horizontal, G − vertical)`. In the horizontal plane the vertical angle is 0, which G lights; rising straight in front the horizontal angle is 0, which R lights; so each channel reproduces its masks exactly. The yaw's fade with elevation lets a light pass over the top without the face flipping sides; for the same reason, within `|x| < 0.25·|y|` (`face_sdf::MIRROR_BLEND`) R blends toward the darker of both sides, `min(R(u), R(1 − u))`, so a light behind and above the head darkens it evenly instead of lighting one side and flipping as it crosses the mirror. In the horizontal plane the blend is empty and R holds exactly. A one-channel map reads G as 1 (A2's SDF class gives it the swizzle `r101`), so nothing on it shadows as the light rises.
  - **The split is the body's.** The margin (threshold units, light angle / 180°) becomes the N·L of a sphere whose terminator follows the face map, `sin(π·margin + asin(threshold))`, clamped to a quarter turn either side: the map's edge falls on the material's threshold, and a quarter turn of light past it, plus the threshold's own angle, is the deepest shadow. From there occlusion (vertex AO), the control map's shadow bias and its locks, the threshold and `shade_tint` (ramp rows at that depth, or bands) apply as they do to N·L, so a face's shadow tones are the body's. The edge's half-width is the face's softness (`FaceShading::softness`, threshold units) times the curve's slope there, `π·cos(asin(threshold))`, or half a pixel's change, whichever is wider. C2's stylized GI is the body's too: a face region's sphere weight spherizes its GI normals around the rig's head center, so face code has no GI of its own.
  - **Faces never take the cast shadow: the contract R4 honors.** The face permutation doesn't call `cast_shadow()`, and R4 adds no shadow-map reads to it: shadow maps are too coarse and unstable at face scale, a face's shadow is its SDF map's alone, and characters don't self-shadow anyway. `cast_shadow()` stays as C2 defines it, for body surfaces, environment casters only. An environment shadow over a face reaches it whole, per character: a value in the character's entry (R4's struct beside the rig and the face) that the face takes as occlusion, moving the whole face into its authored shadow tones at once, never shadow-map texels on it.
  - **Masks** (`character_maps::face_mask`, the face material's surface mask slot; `face_expression`, a new eighth slot): **always lit** (B: eyes, lashes, brows) holds the split at lit whatever the light and gloom; the **face highlight** (G) brightens the lit tone by `albedo × key radiance × FaceShading::highlight`, fading in over `highlight_reveal` (threshold units) from the shadow's edge and never in shadow; the **nose line** (R) is drawn, not lit: the region's outline color where `mask × |sin(turn)|` passes `FaceShading::nose_line`, `turn` being the camera's angle from head-forward around the head, and the mask is authored for a camera on the head's right (the nose's far side from it) and mirrored for one on its left, so the line appears on the far side as the head turns. **Blush** (expression R × the character's weight) multiplies the albedo toward `FaceShading::blush_color`, lit and shadow tones alike; **gloom** (expression G × weight) multiplies the occlusion, pushing the painted region toward its deepest authored shadow tone (an anime "shadowed brow") and taking its GI with it, as AO does; always-lit texels (eyes, brows) keep both their split and their GI.
  - **The asset contract:** `FaceShading` gains `expression_map`, `nose_line`, `highlight`, `highlight_reveal` and `blush_color` (all defaulted, so documents leave them out), packed into the block's `surface` and `surface_color`; the family gains texture slot 7, `expression` (a mask). `Material::VERSION` is 3 and `Mesh::VERSION` 6 with it (C2's 2 and 5, plus one; meshes embed blocks), so every material and mesh recooks. The anime character family binds 8 of a fragment stage's 16 sampled textures.
  - **Test content** (`sapphire_pipeline::samples::face`, `content/samples/face/`): a procedural anime head (`face_head.glb`: a sphere drawn into a jaw, front-projected UVs mirror-symmetric about `u = 0.5`, and a flat hair cap) whose sidecar binds its `Face` slot to `face.material.ron`: two bands in a warm rosy shade, a thin outline, skin's GI weights (sphere 1, skin 1) and the face settings, over a painted base color (large eyes with a two-tone iris and highlight, lashes, brows, mouth), the face mask and expression map, and an SDF map that A7's generator makes from 19 horizontal masks (every 10°) and 7 vertical ones (every 15°): thresholds of a sphere's terminator with a nose shadow from 38°, a far cheek that stays lit longer, and eye sockets, the underside of the nose and the lower lip shadowed early by a top light, on a flattened face. Everything is generated and committed; no third-party art. The sandbox loads the head by ID with its material and maps through the asset server.
  - **Golden image:** `faces` (`--faces`) frames a look-dev rig of ten heads instead of the character. Each head faces the camera, eyes level in the image, and is a character of its own: the sandbox lists it with the gameplay preset resolved for its head, its key light turned to the rig's angles in the head's space, and its face (its real pose and the rig's expression; `faces::character`). Top row: a yaw sweep at 135°, 90° and 45° from the heads' right, then 45°, 90° and 135° from their left; bottom row: a top light (65°), light from 60° to the side and 40° up, a blush, and a gloom with a blush (both lit from 45° and 10° up). Only the Metal golden is committed; lavapipe and WARP come from CI. Assessed visually at 1920×1080 and 640×360: the shadow edges are clean single lines; mirrored pairs are mirror images; the 90° heads split down the nose; at 45° the far edge darkens and the nose casts its small triangle; eyes and brows stay lit on every head; the top light darkens the eye sockets and casts a separate nose shadow above a dark lower face; blush tints both cheeks and gloom darkens the forehead down to the lit eyes. No head shows the nose line, as none turns from the camera.
  - **Tests (Metal, `tests/face.rs`):** the face permutation selects its pipeline and pass (one pipeline more, its outline shared; a body-to-face edit moves the draw with one draw-list rebuild); the horizontal channel's edge lands where the light angle predicts, within 1.5 px, at 30–150° from either side (the mirror), fully lit straight ahead and dark behind; the vertical channel's edge rows at 30°, 60° and 90°, the intersection with the horizontal one against `lit_margin`, a light over the top lighting both sides alike, and one behind and above lighting neither; a one-channel map (R8, `r101`) never shadowed by a top light; the character's key light (not the world's sun) taken in its head's space, turned, tilted back and forward, and unnormalized; the head being the character's whatever the instance transform; a face with no basis ignored and counted, falling back to the sun and a head at rest, as an unlisted instance does; always-lit, the highlight in the key's light and its reveal, and the nose line on each side of the camera and not at a slight turn; blush and gloom per character at full, half and clamped weights, gloom taking the GI with it except where always lit, and none for an unlisted instance; the ramp's depth across a face shadow. Each of nineteen mutations fails a test: reading the sun instead of the key, entry 0's face or the sun's radiance for the highlight; leaving the GI under gloom, or dimming always-lit texels' GI; unclamped weights; an unnormalized basis; accepting invalid faces; a fallback head not at rest; and breaking the mirror, the vertical channel, the yaw's fade, the mirror blend, always-lit, gloom, blush, the nose line's mirror, the highlight's reveal or the sphere mapping. Headless: WGSL `CharacterData` and `CharacterFace` match `GpuCharacter` and `GpuFace` field by field, `FACE_MIRROR_BLEND` and the slot constants match `sapphire_assets`, every face permutation specializes and validates; CPU tests cover the reference math, the face's packing and validation, the packet's ring reuse and the sandbox's rig.
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench 3000`, 1920×1080, 4× MSAA, Apple M5, release, on a machine shared with other agents' builds and benches, load average 13–18): three alternating rounds of c3ac568, C2's head (b349915), this branch and this branch with `--faces` gave CPU p50 92.5–93.8, 94.3–95.3, 97.9–99.6 and 100.4–102.0 µs; the branch's 3–4 µs over C2 didn't reproduce when the two were interleaved ABBA (undisturbed: C2 93.8 and 94.2 µs, the branch 94.1 and 93.2; a second set, all runs slowed by other work, C2 117.4–129.2 and the branch 120.0–125.0), so C3 costs nothing measurable without faces. Allocations stay 305.3 per frame (49 bytes more than C2's, about the turntable's 128-byte entries now changing every frame as its head turns; not traced); the timed main pass is 89–96 µs against C2's 89–111, the GPU frame 219–298 µs against 266–292. With `--faces` (ten more characters, each head 2,450 vertices drawn as a face, a hair cap and their hulls, filling much of the frame): 312.3 allocations per frame (wgpu's recording of the extra draws; the rig adds no `Extract` system), the main pass 409–416 µs and the GPU frame 597–637 µs. A face pixel reads the face map twice, the face mask twice and the expression map once.
- **As built (M1 R4):** `cast_shadow(in)` is filled: a character instance returns `cascade_visibility()` (`frame.wgsl`), the key light's visibility past the cascades, which hold environment casters alone, at its world position offset along its geometric normal, under C2's contract (whatever the rig, a character standing in a building's shadow is in shadow); an instance drawn with this family that isn't a character (a prop, the stage's pedestal) returns `shadow_visibility()`, as environment surfaces do. Faces don't call it: the face pass's per-character visibility (`CharacterData::shadow`) scales the face's split input as occlusion and masks its always-lit eyes and brows, so an environment shadow takes the whole face into its shade tones at once ("Lighting" has the pass). With shadows off the shader is what it was (the `SHADOWS` pipeline constant); no asset format changed, so `Material::VERSION` and `Mesh::VERSION` stay 3 and 6.

- **As built (M1 C4):** the outline program is `frame.wgsl`, `materials.wgsl`, `lights.wgsl`, `anime_character.wgsl`, then `outline.wgsl`: lines are lit through the surface's own functions (`light_step`, `shade_tint`, `character_gi`) and share its group 3, so a line follows its material's split, diffuse mode and the character's rig (see "Outlines"). The surface shader itself is unchanged.
- **As built (M1 C5, the character prepass and its readers: `sapphire_render::character_prepass`, `shaders/prepass.wgsl` and `anime_character.wgsl`, whose header gives the formulas; `pipelines::{Part, SurfaceVariant}`):**
  - **The character prepass** (`character prepass`, every tier) draws the opaque and alpha-tested anime character surfaces of the instances the frame lists as characters (`FramePacket::characters`, rule R4), static or deformed, position-only through each instance's invariant clip position, so its depth is the main pass's bit for bit. It writes a depth (`Depth32Float`, reverse-Z, cleared to 0) and IDs (`R16Uint`: the character's entry in the per-character buffer in the low 14 bits, 0 for none, and its part in the top two: body, face, hair, eye or brow, the material's `CharacterSurface`). Its targets are the renderer's own, persistent at the scene's size and imported into each frame's graph, and group 0 binds them for the main pass (bindings 8 and 9, with a nearest clamped sampler at 10), so it runs every frame with a view, clearing both when no character is listed. The fragment stage's sampled textures go from 10 to 12 of 16 and its samplers from 4 to 5; no storage buffer is added. R2's prepass drew skinned and morphed instances as characters; listing is what makes one now, so a skinned prop or the stage's anime-shaded pedestal stays out of the rim, the bangs' shadow, eyes through hair and temporal AA's responsive mask (below). **Mobile** keeps MSAA color and depth in tile memory, so the prepass is a render pass of its own before the main pass, its two targets stored and sampled: 6 bytes a pixel, about 4.5 MB a frame at Mobile-Low's 1280 × 590, about one draw per character part.
  - **Decision: the IDs' two part bits are the plan's "two stencil bits".** A hardware stencil would mean a depth-stencil main pass (and its MSAA resolve on tilers) for what the prepass already knows; the part bits mark which pixels are a character's hair (the eyes may show through) and which its eyes and brows (C6's hook), per character.
  - **The depth-offset rim** replaces C1's key-light Fresnel rim on listed characters' bodies, faces and hair (eyes, transparent surfaces and unlisted instances take none). Per pixel: along the normal's view-space direction across the screen, `width × region width × model scale × (viewport height / 1080) × the outline's distance curve at the effective distance` pixels (without the outlines' LOD cutoff, so the rim stays past it and with `r.outline_distance` at 0), the character depth there is compared (the comparison sampler, filtered) with the depth of the pixel's own distance plus the threshold: 1 where something farther lies there or no character does. The pixel there being the character's own scales it by the region's overlap; the Fresnel gate (`light_step` at `1 − fresnel` on `1 − N·V`) and the region's strength scale it. That mask is shared; per light, the light gate `mix(1 − light_gate, 1, lit)` and the back-light weighting `mix(1, 1 − V·L, back_light)` scale the light's radiance × the rig's rim scale × a tint (half albedo, half white, × the region's rim color). **Decision: every local light rims through the same mask**, as the key does, held under the rig's ceiling with the rest of their light: a rim from only the strongest light would pop as lights reorder, and the mask costs nothing per light. The width in pixels holds across distance and lens wherever the outline curve is flat (its default holds from 0.5 to 3 effective meters), so a rim and a line keep their proportions.
  - **Hair** (the hair permutation, `PERMUTATION_HAIR`): the hair mask's B occludes the split as AO does; its R reveals the highlight and its G shifts it. The highlight is a band of the key's radiance × the material's color and strength, dimmed on the shadow side (× 0.3), edged by the specular softness and a pixel, over one of three coordinates picked on the block (all computed, so the edge's derivative is taken once, in uniform control flow): a painted band where N·H on the region's GI normals (spherized around the head) plus the shift passes `1 − width` (WuWa, ZZZ); an angel ring where the view-space normal's height is within `width` of the shift (it holds its place on screen as the camera circles); or Kajiya-Kay, `sin(T, H)` past `1 − width`. **Decision: Kajiya-Kay's strands run along UV v**, root to tip, the direction taken per pixel from the screen derivatives of the position and v and tilted along the normal by the shift: meshes carry no tangents (C4's decision), and hair cards and strips are authored with v down the strand.
  - **The bangs' shadow on faces** (the face permutation): the face pixel steps toward the key light across the screen, `hair_shadow.distance` meters at its depth along the light's view-space direction unnormalized (a light along the view casts a short shadow), and gathers the prepass's IDs and depths there: the bilinearly weighted share of its own character's hair at least 5 mm nearer, × the strength, occludes the face's split as a cast shadow would, so the face darkens into its authored shadow tone (C3's), always-lit eyes and brows included. **Decision: one method on every tier.** The plan had offset bangs plus a stencil on mobile; the prepass runs there anyway, two gathers are cheap, and the shadow keeps the exact authored tone without another draw.
  - **Temporal AA's responsive mask, with P1's.** The mask pass is P1's, unchanged (`fs_responsive_mask`): lines' reactivity (1, stored 255) where the scene prepass's outline mask holds a hull; otherwise characters' (0.5, stored 128) where the character prepass's depth is set and at or in front of the scene prepass's; 0 elsewhere. C5 changes which instances that depth holds and nothing else: the frame's listed characters, static or deformed, where R2's prepass (and so R7's and P1's mask) drew every skinned or morphed anime character instance. A moving listed character's surface now gives up half the moving history cap however it moves, a rigid one (the turntable's mannequin) included, which kept the full cap before; an unlisted skinned or morphed instance keeps it, as the world does. Still pixels are untouched (the cap scales with motion), and the values, the lines' priority and the resolve are P1's. P1's line measures barely move with the character listed (its `tests/outline.rs` harness, the sphere listed for the run): a turning body's lines keep 107.6% of 4× MSAA's strength (108.0% unlisted), 93.7% of its contrast (93.8%) and 114.8% of its width (115.2%), changing 0.0336 a frame (0.0332); a circling camera's and an upsampled turn's move by under 0.5% alike.
  - **Eyes and brows through hair:** a `THROUGH_HAIR` face (its always-lit mask, B: eyes, lashes, brows) or eye draws again after the outlines, through one of four pipelines (the opaque and alpha-tested face and eye variants; `vs_through_hair`, `fs_through_hair`; blended, depth-tested greater-or-equal, no depth write, back faces culled). A fragment survives where the prepass's ID at its pixel is its own character's hair and it lies within `through_hair.distance` behind that hair; its alpha is the opacity × the mask × `min` of the yaw and pitch fades, each `1 − (1 − cos θ) / (1 − cos fade)` of the camera's angle from head-forward around the head's up and right axes (the character's `CharacterFace` head). It writes the hair's depth × 1.002 (nearer, reverse-Z), so whatever hides the hair hides it too. **Fog** (M1 W3): its color ends in `fogged` at the eye's own position with its character's share (`fog_share`), as the surface's does, so it blends over hair fogged at the hair's distance; the rim and the hair's highlight are lit before the surface shader's one `fogged`. C6 layers eyes on this: the eye permutation (always lit, going into an environment shadow with the head, no rim or cast shadow) and its through-hair pipeline.
  - **Variants:** a part is body, face, hair or eye (`pipelines::Part`); surface variants are three coverages × five shadings (the environment and the four parts), fifteen, and W3's fog cards, sixteen in all, each built per position format when first needed; hair and eye are real permutation bits now, still eight in all (a unit test). `RenderStats` counts hair and eye draws, and `character_prepass` (`CharacterPrepassStats`): the prepass's draws, the characters whose face casts the bangs' shadow and the draws through hair. The overlay shows them, and its GPU budgets a "Culling + prepass" row: both prepasses' time against §3.3's 1.5 ms. The sandbox's bench logs `gpu_character_prepass_us` and its draws.
  - **Test content:** the face test head (`samples::face`) gains a hair cap material with an angel ring, a fringe of nine pointed strands 12 mm off the forehead over the brows and the eyes' tops in a bangs material with a painted band and a generated hair mask, and brows as meshes in an eye material drawn through the bangs at 0.7; the face material casts the bangs' shadow and draws its eyes through them at 0.5. The mannequin's arms are its skin's second region, whose rim stays off the torso (`rim_regions` extras, overlap 0), and its outfit has ZZZ's back-light weighting. All generated and committed.
  - **Sandbox:** `--face-layout lights|hair|highlights|turn` with `--faces` (C3's rig; keys around the front and above for the bangs' shadow; heads nodding under one key for the highlights; heads turning and nodding from the camera for eyes through the bangs), and `--rim-rig angles|backlit`, a row of the character without its pedestal before dark and light panels, a seam behind each figure, each lit from its own angle around it (`sandbox::rims`).
  - **Golden images (Metal):** six new scenes, pinned like the character's earlier ones (`--plain-sky`, `--plain-post`, 4× MSAA, shadows off) and, like every scene but W3's `fog-*`, `--fog off`: `rim-angles` (keys from the front, 75° to the side, 140° behind to the side and −110° across), `rim-backlit` (keys from behind at 10°–55° up), `hair-highlights`, `hair-shadow`, `hair-shadow-mobile` (Mobile-Low) and `eyes-through-hair`; 49 scenes in all. Every earlier scene changed, since every one shows a character's rim. Against a560a84's goldens (W3 on P1; FLIP mean / p99):
    - **The rim:** the mannequin's still scenes moved by 0.0019–0.0055 / 0.063–0.158 (the old Fresnel rim lit whole surfaces at grazing angles; the new one lines silhouettes), `outlines-telephoto` by 0.0114 / 0.245, past its mean's threshold (its pedestals' tops, lit whole at the telephoto's grazing angle, lost that rim); P3's three screen effect scenes by 0.0040–0.0054 / 0.120–0.155 (the brighter silhouettes cross the impact frame's duotone threshold at about 30 edge pixels); W3's seven fog scenes by 0.0017–0.0059 / 0.055–0.141, all on the characters (`fog-characters`, the outline row, most). Every still scene's numbers equal, to four places, those measured against 5955ca4 before W3 and P1 landed: the rebase adds nothing to them.
    - **Hair, bangs and brows:** `faces` and `shadows-faces` by 0.063 / 0.898 and 0.060 / 0.906, the head having gained them.
    - **P1's moving scenes,** the rim and the responsive mask's characters: `turntable-taa-motion` by 0.0035 / 0.110, `outlines-turning-taa` 0.0052 / 0.139 and `outlines-turning-taa-upsampled` 0.0064 / 0.157. With characters' reactivity held to 0 for the experiment, as the unlisted-and-rigid mannequin had it before, the rim alone moves them by 0.0030 / 0.098, 0.0041 / 0.122 and 0.0048 / 0.130; the rest is the turning mannequin's surface now giving up half the moving cap (1.4–2.7% of pixels, by 2.5–3.0 code values on average where they differ). The still TAA scenes (`turntable-taa`, `turntable-taa-upsampled`) moved by the rim alone, 0.0040 / 0.12.
    - Nothing else moved. All 43 earlier Metal goldens were regenerated; lavapipe's and WARP's come from CI: bootstrapped for the six new scenes and taken from the captures for the three past their thresholds, the rest re-baselined from a passing run.
  - **Tests (Metal, `tests/rim_hair.rs`):** the rim's width in pixels at 3, 6 and 12 m through 38°, 20° and 10° lenses (within 1.5 px of the predicted 9.5) while the ball's size on screen changes, and a region's width scale halving it; the light gate keeping it off the shadow side, and at 0.5 halving it there; a region's strength turning it off; a region's overlap keeping it off the character's own step but not another character's; the bangs' shadow below a patch of hair for a key from above, beside it for one from the side, absent for one along the view and at distance 0, with the counters; eyes through hair at half opacity facing, faded as predicted at 35° and −50° of yaw and 25° and −30° of pitch, gone past the fades and at opacity 0, never through another character's hair, the character's own skin or hair past the distance, and drawn whole uncovered; under a haze of which characters take three quarters, the hair and the eye through it are `Fog::ray`'s fogged colors at their own distances, blended (a through-hair pass that skipped `fogged` fails it). Headless: the block's offsets and WGSL constants match Rust's (`CHARACTER_ID_ENTRY_BITS`, the part order, `HAIR_MASK_NO_SHIFT`, the highlight styles), every permutation and through-hair entry point specializes and validates, the permutation bits stay eight, the material's v1 upgrade and validation, the importers' mappings, the samples and the sandbox's rigs. The GPU tests that listed no characters (temporal AA's responsive mask, skinning) now list theirs.
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench 3000` and its GPU-timed frames, 1920×1080, Apple M5, release, six rounds alternating with a release build of a560a84, W3 on P1, on a machine shared with other packages' builds and benches, load average 10–33; minima over the rounds; GPU times are the passes' mean exclusive times):
    - **Desktop-High** (TAA; the turntable, one character, five prepass draws): the character prepass takes 33 µs, mostly clearing and storing its two 1080p targets, about 8 MB (on Metal its span sometimes reads 90–130 µs, overlapping the scene prepass's, so the two are compared together); both prepasses 134 µs against 100 (a560a84's character prepass ran under TAA too, drawing nothing for the static mannequin), 0.13 ms against §3.3's 1.5 ms for culling and prepasses. The main pass, where the rim and the hair code run, takes 440 µs against 424, TAA 569 against 569, the GPU frame 1,689 µs against 1,670; CPU p50 144 µs against 142.
    - **Mobile-Low's settings** (1049×590, 4× MSAA): the character prepass is a pass of its own here, 17 µs; the main pass 131 µs against 135, the GPU frame 251 µs against 243; CPU p50 99 µs against 94.
    - **Allocations a frame,** taken apart with a temporary counting probe (not committed) around each pass's recording, each encoder's creation and `finish`, and `Renderer::prepare` and `record`. `prepare` allocates nothing in either build, and C5 makes no bind group or encoder a frame; every allocation it adds is the frame graph's per-pass bookkeeping or wgpu's recording:
      - **Mobile-Low, 265.4 → 297.5 (+32.1),** where a560a84 runs no character prepass. Taken apart by leaving the pass out of the graph, adding it recording nothing, then beginning its render pass without a draw: +1.0 the frame graph's per-frame vectors (the main pass's two more reads and the two imported targets); +10.1 one more pass in the graph (its boxed record closure and access list, 2; the executor's encoders, about 6; the GPU timer's entry, 1.5); +11.9 wgpu beginning and ending a render pass with a depth and a color attachment; +9.1 wgpu recording its state and five draws (4 while recording, 5 at the encoder's `finish`).
      - **Desktop-High, 459.8 → 471.8 (+12):** a560a84 already ran the character prepass under TAA, depth-only and drawing nothing, so its pass costs nothing new; the IDs' color attachment adds 3.9 and recording the five draws 8.
      - **With the draws:** wgpu's recording grows with them. On the face rig (45 prepass draws and the draws through hair) Mobile-Low goes from 296.0 to 349.5 (+53.5: 3 in the graph's vectors, 10 recording the prepass's draws, 37.5 at `finish`, 2 in the timer, 0.5 recording the main pass's draws through hair) and Desktop-High from 462.7 to 501.7 (+39).
    - **The face rig** (`--faces`: ten more heads with bangs, brows, hair caps and eyes through hair; 45 prepass draws): the character prepass takes 34–36 µs on Desktop-High and 12–16 µs on Mobile-Low, a character's few position-only draws costing less than the runs' noise on top of the targets' fixed clear and store; CPU p50 rises by about 30 µs (153–155 → 181–184 on Desktop-High), recording the more draws. **Eyes through hair** first shaded every face fragment and then discarded most: writing depth turns early depth testing off. Measured at load 5–13 by leaving its draws out, it took the main pass from 676–696 to 1,014–1,016 µs on Desktop-High (a560a84: 641–650) and from 350–353 to 609–656 µs on Mobile-Low (296–304), about 0.3 ms either way. It now tests the prepass first and shades only the quads with a pixel under the character's own hair, its pixels byte for byte the same (every golden 0.0000). **Its saving is not measured:** the three alternating rounds after the change ran at load averages of 24–82 (the main pass's minima: a560a84 482 and 413 µs, this branch 714 and 562, without the draws through hair 658 and 500 on Desktop-High and Mobile-Low, each from a different load), which can't be compared; the face rig needs a quiet machine.
  - **What remains** (the crate docs' later milestones have the rest): **alpha-tested surfaces aren't cut out of the character prepass** (nor the scene prepass; M1 C7 cut them out of both, below): a lash or brow card that is a quad of alpha-tested texels draws its whole quad into the depth and IDs, so the rim, the bangs' shadow and eyes through hair would see the quad, not the drawn shape. M1's content has none (the samples are opaque, and X1's VRM's materials all OPAQUE), but VRoid exports draw lashes and brows as MASK materials, so the prepass needs the alpha test's discard before such a character ships. Also: a rim against the environment's depth step (today anything that isn't a character counts as far behind); under MSAA, a through-hair depth nudge from the hair depth's slope rather than a fixed ratio.
- **As built (M1 C6, layered anime eyes: `shaders/anime_character.wgsl`, whose header gives the formulas; `materials.wgsl` (`character_eye_layer`); `prepass.wgsl`; `sapphire_render::{character_prepass, scene::DrawList}`; `pipelines::SurfaceVariant::EYE_LAYERS`):** eyes are layered meshes on the eye permutation, built on C5's eye part rather than beside it.
  - **Layers** (`EyeShading::layer`, §5.10): the eye itself (the eyeball, and lashes or brows modeled as eye parts), opaque or alpha-tested, drawn and prepassed as C5's eyes are; then, blended over it in order, the upper lid's shadow on the eye white, the iris and the highlights. **Decision: four layers, not the plan's three.** The iris is a mesh of its own over the eye white, as VRoid's and the reference games' are, so gaze (look morphs or eye joints) moves it across the white, which clips it; the eye white could carry a painted iris instead, and still takes the parallax. The shadow layer draws in the eye's shadow tone (its region's shade, as a shadow side does: lit forced to 0), never a darkened white.
  - **Drawing:** the blended layers are one surface variant, eye × transparent (`SurfaceVariant::EYE_LAYERS`), so no permutation bit is added (five of eight in use). They draw with the transparents after the sky, ordered by layer and then by draw (the draw list sorts that variant in place by the material record's `layer`, read from the block's `modes.w`), depth-tested greater-or-equal without writing, so a layer modeled on the eye's own surface passes.
  - **The eye depth mask:** the character prepass's IDs become `Rg16Uint`. Every part writes both channels but hair, which draws last through a pipeline writing R alone (`HAIR_ID_WRITES`), so R is still the nearest surface's ID and G the nearest surface's but for hair. A layer's fragment keeps its alpha only where G is its own character's eye part (`eye_mask`): a lid closing over the eye takes the iris and highlights with it whatever their depth, a gaze carrying the iris past the white is clipped at its edge, and nothing spills onto the skin around it. Eyes through hair now also need G to be the drawing part's own (a closed eye stays hidden under the bangs), and the blended layers draw through hair too, by their coverage. **Decision: a second channel, not a stencil or another pass.** The prepass already draws every eye; the channel costs 2 bytes a pixel (Mobile-Low's stored prepass targets grow from 6 to 8 bytes a pixel, 4.5 to 6 MB) and one more write mask.
  - **The parallax refraction fake** (`iris_parallax`, the eye and iris layers): the base color is read where the view ray through the point would lie the material's depth below the cornea, scaled by the eye mask's R, or by a dome over the material's iris ellipse, or whole. Offset-limited (Welsh): the view's along-surface part times the depth, not divided by N·V, so it never runs off at grazing angles. The offset goes into texture space through the gradients of u and v from screen derivatives (the dual basis of the position's and UV's derivatives), as `mapped_normal` finds its frame: meshes carry no tangents. The pupil shifts against the limbal ring as the view turns.
  - **Highlights live:** each drifts along a 2 : 3 Lissajous curve across the model's front (X and Y in object space, in the vertex stage) and glints, `1 + sparkle × (½ + ½ sin)^6`, scaling its color and its coverage both, so a highlight tone mapping already holds at white still swells at its soft edge. Both run on the frame's clock (`frame.time.x`, the packet's time, rule R4) at a phase per instance slot (the golden ratio's step), so neighbors don't glint together.
  - **Fog:** every layer ends in `fogged` at its own position with its character's `fog_share`, as the surface does, and so does each layer drawn through hair.
  - **Counters:** `CharacterPrepassStats::eye_layer_draws`, shown on the overlay's "Character prepass" row and logged by the bench; through-hair draws now count eye layers.
  - **Test content:** the face test head's eyes are layered meshes (§5.27): eye white, lid shadow, iris (a gradient from deep blue to cyan with fibers, a crescent glow, a limbal ring and a vertical pupil), two highlights, a winged upper lash and a lower one, and lids in the face's material that close over the eye on morph targets, with gaze morphs carrying the iris and highlights. Heads draw through the morph path with their `FaceRig`'s weights. **Sandbox:** `--face-layout eyes` (one head close up), `eyes-turned` (35°), `gaze` (left, ahead, right; up, down) and `blink` (half, closed); the face rig schedules the pose extraction when it spawns, as the look-dev stage does (M1 X1), so scenes without poses don't run it.
  - **Golden images (Metal):** six new scenes, pinned like C5's (plain sky and post, 4× MSAA, no shadows, no fog) but for one's post: `eyes-front` (the close-up at 180 ticks, the highlights between glints), `eyes-three-quarter`, `eyes-gaze`, `eyes-highlight` (at 105 ticks, the glint's height, under post's full look), `eyes-lids` and `eyes-mobile` (the close-up on Mobile-Low); 55 scenes in all. The six earlier scenes that draw the face test head changed with its eyes (FLIP mean / p99 against a3fbcd1; pixels differing): `faces` 0.0114 / 0.394 (3.8%), `shadows-faces` 0.0114 / 0.387 (4.2%), `hair-highlights` 0.0113 / 0.459 (2.9%), `hair-shadow` 0.0089 / 0.363 (2.5%), `hair-shadow-mobile` 0.0089 / 0.362 (2.7%) and `eyes-through-hair` 0.0105 / 0.373 (3.7%). The differences above 6 code values are in and around the eyes (their draws through the bangs included); elsewhere single pixels on the hair, the faces' edges and the sky move by 1–6, from post's glow spreading the brighter eyes and from the heads now drawing through the morph path (f32 positions, normals decoded and encoded again). The other 43 Metal goldens are byte-identical. lavapipe's and WARP's for the twelve come from CI. Rebased onto X1 and C2's GI scenes, four more changed: `gi-golden-hour` 0.0132 / 0.412 (4.4%) and `gi-clamped` 0.0119 / 0.388 (4.4%), the face head's eyes; `lookdev-town-hero` 0.0000 / 0.000 (42 pixels, at most 29 code values, in the eyes) and `lookdev-town-day` (3 pixels by 1), the look-dev character's eye documents on C6's layers.
  - **Tests (Metal, `tests/eyes.rs`):** the layers blend in order within the eye and nowhere else (an iris nearer than the skin above the eye is clipped to the eye); a lid closed over the eye's top half, nearer than the eye but behind the iris and highlight, hides them; the pupil appears where the offset-limited parallax predicts it at 0°, ±30° and 50° (within a pixel) and holds the center without parallax; a highlight is where the drift predicts it and as bright as the sparkle predicts at four times; an eye and its iris through the bangs at half opacity open, and nothing through them closed; the layers fogged with their character's share. Unit tests check the WGSL constants against Rust, the variant's pipelines, the block's packing and validation, the sample head's layers, morphs and textures, and the VRM mapping.
  - **What remains** (the crate docs' later milestones have the renderer's part): the eye depth mask is single-sampled under MSAA; through hair, stacked layers pale the iris a little; both highlights of an instance share its phase; an offset limit, not a refracted ray. Alpha-tested eye parts (VRoid's eyelines and brows are MASK materials) still write their whole quads into the character prepass, and so into the eye depth mask: C7's prepass discard, which its proximity fade needs too, covers them. **Look tuning** (X1 part 2): the sample's upper lash is one solid strip, where WuWa and Genshin draw lash spikes and clumps; its highlights are plain discs, where the reference games shape theirs (stars, crosses, ZZZ's hard-edged glints); there is no double-lid crease line over the eye; a closing lid doesn't lift the lower one; and in three-quarter views the far eye sits against the head's silhouette, half behind the side hair, where the reference games push it toward the camera (an offset by view angle, or a face bone).
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench 3000` and its GPU-timed frames, 1920×1080, Apple M5, release; minima over five rounds alternating with a release build of a3fbcd1, C5, at load 3–15; GPU times are the passes' mean exclusive times). The layers' own share was taken apart by a build that leaves their draws out (not committed), four rounds alternating with this one:
    - **The eye layers** (shadow, iris and highlights, and their draws through the bangs) add 74 µs to the main pass on Desktop-High in the `eyes` close-up (813 → 887 µs; both eyes about a third of the screen's height) and 53 µs with the face rig's ten heads (702 → 755); on Mobile-Low's settings 51 µs (293 → 344) and about 80 µs (420 → 501, the noisiest pair). Against §3.3's "Hair, eyes, transparents" row (0.8 ms Desktop-High, 2.0 ms Mobile-Low) that is under a tenth of either.
    - **The face rig against a3fbcd1:** the main pass gets cheaper, 852 → 748 µs on Desktop-High and 520 → 505 on Mobile-Low, since the face no longer draws through the bangs (its eyes and lashes do, a fraction of its area); the GPU frame 2,261 → 2,175 µs and 734 → 749. Its CPU p50 rises 161 → 232 µs and 124 → 187: each head draws six eye submeshes more (main pass, prepass for the eye, lashes and lids, through the bangs and into the character shadow maps, 154 draws in all), drawn from its deformed outputs (posed once, so the skinning pass skips it on every later frame: `skinned` 0). Allocations a frame 501.7 → 565.7 and 349.5 → 368.5, all wgpu's recording of the added draws.
    - **Without faces** (the turntable): CPU p50 137 µs either way and the GPU frame 1,793 → 1,779 µs. The measured build ran the pose extraction every frame, one more allocation a frame (471.8 → 472.8); rebased onto X1, the face rig schedules it as the look-dev stage does, so a scene without poses allocates as before.
- **As built (M1 C7, materials on the base: `shaders/anime_character.wgsl`, whose header gives the formulas, `prepass.wgsl` and `outline.wgsl`'s masked prepasses, `frame.wgsl`'s dither; `sapphire_assets::material::{Specular, SpecularModel, Matcap, RegionMatcap, Stockings, Emission}`, `character_maps::layer_mask`; `sapphire_render::characters`; `sapphire_world::fade`):**
  - **Layers** over C1–C5's shading, each off until a region turns it on (black specular, no matcap on the material or `MatcapChannel::Off`, metallic at or below 0.9, no stocking weight, black emission), so every earlier material without a matcap draws as it did (the matcap's decision is below).
