Each is a branch on the block's flags (`AnimeCharacterParams::FLAG_SPECULAR` to `FLAG_EMISSION`, `modes.x` bits 12–16), taken in one block before the lighting, in the variants with the `LAYERS` permutation bit (below); its light is added with the rest. A body's **layer mask** (`layer_mask`, linear, in the surface mask slot, which faces, hair and eyes hold their own masks in) scales them: R the matcap, G metallic, B emission, A stocking density; unbound, 1. Per region they live in `CharacterLayerRegion` (48 bytes; the block grows to 1,584 bytes), with the material's `layers`, `stocking_*` vectors. Material 8, Mesh 11 (after W6's 7 and 10).
    - **Highlight:** `SpecularModel::Blinn`, `N·H^size` (Genshin, WuWa), or `Ggx`, the GGX distribution at roughness `size` normalized to 1 at the half vector (ZZZ), on the shading normal toward the character's key light, cut at `1 − B` of the control map (0.5 without one) by `light_step` with the material's softness or a pixel (the shape's slope × N·H's screen change), × the control map's R (1 without one, which only C7 reads), on the lit side, in the region's color × `mix(1, albedo, Specular::albedo)`.
    - **Matcap:** by the view-space normal (`0.5 + 0.49 · (n.x, −n.y)`), the region's channel (the color matcap, or R, G, B or A as four grayscale ones, WuWa's `_MatCapTex`) × its tint, × the mask's R and the lit or shadow strength by the split; `MatcapBlend::Add` adds it in the key light's color, `Multiply` paints it into the albedo before the light (Genshin's metal map). **Decision: a document's matcap shades now.** C1's schema already had `matcap` (map and strengths) and the shader didn't read it ("Matcap reads join with C7"); a region's default (`Rgb`, added, white) is MToon's matcap, so an imported VRM's `matcapTexture` shows as it does in MToon. No committed content had one, so no golden moved.
    - **Reflection add:** above `layer_mask::REFLECTS` (0.9) of metallic × the mask's G, smoothstepped over 0.02: W5's prefiltered reflection cube (group 0 binding 14, through the ambient sampler) along the reflected view ray, at the mip for the region's `roughness` (`roughness ×` the roughest mip), × the albedo and vertex AO, on every tier. Its mip 0 is the sky at the ambient's scale (W5's projection), so it dims with the ambient at night. **Decision: a reflective character makes and prefilters the cube** (`Ambient::set_reflective`). The draw list says whether any anime character draw's material reflects (`MaterialRecord::reflects`: a region with metallic over the gate and the layers' flag); the first time one does, the ambient pass builds the projection's three pipelines (with their sums buffer, #58's) and the prefilter and makes the cube at the tier's `ReflectionSettings` size, grid or not (rebuilding the frame group once), and while one does it projects and prefilters the faces the sky cube draws, all six when that starts again after a pause (`RelightSchedule::reproject`). With none, nothing is built or dispatched for it; a grid with environment specular prefilters as before. The phase-1 stand-in (W2's sky cube, unfiltered, through group 2) is gone. The matcap stays a metal's base look; the reflection adds the world on top.
    - **Stockings:** the albedo goes toward the fabric's color by `mix(edge_opacity, facing_opacity, smoothstep(range, N·V))` (denier: dark at the silhouette, the skin through it facing the camera) × the region's weight × the mask's A, the opacity varied by a knit `sin(2π u·s) · sin(2π v·s)` × `weave_strength`, faded to its average as a cell shrinks from four pixels across to two; a sheen `sin(T, H)^sharpness` in `sheen` × the key light on the lit side, T the UV axis the fabric runs along (`FabricDirection`, from the position's and UV's screen derivatives, as Kajiya-Kay's strands are). Opaque, so nothing sorts.
    - **Emission:** the region's glow × the mask's B, unlit, breathing `1 − depth · (½ − ½ cos(2π t / period + phase))` with a phase per instance slot (the golden ratio's fraction of a turn); `Emission::bloom` × the glow's share of the pixel's luminance is written as scene color's alpha (the world's share, P2's character mask), so post blooms the glow as the world's light while the character around it keeps its restrained bloom.
    - **Decision: a permutation bit, `LAYERS`.** Compiled into every variant (phase 1), the never-taken branches cost frames without layers a few percent of the main pass on Apple GPUs (+19 µs of Mobile-Low's main pass, 18%, and +16 µs of Desktop-High's with GTAO; with the bit, none measurable (below)), and no single layer accounted for it: each compiled out alone recovered none, nor did moving the matcap's and highlight's derivatives into the branch (`layer_footprint`, a quad-uniform branch under `@diagnostic(off, derivative_uniformity)`, kept) or computing every layer before the lighting. Two of the eight bits were spent on nothing: skinned and morph select no shader variant since R2 (a deformed mesh draws through the f32 position pipelines), so `Permutation` drops them, `LAYERS` takes bit 0 and bit 3 is free. The cooker sets `MaterialFlags::LAYERS` (bit 7) when any region turns a layer on; without the bit the shader reads no layer flags (`select(0, flags, PERMUTATION_LAYERS)`) and its branches compile out. `SurfaceVariant` gains `layers`: nine variants a coverage (body, face and hair, plain or layered; eyes, always layered, as C6's eye layers share the code; environment; foliage), 28 with the fog cards; layered faces draw eyes through hair too.
  - **The proximity fade** (`CharacterInstance::opacity`, 1 by default): the world resolves it (`ProximityFade`, smoothstepped from 1 at `start` = 0.5 m from the body capsule's surface to 0 at `end` = 0.1 m, so a gameplay camera never fades a character and one pushed into it does), and the renderer draws a share of the character's pixels through a screen-space dither every pass shares. The threshold is a 4 × 4 ordered dither at the pixel (`FADE_ORDER`), `fract((o + 0.5) / 16 + turn + fract(entry · 0.618))`: turned per character entry, so two fading characters keep different pixels, and under temporal AA per frame by `temporal::fade_turn` (`ViewUniforms::dither`), the radical inverse of the frame in a 16-frame cycle, so every pixel takes each of the sixteen thresholds once a cycle; without temporal AA it holds still. A pixel is covered where the threshold is at least the fade; under MSAA, `floor((1 − fade) · N + threshold)` of its N samples are (`fade_coverage`), as a run starting where the threshold says (`fade_sample_mask`), which the resolve blends.
    - **Where the fade is:** `CharacterFade` in the per-character entry (240 bytes with W5's ambient), for the surfaces and outlines, and `InstanceData::fade` (a padding word), for the masked prepasses' vertex stage (the character prepass binds no group 0).
    - **Routing:** while an instance fades, the draw list moves its opaque anime character draws to the alpha-tested variants of their parts, so opaque pipelines never carry a discard (it would cost every opaque draw its hidden surface removal on tilers); an opaque material routed there doesn't alpha-test, by its flag. Starting or stopping rebuilds the draw list once (`fading_changed`), building the variant's surface, outline, through-hair and masked prepass pipelines then if no draw had (through `checked_build`, so an edited shader that breaks them falls back to the compiled-in ones).
    - **Every pass keeps the same pixels:** the surfaces (the alpha-tested and transparent variants; under MSAA the alpha-tested variants' covered entry points, `fs_*_covered`, write the sample mask), the outline hulls, eyes through hair and C6's eye layers (a wholly faded character leaves none, though they still draw), and the prepasses through their masked pipelines (below), which take the main pass's sample count (`FADE_SAMPLES`) and keep the pixels where the main pass covers any sample, so the rim, the bangs' shadow, eyes through hair, temporal AA's responsive mask and reprojection see the character where it is drawn. **The rim** goes while a character fades (its depth step would find the character's own holes); **its shadow** stays whole. **Only the anime character family fades:** a listed instance's environment or foliage surfaces stay whole.
    - **Under temporal AA** (phase 2; `fade-taa` showed a faint screen door): the masked prepasses mark a fading character's surfaces (0.25) and hulls (0.75) in the scene prepass's outline mask (`pipelines::outline_mask`; the masked scene prepass now writes it), and the responsive mask gives them the world's reactivity, 0, rather than characters' 0.5 or lines' 1, so the history keeps every turn of the dither, moving or not; GTAO still leaves every hull out (above 0.5). The turn has its own cycle because the jitter's eight phases turned the pattern unevenly (Halton's x over them is a sixteenth off at its eighth). **Decision: the order isn't Bayer's.** Any three consecutive values of `FADE_ORDER` (cyclically, as the turns take them) lie in three different rows and columns, so every 3 × 3 block holds one of them; Bayer's don't (0, 1 and 2 share two rows), and where the resolve's 3 × 3 neighborhood held only the character, its clip pulled the history back to this frame's color: at opacity 0.8 the blend kept a pattern of ±0.07 between the wall's 0.3 and the ball's 0.6, and ±0.013 with this order. So from a fade of 3/16 to 13/16 the clip keeps the blend; runs of one or two thresholds can't be in every block of any 4 × 4 order. Of such orders it is the most even a search found (a Gaussian energy over every run of consecutive values on the tiled plane, within 2% of Bayer's).
    - `RenderStats::{faded_characters, faded_draws}`; the overlay's characters row shows them.
  - **Masked prepasses (C5's open item):** the alpha-tested anime character variants, fading characters' routed draws among them, draw into the character prepass, the scene prepass and the outline hulls' prepass through masked pipelines (`vs_/fs_character_prepass_masked`, `vs_/fs_scene_prepass_masked`, `vs_/fs_outline_prepass_masked`), on layouts that add the family's group 3 (the base color map, binding 0, and the material sampler, 8) and read the surface stream's UVs. They cut out what the main pass cuts: the base color's alpha × its map's against the cutoff, for materials that alpha-test, and the dither. The masked character prepass writes C6's `Rg16Uint` IDs, with an R-only twin for hair, whose prepass writes one ID (C6). Opaque variants keep the plain position-only pipelines. A lash or brow card is in the depth and IDs at its drawn shape, and GTAO (W5) sees through character cutouts as W6 has it see through foliage's. `MeshPipelines` builds the four with the first alpha-tested variant and rebuilds them on shader edits.
  - **Cutting pixels out on Metal.** The fade's holes first showed black, the character's depth and color written where it should have been cut: on Apple GPUs a pixel discarded early while the rest of its 2 × 2 quad shades on can still write depth and color. **Decision:** fragment stages that cut pixels out decide early and discard at their one exit, after the shading; only a quad cut out whole leaves early (frame.wgsl's `quad_any`, from fine derivatives of the kept flag, shared with C5's eyes through hair and the sky). That is the anime character surfaces' alpha test and fade, the outline hulls' and eyes through hair; their final color is one variable at that exit, for F5's debug views. The entry points' discards are gated by the permutation constants (`(PERMUTATION_ALPHA_TEST || PERMUTATION_TRANSPARENT) && !kept`), so an opaque variant has no reachable discard and keeps hidden surface removal even where the shading function isn't inlined. docs/conventions.md has the rule. `environment.wgsl`'s and `foliage.wgsl`'s alpha tests still discard early and may show the same at cutout edges; they are W4's and W6's to check.
  - **Content and sandbox:** the pipeline's `samples::materials` writes five materials over the layers (crimson lacquer, gold trim with reflective stripes, leather with a multiplied falloff, skin in thigh-high stockings, a dark suit with breathing light lines), a painted matcap (RGB, and a grayscale falloff in A) and a layer mask, all generated, sidecars committed. The sandbox's `--materials` stands them beside the character (four spheres and a leg); `--fade <0..1>` and `--proximity-fade` fade the characters.
  - **Golden images (Metal):** four new scenes, 79 in all: `materials-day` and `materials-night` (the samples, pinned like the character's earlier scenes: `--aa msaa`, shadows, fog and AO off, `materials-day` with `--plain-post`), `fade-taa` (half-faded on the environment, Desktop-High under temporal AA, 32 frames) and `fade-mobile` (the same on Mobile-Low, 4× MSAA). Every earlier golden is byte for byte the same (FLIP 0.0000), the debug views' and the look-dev town's among them. Phase 2 regenerated the four: `fade-taa` (the turn's cycle, the order and the fading pixels' reactivity), `fade-mobile` (the order moves which of its samples each pixel covers) and `materials-day` and `materials-night` (W5's cube for the sky cube).
  - **Tests (Metal, `tests/layers.rs`):** the highlight's size for both models where N·H's cut predicts it, tinted by the albedo, sized and masked by a control map and gone on the shadow side; the matcap's channels, tint, blend, shadow strength and mask; the reflection add against the sky cube at the ambient's strength, gated at 0.9; stockings darker toward the silhouette, with a sheen along the fabric; emission unlit, breathing and painted by the mask's B, blooming as the world's light by its bloom and kept out of bloom without it; a fading character keeping its share of pixels in 4 × 4 blocks (whole quads cut out too), the wall through its holes, its outline keeping the same share and drawing nowhere new, whole again at 1 with one draw-list rebuild each way; an alpha-tested card cut out of the character prepass (the rim finds the step behind it); no rim while fading; under MSAA, the share of every pixel's samples; under temporal AA, a resolved blend at opacities 0.3, 0.5 and 0.8 (the pattern within ±0.03), and with the camera circling (within ±0.045, which a moving character's reactivity misses at ±0.052); the reflection add against W5's cube at the ambient's scale, a rougher metal's blurred, its ambient work (four pipelines, the projection's three and the prefilter; a 128² cube; the projection's three dispatches and one a mip; six faces and then one a frame) only while a reflective material draws. **`tests/eyes.rs`:** a fading character's eye layers keep the pixels the eye and skin keep, and leave none wholly faded. **`characters::gpu_tests`** reads the prepasses back: under temporal AA, the character prepass's IDs and depth and the scene prepass's depth hold the fading character exactly where the main pass's depth does, bit for bit; under 4× MSAA at opacity 0.1 the prepasses keep the 40% of pixels the main pass covers a sample of. Headless: the fade's dither order (every 3 × 3 block holds one of any three consecutive values) and turn (each sixteenth once a cycle), the outline mask's codes against temporal AA's and GTAO's thresholds, the permutation bits and variant sets, the blocks' layouts and flags against WGSL, the layers' CPU references (`SpecularModel::shape`, `Stockings::opacity`, `Emission::level`), validation, the masked and covered entry points' vertex inputs and overrides, the samples, `ProximityFade` and the sandbox's options.
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench 1500`, 1920×1080, Apple M5, release, five rounds alternating with a release build of dd118d1 in its own checkout, on a machine shared with other work; minima over the rounds; GPU times are the passes' mean exclusive times. On Desktop-High Metal reports the main pass inside the ambient pass's span, which GTAO overlaps, so the two compare together there):
    - **Without layers or a fade** (the turntable): Mobile-Low's main pass 110 µs against 115, the GPU frame 246 against 252; Desktop-High's main pass with GTAO 786 against 802, the GPU frame 1,944 against 1,958; CPU p50 100 against 99 and 145 against 145; allocations a frame 297.9 and 475.7, the same. None is measurable: the differences, mostly in C7's favor, are noise. Phase 1, without the `LAYERS` bit, paid 125.7 µs against 106.8 on Mobile-Low's main pass and 701 against 685 on Desktop-High's with GTAO (against 7aa2871, measured the same way).
    - **The layers** (`--materials`, the samples beside the character, which the base can't draw): Mobile-Low's main pass 298 µs and Desktop-High's 644; phase 1 measured them against a build with the layer block compiled out (Mobile-Low +67 µs).
    - **A reflective character** (the gold sample): the ambient pass's projection (three dispatches since #58) and prefilter, 23 µs on Mobile-Low (a 32² cube, the faces the sky cube draws; 93 µs with W5's one-workgroup projection), and about 5 allocations a frame; nothing without one.
    - **A half-faded character** (`--fade 0.5`, against the base unfaded): Desktop-High's GPU frame 1,960 µs against 1,875 (+85: the alpha-tested variants and their outlines lose early depth testing), both prepasses 144 against 133; Mobile-Low's main pass 127 against 112 (+15), the GPU frame 256 against 241, the character prepass 22 against 19; 4 and 2 allocations more. Transient: a gameplay camera fades no one.
    - Against §3.3: the main pass stays far inside forward opaque's 3.5 ms (Desktop-Low) and 8 ms (Mobile-Low), and the outlines' 0.6 and 1.5 ms are untouched.
  - **What remains:** SSR on Desktop, and specular occlusion for the reflection add. The masked prepasses alpha-test an eye at its plain UVs, while the main pass parallax-shifts an alpha-tested eye's (none in M1's content). Under temporal AA, fades under 3/16 or over 13/16 (a run of one or two thresholds) can leave a 3 × 3 neighborhood one-sided, so the very start and end of a fade keep a faint pattern. The environment and foliage families don't fade with a character. MToon's emissive texture and factor aren't mapped to the emission layer yet. Screen-pattern and SDF-reveal effects are M2's.

**Outlines**

- **Characters:** an inverted-hull pass. The cooker bakes smoothed normals into a spare vertex attribute (octahedral-encoded). Width comes from vertex color × a distance curve × a resolution scale; each material sets the outline color.
- **Environment (desktop, optional):** a screen-space edge pass driven by the prepass normals and IDs.
- **TAA interaction:** composite character outlines after TAA, or mark them in a responsive mask.
- **As built (M1 R7):** the hull still draws only in the main pass, with no velocity. The resolve finds its pixels where the main pass's depth is nearer than the scene prepass's, gives them the velocity of the nearest surface matching their depth within an outline's width, and treats them as responsive (see "Anti-aliasing and upscaling"). C4 writes the hull's own velocity; P1 settles how outlines and TAA meet.
- **As built (M1 C4, outlines v2: `shaders/outline.wgsl`, whose header gives the formulas; `sapphire_render::outlines`; `sapphire_assets::material::{Outline, OutlineCurve}`; `sapphire_assets::mesh::outline_frame`):**
  - **Outline normals** are the cooker's welded, angle-weighted bake or the artist's, now stored in the tangent frame of the vertex's own normal, a frame derived from the normal (§5.10 has the decision). **Skinning** (replacing R2's "the outline normal isn't morphed") decodes it to object space, turns it by the shortest rotation taking the bind normal to the morphed one, so it follows the morphed surface, transforms it by the blended matrix with the normal and stores it in the deformed normal's frame. It transforms the outline normal itself rather than rebuilding it from the deformed frame, so a hard edge's split vertices, which share an outline normal and influences, get one result through linear blending, which isn't a rotation at a bent joint. A morph that bends a hard edge turns each split vertex's outline normal by its own normal's turn and can open the hull there by the bend's angle; character morphs move smooth surfaces, and per-target outline deltas can be cooked if hard-edged morphs need them.
  - **Width,** in pixels at 1080 rows and scaled with the target's height: `width × region scale × vertex color G × model_scale × curve(d) × fade(d)`. `d` is the **effective distance**, the view depth × `tan(fov_y / 2) / tan(22.5°)`: where the model would look as large through a 45° lens, so zooming in is moving closer and a line keeps its width wherever a character looks the same size. The curve is two linear segments through `near`, `mid` and `far`, and holds the line's width in meters outside them: wider in pixels in extreme close-ups, thinning past 20 m without vanishing early. **Decision: the model scale is per material** (`Outline::model_scale`, StarRailNPR's `_ModelScale`): a chibi's materials are its own and the block already reaches every hull; a per-character scale can join `CharacterData` if one material ever needs two.
  - **LOD cutoff:** past `curve.cutoff` (80 effective meters by default) × `r.outline_distance` (a cvar, 0–4, 1 by default; `Renderer::set_outline_distance`; the sandbox's `--outline-distance`) no hull draws. The shader fades the width to nothing over the cutoff's last quarter; on the CPU (`outlines::OutlineCull`, each main-view `prepare`) a draw's hull is dropped once its instance's bounding sphere (the mesh's cooked bounds, transformed) is wholly past the cutoff, so nothing pops. `RenderStats::{outline_draws, outlines_culled}` count them.
  - **Flatten and push-back:** the hull extrudes in view space along the outline normal's part across the screen with `flatten` (0.1 by default; Genshin uses about 0.01) behind it in place of its own depth, normalized: normals across the screen extrude the full width, ones facing the camera less, sinking behind the surface instead of poking through it at grazing angles. The vertex is also pushed away from the camera along the view ray by Z offset × vertex color B plus the extrusion's depth, by scaling its clip position, so it doesn't move on screen. It all goes through one unjittered matrix before the jitter (`outline_clip`), so the prepass places a hull vertex in the last frame exactly as in this one.
  - **Color:** the region's outline color, blended toward that color × the albedo by `albedo` (0 by default), then lit by `lighting` (1 by default, MToon's outline lighting mix): the character's key light through the material's split on the outline normal and its diffuse mode's shadow tone, plus the rig's stylized GI flattened toward the camera (`character_gi` with the view direction as the line's normal, turned toward the head sphere's by the region's sphere weight, as faces' and hair's surfaces are). Lines take the key's tint and darken at night; local lights don't reach them. With a control map the color's region is read per pixel, as on the surface; the width's region is the vertex's (the vertex stage reads no textures).
  - **Decision: a character's lines are in post's character mask** (M1 P2, rebased onto it): its hulls write its 0 into scene color's alpha, as its surface does, so a line blooms by the character's threshold and the mask's edge is the line's outer edge, not a band of world just inside the silhouette; an unlisted instance's hulls write 1, as its surface does. P2's note ("Post-processing") listed outlines among the passes writing 1.
  - **Decision: velocity from hulls drawn into the scene prepass.** After the surfaces, the scene prepass draws every hull (front faces culled, `vs_outline_prepass`) from this frame's and the last drawn frame's positions (skinning's previous positions, the instance's last transform, the view's last matrix), so a hull pixel's depth and velocity are the hull's own, bit for bit the main pass's depth. The hulls also write a third target, the outline mask (`R8Unorm`, 1 where a hull is nearest), which the responsive mask takes in, so lines keep R7's responsiveness. An MRT in the main pass was rejected: velocity would come from two passes and need merging, multisampled under MSAA, and the prepass's depth, which TAA, GTAO and screen-space edges read, would still lack the hulls. So was a velocity pass of its own: a third set of hull draws, and still a merge. The prepass costs one more draw per outlined draw, only where something reads it (TAA on the desktop tiers).
    - **R7's hull search is redundant:** the main pass's depth now equals the prepass's on hull pixels, so the resolve's test (main pass nearer than the prepass) finds no hull pixels and its ring search never runs for them. The resolve is unchanged; P1 removes the search and `OUTLINE_REACH`.
    - **The turning body** (R7's case: the sandbox's character turning at 3 rad/s, 1920×1080, after 32 TAA frames, against 4× MSAA; line strength is the darkness of thin lines against their 5 × 5 surroundings, summed over the head and over the whole figure): TAA keeps 80.9% (head) and 83.5% (figure) of MSAA's line strength, against R7's search's 83.8% and 86.7%; the mean luma difference from MSAA is 0.0103 and 0.0040 against R7's 0.0102 and 0.0043. On an analytic sphere turning at the same speed (`tests/outline.rs`) TAA keeps 101% of MSAA's outline darkness, R7's search 104% and hull velocity without the outline mask 96%. An experiment that wrote the hull's velocity but not its depth, keeping R7's detection of hull pixels, kept 87.5% and 89.2% on the character: R7's search then still decides those pixels' velocity, which is what P1 is to replace; P1 owns tuning how responsive hull pixels resolve. The `outlines-turning-taa` golden shows the lines at the close-up.
  - **Cost** at 1920×1080 on an Apple M5, release. Other packages' benches and GPU tests shared the GPU throughout: in the sandbox's `--bench 3000`, seven rounds alternating with a release build of 6e5ad54, the main pass and the prepasses moved by up to ±200 µs between rounds, far more than the hulls cost, and base and branch overlapped. So the cost was also measured inside single runs (two rounds per scene and tier), with outlines on and off on alternate frames (`r.outline_distance` 1 and 0) over 4,000 timed frames, as the median of adjacent frames' differences, which other work affects alike:
    - **Desktop-High** (TAA): the character's five hulls 4–8 µs (main pass 0.6–1.2, scene prepass 3.6–7.1), 5–7 µs close up, and 14 µs for `--outline-row`'s six characters (25 hulls), against the 0.6 ms budget.
    - **Mobile-Low's settings** (4× MSAA at render scale 0.55, no prepass): 1.5–4 µs, 4–8 µs close up and 16–35 µs for the row, against 1.5 ms.
    - Allocations per frame: 413.7 against 407.7 on Desktop-High, presumably wgpu recording the hull prepass's draws (not traced), and unchanged at 264.4 on Mobile-Low. CPU p50 131.5 µs against 120.2 on Desktop-High in one pair of runs and 91.6 against 94.1 on Mobile-Low, within this shared machine's noise; the cull is a sphere test per outlined draw.
  - **Tiers:** every tier draws the same lines; nothing is simplified per tier, since Mobile-Low's cost is far inside its 1.5 ms. Phones run no scene prepass (no TAA), so their hulls draw once.
  - **Tests (Metal, `tests/outline.rs`):** the width along the curve inside and outside its segments (within 0.4 px + 4%), the same width at 30°, 60° and 90° for one effective distance (within 0.3 px), and the model scale; the cutoff's fade (60% at 8.5 m of a 10 m cutoff), no hull past it and the cull counted, a narrow lens bringing it back, and `r.outline_distance` at 0.9 and 0; flattening narrowing extrusions that face the camera by the predicted factor and pushing them back by the predicted depth (within 20%); lines lit by the key on the lit side and shadow-toned on the other, the albedo weight, unlit lines, and darker, bluer lines at night; hard-edged boxes with the cooker's welded outline normals showing no gap pixels, static and bent 70° at a joint by linear blend skinning, while unwelded ones open (the test sees cracks); a hull's velocity by its own depth for a translation and a camera move (within 1.5%), exactly zero held still, and a turning sphere's hull moving with its points behind the silhouette; and a turning body under TAA within 85–115% of 4× MSAA's outline darkness over a jitter cycle. Skinning (`skinning/gpu_tests.rs`): split vertices keep one outline normal through random palettes (within 0.05°), a morph that turns smooth vertices' normals by 35° turns their outline normals with them, and the scene prepass's depth, hulls included, equals the main pass's. Headless: the WGSL frame constants, frame uniforms and block offsets match Rust's, and every outline entry point specializes and validates. CPU: the curve and validation, the frame (orthonormal and right-handed, near the axis too), the stored round trip, the MToon mapping and the sandbox's scenes.
  - **Golden images (Metal):** every scene's capture changed, since every one shows lines: lit and tinted now, flattened, normalized to 45° (the sandbox's lens is 43°), and under TAA moving with the hull. Before regeneration all passed their thresholds: FLIP means 0.0010–0.0028 and p99s 0.028–0.079, and `turntable-taa-motion` mean 0.0072 and p99 0.225, where the pedestal's back edges, doubled under R7, now show single lines. The 16 Metal goldens were regenerated, and so were W4's three environment scenes and P2's three post scenes, whose character's lines changed alike (FLIP means 0.0013–0.0024 and p99s 0.037–0.062 against their own goldens); the scenes P2 pinned to `--plain-post` kept their pins. Lavapipe's and WARP's come from CI. Four scenes are new, pinned like the character's earlier ones (`--plain-sky`, `--plain-post`, and 4× MSAA but for the TAA one), so they show the lines alone: `outlines-distance` (the sandbox's `--outline-row`: the character with five more from 4 m to 90 m behind it, the last past the cutoff), `outlines-telephoto` (the same through a 20° lens, the character framed alike: its lines keep their width, the row's widen), `outlines-hard-edges` (`--hard-edges`: the pipeline's flat-shaded cube, prisms and gem, every corner closed) and `outlines-turning-taa` (a close-up of the character turning at 3 rad/s under TAA).
- **As built (M1 R4):** lit lines receive the cast shadow as their surface does: the line's split takes `cast_shadow_at` at the hull (`anime_character.wgsl`'s `cast_shadow` at a given point: the cascades alone for characters) as occlusion, and a face material's line the face pass's whole value, so a line goes into a cast shadow with what it outlines and stays lit with shadows off. Outline pipelines take the shadow constants with their coverage's. Hulls cast no shadow: the shadow passes draw surfaces alone (`DrawSet::Surfaces`). The LOD cutoff reads the mesh records' bounds, the copy the light lists and the casters read, instead of a store of its own.
- **As built (M1 P1, outline-aware TAA: `shaders/outline.wgsl`'s `fs_outline_prepass`, `shaders/temporal.wgsl`, `sapphire_render::temporal`):** under temporal AA a line follows the silhouette it draws, and in motion it is drawn from the current frame. R7's hull search and `OUTLINE_REACH` are gone, with the main pass's kept depth that only they and disocclusion read.
  - **The problem** (R7's and C4's notes above): a hull pixel shows a point of the hull up to a line's width behind the silhouette. Whenever the body turns against the view, about its own axis or under a camera circling it, those points slide through the silhouette (on the GPU tests' sphere turning at 3 rad/s, at 540 rows, 1.7 px a frame 3 px outside the silhouette), while the silhouette, and so the line, holds still or moves otherwise. C4's hull velocity reprojected the history along that sliding and smeared each line across itself.
  - **Decision: a hybrid, all of it in the resolve's inputs and the resolve.** The plan's options were a responsive mask tuned in the resolve, compositing the lines after the resolve, or both. Each part was measured on its own (below):
    - **The line's velocity** (`fs_outline_prepass`): a hull pixel writes the silhouette's motion rather than its hull point's. Across a line the motion changes with how squarely the outline normal faces the camera, and the silhouette is where it faces across, so the pixel extrapolates its velocity along the facing's screen gradient to facing 0, `v − f (∇v · ∇f) / |∇f|²`, from screen derivatives, followed at most two line widths (`OUTLINE_SILHOUETTE_REACH`: the facing grows like the square root of the distance from the silhouette, so a linear estimate from inside the line overshoots it, by 1.6 times at a sphere line's inner edge, and one line width corrected about half the sliding). A translation or a swinging limb, the same motion across the line, keeps its velocity, and a hull that holds still keeps exactly none (corrections below a thousandth of a pixel are dropped as derivative rounding). On the turning sphere the written velocity is 19–28% of the hull points' sliding at each measured pixel, 18% in all; under a translation and a pan it is the silhouette's within 2%.
    - **Lines are fully reactive:** the responsive mask now holds each pixel's reactivity, the share of the moving history cap it gives up (fractional, as FSR's reactive mask is): characters' 0.5 (stored 128, the old cap) and lines' 1 (`tuning::OUTLINE_HISTORY` 0, stored 255). A moving line is drawn from the current frame, as Blue Protocol keeps TAA's blur off its lines; a still one keeps the full history. 0.1 and 0.25 kept less of the lines (head strength 86% and 84% against 89% at 0).
    - **The resolve tries other reprojections on lines** (`line_motion`): a pixel with a line in its 3 × 3 (the hull's own, and those its antialiased edge reaches, which carry their surface's or the sky's velocity) also tries the camera's motion alone (a body turning in place) and no motion (a silhouette holding still on screen), and keeps the reprojection whose bilinear history best matches this frame's filtered color, its own on a tie. Two more history taps on line pixels; nothing where neither the pixel nor the camera moved.
    - **Rejected: compositing the lines after the resolve.** A prototype drew the scene's depth again at the output's size, unjittered, then the hulls over a copy of the resolved frame, either alone or over hulls the resolve had already seen. Its lines are one sample a pixel, aliased in motion and still: on the turning character 96–116% of 4× MSAA's line strength and 103–118% of its contrast, with line-zone errors of 12.2–14.3 (head) and 10.6–11.1 (figure) against the hybrid's 11.8 and 9.3, and held still 8.4–9.4 and 6.9–8.1 against TAA's 5.5 and 4.4, worse than the resolve converges to. It cost 190–215 µs more at 1080p for one character, growing with the hulls and the output's area (a 4× MSAA composite would cost more again); lines would draw over transparent surfaces and particles; and the world's occlusion of the lines needs the whole scene's depth at the output's size, which an external upscaler doesn't produce.
  - **Measured** against 4× MSAA at the same pose. Line strength is thin lines' darkness against their 5 × 5 surroundings, summed; contrast and width are the same lines' peak and spread; line-zone error is the mean luma error, in code values, within a pixel of MSAA's lines. Base (5955ca4) → P1, head / whole figure:
    - **Turning** (the sandbox's character at 3 rad/s after 32 TAA frames, 1920×1080, as R7 and C4 measured): strength 81.1% / 82.9% → 96.0% / 93.4%; contrast 76.8% / 86.4% → 92.1% / 93.0%; width 105.6% / 96.0% → 104.3% / 100.4%; line-zone error 20.7 / 14.6 → 11.8 / 9.3. The parts alone: full reactivity 89.0% / 90.7% (errors 16.3 / 11.0); with the search but the hull points' velocity 93.2% / 92.3% (13.5 / 10.4); with the line's velocity but no search 94.3% / 92.4% (14.2 / 9.4).
    - **Eight consecutive turning frames:** strength 82.7% / 86.0% → 98.1% / 96.8%; line-zone error 20.3 / 13.6 → 12.0 / 9.3; how much that error changes from frame to frame (shimmer) 10.4 / 13.5 → 8.3 / 10.5.
    - **Upsampled** (Desktop-High's 3840×2160 from 2560×1440, turning, against native 4K 4× MSAA): strength 86.1% / 89.9% → 100.9% / 99.9%; contrast 77.4% / 82.2% → 96.6% / 99.1%; width 111.2% / 109.4% → 104.4% / 100.8%; line-zone error 24.8 / 17.9 → 14.4 / 11.5. Lines keep their authored width in output pixels.
    - **Still camera:** unchanged, as it must be with nothing moving: strength 98.0% / 94.1%, contrast 94.9% / 93.9%, and from frame 40 to 41 a mean change of 0.013 code values with 178 pixels changing by more than 2.
    - **The GPU tests' sphere** (`tests/outline.rs`, 4× MSAA alongside): turning about its axis, contrast 82% → 94% of MSAA's, width 122% → 115%, frame-to-frame change 4.2 → 2.9 times MSAA's; under a camera circling it 82% → 96%, 122% → 112%, 4.0 → 3.1 times; upsampled from two-thirds scale 69% → 88% contrast and 147% → 118% width; still 91% and 116%, a pixel changing by 0.015 a frame, as before.
    - **No ghost trails:** a character moving 4.5 px a frame under a still camera, and a still one under a camera panning as fast, match 4× MSAA away from its edges after 24 frames: away from MSAA's edges no pixel is more than one code value off in either, so the search never picks a reprojection that leaves a line behind.
    - **A render-scale step** (dynamic resolution, 1 to 0.85 in mid-turn) is a non-event for lines: the history is at the output's size (R7) and a moving line is drawn from the current frame at whatever size the scene is. Stepping halfway through the measured frames, the sphere's line measures between its measures at either scale held (107% of MSAA's strength against 105% and 108%, 93% of its contrast against 92% and 94%) and changes from frame to frame less than at 0.85 alone (0.037 a pixel, against 0.043 at 0.85 and 0.033 at 1).
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench`, release, Apple M5, Desktop-High) on a machine shared with four other agents' builds and GPU work, so single runs swung by up to 2.5 times. Three alternating `--bench 3000` rounds against 5955ca4: the TAA passes took 644–1,259 µs (base) and 1,083–1,250 µs (P1) at 1920×1080, and 2,289–3,659 against 2,235–4,312 µs at 3840×2160 from 2560×1440. Eight shorter alternating rounds each (240 GPU-timed frames), whose minimum is the least disturbed: 638 → 589 µs at 1080p (R7 measured 598 on a quiet machine), inside §3.3's 0.8 ms, and 2,315 → 2,149 µs at 4K; medians 1,686 → 1,539 and 2,762 → 2,658 µs. P1 costs nothing measurable: the resolve reads one texture fewer and searches on line pixels alone (two taps each), the hulls' prepass adds a few derivatives a pixel (prepasses 101 → 112 and 207 → 185 µs at their minima), and the main pass's depth stays on tile (main 361 → 334 and 632 → 631 µs).
  - **Tests (Metal):** `tests/outline.rs`: `hulls_move_with_their_silhouette` replaces C4's hull-velocity test (exactly none held still; the silhouette's motion within 2% under a translation and a pan; on the turning sphere 19–28% of the hull points' sliding at each measured pixel, 18% in all). A turning body and a circling camera keep 90–115% of 4× MSAA's line strength, over 90% of its contrast, under 119% of its width and under 3.6 times its frame-to-frame change; upsampled lines keep under 130% of its width and over 84% of its contrast; a render-scale step leaves the line between its measures at either scale (within 3%), changing no more than 1.1 times as much; still lines hold steady (a pixel changing by under 0.02 a frame). `tests/temporal.rs`: moving lines leave no trail (above), and the responsive mask holds characters' 128 and lines' 255 and nothing else. Forcing the hulls' velocity to zero fails three of the outline tests, and C4's resolve (hull points' velocity, characters' reactivity, no search) four.
  - **Golden images (Metal):** a new scene, `outlines-turning-taa-upsampled` (the turning close-up at render scale 0.667, as Desktop-High upsamples 4K). Regenerated: `outlines-turning-taa` (FLIP mean 0.0072, p99 0.185 against the old golden) and `turntable-taa-motion` (0.0054 and 0.151): darker, crisper lines on the turning character, close to 4× MSAA's where the old ones were faint. Thirteen other TAA scenes (`turntable-taa`, `turntable-taa-upsampled`, the three `environment-*`, `post-day`, five `shadows-*`, and P3's `effects-blur-lines` and `effects-impact-frame`) changed by one code value (two on a few pixels) on 3–181 pixels each, all in flat areas, sky gradients mostly (FLIP means 0.0000–0.0001, p99 at most 0.0018): rounding, from the resolve compiling differently, since with nothing moving the resolve computes the same values. They were regenerated so main's goldens match exactly. MSAA-pinned scenes are byte-identical, and so are `shadows-capsule`, `post-sunset` and `effects-chromatic-inversion`. Lavapipe's and WARP's goldens for the two turning scenes need re-baselining from CI's captures, and the new scene's bootstrapping.
  - **What remains:** a turning line drawn from the current frame changes about three times as much from frame to frame as 4× MSAA's, as each frame's jitter draws it anew (2.9 times on the sphere, 4.2 before); reprojected exactly (no motion, exact for that sphere alone) it would keep its history and change 2.0 times as much. Nothing in the frame gives a silhouette's exact motion; a closer estimate than the prepass's extrapolation would let moving lines keep some history.
  - **External upscalers** (later) take what the resolve takes and nothing more: `TemporalInputs` lost the main pass's depth. Hulls are in all of their inputs: the scene prepass's depth, their line's velocity, which any upscaler needs (a hull point's would smear a turning body's lines in each of them), and a reactivity of 1 (FSR's reactive mask, DLSS's bias-current-color mask, MetalFX's `reactiveMaskTexture`), so an upscaler too draws a moving line from the current frame. Only `line_motion` is the resolve's own.

**Environment**

- **Shading:** a stylized GGX uber-shader.
- **Foliage:** instanced with vertex-shader wind. LODs go from cards up close to billboards to impostors.
- **Grass:** instances generated on the GPU on desktop; low-density CPU-placed instances on mobile.
- **Sky:**
  - A gradient sky plus animated 2D cloud layers, and sun and moon discs.
  - Everything is driven by artist time-of-day curves, not physical atmosphere: one `TodTimeline` of `TodKey`s, evaluated in OkLab, is the single source for sky, fog, ambient, shadow tint, post and the character light rig.
- **Fog:** distance and height fog colored by the sky horizon, plus fog cards, on every tier. Volumetric fog is desktop-only and comes later.
- **As built (M1 W1):**
  - Time of day lives in `sapphire_world::tod` (§5.21), not here. The renderer receives its resolved scene constants (§4.6) and draws the key light, sky gradient and post-processing from them as before. Sky discs, fog, ambient, the shadow tint and the character key light wait for W2, W3, W4/W5, R4 and C2.
  - Neighboring keys' grades blend as post's two LUTs: `grade_a` and `grade_b` are the two keys' grades and `grade_blend` the eased weight, with B unused while one grade shows. A running day therefore bakes one LUT per key crossed, and none between keys.
  - The frame uniforms gain `day_night` for C1's ramp-pair blend; no shader reads it yet. All six existing golden captures are byte-identical.
  - The golden scene `turntable-sunset` (`--time-of-day 18.5`) sits between the default day's golden-hour and sunset keys, 80% through their LUT blend. It shows a low orange key from the right with a crisp two-tone split, violet-tinted shade, a gold horizon band under a salmon sky, and a violet ground. Only its Metal golden is committed; lavapipe and WARP come from CI.
- **As built (M1 W2, `sapphire_render::sky`, `shaders/sky.wgsl`):**
  - **Layers.** The sky stays one fullscreen triangle in the main pass at the far plane, after the opaque surfaces and outlines (depth test `Equal`), writing HDR scene color. Over `sky_color()`, unchanged (the anime character shader reads it for ambient light), it draws back to front: procedural stars and the Milky Way on the celestial sphere, the sun's and moon's glows, their discs, the cirrus and the card cloud band. Time of day gives every color and amount (`FramePacket::sky_layers`, §4.6); the sky material gives every shape, map and motion.
  - **Sun and moon.** Glows are compact (a halo with a brighter core, nothing past their radius, so a body out of view adds exactly nothing), spread along the horizon while their body is low, and fade out just under it. Discs are HDR, so the sun's blooms, with an edge as soft as the material says or a pixel, and are cut by the horizon. The moon's face map spans its disc upright; its alpha can paint a crescent, and the moon hides the stars behind it.
  - **Stars** are procedural: two grids of cells (160 and 44 per face edge) on a cube around the celestial sphere, each cell holding at most one star at a hashed spot (PCG), with a hashed brightness, warmth and twinkle; the star map sets their density and tint. A star is a normalized Gaussian 0.6 or 0.85 pixels in radius, measured from the pixel's own angular footprint, so its light summed over its pixels is the same wherever it falls between them: stars hold still under sub-pixel camera motion, where point stars would flicker, and a grid fades out before its cells shrink below 3–6 pixels. The Milky Way is a map on the same sphere. Time of day turns the sphere once a day about the sky's pole (§5.21), so stars rise and set with the hour.
  - **Card clouds:** a band of painted cumulus around the horizon. `u` runs with the azimuth, a whole number of repeats a turn, drifting; `v` with the direction's height (the sine of its elevation, for one arctangent less), its texels square at the horizon and the band's base just under it. The map is RGBA in the Genshin scheme: R the lit amount (its 0.5 line the painted terminator), G a painted rim, B a signed distance to the cloud's edge (0.5 on the silhouette, rising inward), A opacity. Coverage moves the dissolve threshold along B, so clouds shrink to their cores and vanish rather than fade. Silhouette and terminator are linear ramps a pixel wide (the texel's own screen derivatives) or the authored softness, so they stay crisp at any magnification. The lit and shade tones are authored per key, never a darkened albedo; the rim (the painted one and a band just inside the dissolving edge) is strongest toward the key light, and low clouds melt into the horizon color. Texture coordinates and their gradients come analytically from the view direction (`textureSampleGrad`), so the azimuth's wrap leaves no seam.
  - **Cirrus:** a tiling map on a plane over the camera, scrolled both ways by the wind, its directions lifted so the plane recedes smoothly to the horizon, where it fades out. It dissolves like the card clouds and has no rim: its wisps are too thin for one.
  - **Drift** is reduced on the CPU in `f64` from the packet's unwrapped time, so the clouds never jump, even weeks into a session.
  - **Data and variants.** Each frame the CPU folds the packet's layers and the sky material's block (read from the material buffer's CPU mirror: one sky draws at a time, so the family's GPU buffer goes unread) into a 320-byte `SkyUniforms` block, with every per-draw constant (the glows' inverse radii and stretch, cloud repeats) worked out there. It is the sky pipelines' own group 2, at binding 2 (bindings 0 and 1 are the scene group's, which the sky doesn't read); group 3 is the sky family's material group. `FrameUniforms` and `ViewUniforms` are unchanged. The pipelines specialize three overridable constants: `SKY_STARS` and `SKY_CLOUDS`, on while time of day gives the stars a brightness or a cloud layer a coverage, pick one of four main pipelines each frame, so a frame never pays for a layer it doesn't draw; `SKY_DISCS` is off for the cube. The shader stays in `sky.wgsl`, concatenated with `frame.wgsl` and `materials.wgsl` as before, so A8's hot reload reaches it like any other.
  - **The sky cube** (`Sky::cube_view`): the sky alone, without geometry, for the probes and reflections that will read it (W5's ambient probes, W4's and W5's reflection misses, C7's reflection add). It holds the gradient, the glows and both cloud layers; not the discs, which shading adds as the key light (a disc would add them twice and alias at probe resolution), and not the stars, far smaller than its texels. `Rg11b10Ufloat` where the device renders and filters it, else `Rgba16Float`; one level, since readers prefilter. Faces are WebGPU's, in its order and orientation; one face is drawn every `cube_interval` frames, and all six on the first. It is a persistent texture outside the frame graph, like the renderer's buffers, until textures can outlive a frame in the graph.
  - **Per tier** (`QualityTier::sky`): 256² faces, one a frame, on both desktop tiers (so goldens match on every desktop GPU); 128², one a frame, on Mobile-High; 64², one every other frame (a whole cube every 0.4 s at 30 fps), and no cirrus on Mobile-Low, where the card clouds carry the look. Card clouds, stars and discs draw on every tier.
  - **Stats** (`RenderStats::sky`, rule R9): the cube's size, faces drawn and the last, which layers the last frame drew, and ignored sky materials, in the overlay's stats panel.
  - **Content:** the sandbox's sky material and every map it names are generated by the pipeline's `samples` module (§5.27) and cooked as ordinary textures: nothing is downloaded or third-party.
  - **Golden images.** The ten scenes from before (C2's `turntable-night` and C3's `faces` included) pin the sky's gradient alone (`--plain-sky`: no discs, glows, stars or clouds), and their Metal captures are unchanged, byte for byte. Three new scenes look at the sky: `sky-day` (09:00, looking up past the sun: its disc and glow, card clouds and cirrus), `sky-sunset` (18:30: the sun low in a gap of the card clouds, its glow spread along the horizon, gold-lit clouds over violet shade with bright rims toward the sun, cirrus catching the color) and `sky-night` (21:00: the moon's disc and halo, stars and the Milky Way, dark clouds with moonlit rims). Only their Metal goldens are committed.
  - **Tests.** GPU tests render and read back: the gradient is exactly unchanged by layers that draw nothing in view; the discs sit where `Celestial` puts them, under the horizon not at all; stars scale with their brightness, vanish at 0, and keep the frame's total star light within 1% while the camera turns by quarter pixels; card clouds dissolve from their edges to where coverage puts the threshold, and vanish at 0; the cirrus drifts at its speed, as well five million seconds in; the cube draws every face first, then one a frame in order, reads back as the sky through a cube view, and keeps the glows but not the discs; a handle that isn't a sky material draws the default sky. Layout tests check `SkyUniforms` against the WGSL struct, the texture slots and bindings, and that every variant of the three constants builds.
  - **Cost.** Against c3ac568 (sandbox `--bench 3000` and its 239 GPU-timed frames, Apple M5, release, three alternating rounds; the default scene, whose sky fills most of the frame; GPU times are the passes' mean exclusive times): on Desktop-High at 1920×1080 the main pass grows by about 0.19 ms at 10:00, with card clouds, cirrus and the sun's glow and disc (286–340 µs against 95–144), and by 0.25–0.32 ms at 22:00, with the stars, the Milky Way and the moon as well (346–400 µs against 78–99); the cube's face adds 35–45 µs, mostly a render pass's own cost. With Mobile-High's settings (1316×740, 128² faces) the sky costs 0.12–0.13 ms by day and about 0.16 ms at night, cube included; with Mobile-Low's (1049×590, no cirrus, a 64² face every other frame) about 0.06 ms by day and 0.08–0.1 ms at night. The mobile tiers' settings therefore landed inside the 0.1–0.3 ms budget on this GPU, and 1080p at night over it; the budget follow-up below brings every tier and hour inside it in this scene (Desktop-High 0.16 ms at 10:00 and 0.2 ms at 22:00, cube included). Phones are measured in X1. The cost is arithmetic per sky pixel, so the shader keeps it lean (CPU-side constants, a polynomial arctangent, linear edge ramps, one hash per star cell before the star's reach is known), and each pipeline variant leaves out what time of day isn't drawing. Measured by layer at 1080p: the derivatives every layer needs with the sun's glow and disc about 75 µs, each cloud layer about 70 µs, and the stars, Milky Way and moon about 65 µs. CPU p50 moves by a few microseconds, within the rounds' spread (115–145 µs against 97–131 at 10:00, 85.8–90.5 against 82.7–89.9 with Mobile-Low's settings). Allocations go from 305.3 to 309.6 per frame (2.2 KB more) on the desktop tiers and Mobile-High: the cube's render pass, and the frame graph grouping one more pass into command buffers (five instead of six); on Mobile-Low, whose cube pass runs every other frame, from 263.3 to 263.9.
  - **As built (M1 W2 follow-up, the budget):** pixels skip what can't reach them, and keep their color bit for bit.
    - **Where the time went**, found by switching each layer off in a full-screen copy of the sky's draw (1080p, Desktop-High, Apple M5). It is arithmetic, not texture reads: the cloud and Milky Way samples made constant save about 0.02 ms each. Opaque geometry already costs nothing, since the draw comes after it with depth test `Equal`. The default scene's camera looks a little down, so most sky pixels lie under the horizon, where the cloud layers (about 0.15 ms of full-screen shading for the two), the glows (0.04 ms) and the discs (0.03 ms) shaded for nothing: `above` and the cirrus's fade are 0 there. Above the horizon, at night, the stars and the Milky Way take about 0.2 ms, the clouds 0.15 ms and the fog 0.07 ms.
    - **The skips.** The discs, stars and glows are skipped under the glows' fringe (a direction's height 0.08 under the horizon). The cloud layers are skipped per 2 × 2 quad where no pixel of the quad reaches above the horizon: a branch uniform per quad, from fine derivatives as the eyes through hair do (§5.12, C5), since the clouds' texels take derivatives. A cloud layer's shading (its lit split, rim and haze) runs only where its texel covers the pixel. The star grids share the cube face their direction points through, each still dividing for itself: sharing the quotient rounded a few stars differently.
    - **Tried and dropped,** each as slow or slower: a test on the cube face's plane before a star's normalization, rejects before the discs' and glows' square roots, a quad test per cloud layer inside its layer's uniform branch (0.08 ms slower in the default scene than one test outside any branch), and an arccosine for the Milky Way's polar angle (4 µs, and not exact). The fog's per-draw parts cost nothing to compute per pixel: the compiler hoists them.
    - **Tests and goldens.** Every golden image is byte-identical. A GPU test renders a view rolled across the horizon, with every layer on, and the cube, with the skips and with the shader edited to skip nothing, and finds them identical; it fails if the quad test or the glows' fringe skips a pixel too many.
    - **Cost** against 8291e2a (release, `--bench 300` and its 240 GPU-timed frames, four alternating rounds on a loaded machine, minima of the passes' mean exclusive times; the sky is the main pass less the main pass with an empty sky shader, plus the cube's face). Desktop-High at 1920×1080: at 10:00 0.29 → 0.16 ms (main pass 439 → 314 µs, cube 35 → 32 µs, frame 1,674 → 1,546 µs), at 22:00 0.35 → 0.2 ms (main 502 → 359 µs, cube 36 → 30 µs, frame 1,745 → 1,610 µs). Mobile-High's settings: 0.15 → 0.09 ms by day and 0.18 → 0.12 ms at night. Mobile-Low's: 0.08 → 0.05 ms and 0.11 → 0.08 ms. In the courtyard (`--environment`, the sky over walls and hills): about 0.07 ms by day and 0.11 ms at night, as before. A sky filling the view is the costliest case, still just over the budget: 0.38 → 0.32 ms at night (`--view 112,-20`) and 0.32 ms by day (`--view 99,-22`), unchanged. The cube's face is about 0.03 ms, a third of it the GPU starting the frame, since it is the first pass. CPU time and allocations are unchanged.
- **As built (M1 W4, the environment shader: `shaders/environment.wgsl`, `sapphire_render::environment`, `pipelines::{SurfaceVariant, Shading}`):**
  - **The model.** One uber-shader for every environment material, a stylized GGX for the world kept softer and less contrasty than the anime characters drawn over it, so they read on top (look pillar 4; world_lighting_post.md §2). The key light is the world's (`FramePacket::sun`, the frame uniforms' `sun_*`: the sun by day, the moon at night), never a character's rig. Per pixel:
    - **Terminator.** N·L, pushed toward the deepest shadow by the cast shadow in half-Lambert space (`occluded`, as vertex AO enters the characters' split), passes `smoothstep(−w, w, ·)`: `w` is the material's width up close plus its growth × view distance (Blue Protocol's distance-dependent edges), and at least half a pixel's change of N·L, so a near-zero width is a clean two-tone step for props. The terminator sits at N·L = 0, where cast shadows begin, so the two always meet.
    - **Wrap lighting.** The lit side falls off as wrapped Lambert, `(N·L + wrap) / (1 + wrap)`: 0 is Lambert's; 0.3–0.5 keeps light to the terminator, which then shows as a soft colored step rather than a fade to black.
    - **Shadow tint and band.** The key light's diffuse is `key × mix(keep, 1, lit × falloff) × band`. `keep` is time of day's shadow tint (W1's `FramePacket::shadow_tint`, now in the frame uniforms) × the material's: shadows and the shadow side keep a colored share of the key light, never black. The band is a one-row ramp read at the terminator's value (0 on the shadow side, 1 lit), blended in by its strength: a warm band along the boundary, white at the ramp's ends.
    - **Ambient** is time of day's hemisphere through C2's `ambient_light(n)`, × vertex AO (vertex color R, the characters' contract) × the mask map's AO and cavity.
    - **Specular:** GGX with the perceptual roughness (× the mask's A) remapped into the material's range, reflectance 4% or the albedo for metals, × the mask's specular mask and cavity; each light's highlight is held under the material's specular clamp × its radiance. A reflectance the mask takes under about 2% takes the grazing reflection (Schlick's F90 and the environment BRDF's bias) down with it, Filament's specular occlusion, so a masked texel is matte at every angle on both tiers that reflect. Lambert carries no 1/π in Sapphire's convention (a unit key on a surface facing it gives its albedo), so the lobes carry π. The hemisphere also reflects, seen along the reflection vector through Karis's analytic environment BRDF, so metals and glossy surfaces keep a sky-colored sheen until W5's probes and the sky cube's prefiltered mips (decision: W2's cube has one level, which would alias as a rough reflection, so W4 doesn't sample it).
    - **Distance desaturation:** the albedo moves toward its own luminance, eased from the material's start distance to its end by its amount, so far surfaces read as painted masses while the frame's light keeps its hue. Emission keeps its color.
    - **Emission** (color × map) adds HDR light whatever the lighting.
    - **Local lights** (R5) walk the same lists as the anime character shader (`light_list`, moved into `lights.wgsl`): the diffuse through the same terminator (at least a pixel's change of each light's N·L) and wrapped falloff, a highlight each, no shadow tint, and no luminance ceiling (the world takes light as it comes).
    - **Final color** is produced in one place at the end of `fs_environment` (lit color plus emission), where W3's fog joins through a shared function.
  - **Tiers** (`QualityTier::environment`, `EnvironmentSettings`, `tiers.ron`; the shader's `ENVIRONMENT_SPECULAR` override): `Full` on both desktop tiers (GGX distribution, height-correlated Smith visibility, Schlick Fresnel); `Approximate` on Mobile-High (the distribution with visibility and Fresnel folded into `1 / (4 · max(L·H², 0.1) · (α + 0.5))` at the reflectance, Zioma's mobile GGX as Unity's mobile pipeline ships it); `Off` on Mobile-Low (no specular, metals shade as diffuse in their own color). Terminator, wrap, tint, band, desaturation, emission and local lights run on every tier: a handful of ALU operations the look rests on.
  - **The cast-shadow hook** (`cast_shadow(in: EnvironmentVaryings) -> f32` in `environment.wgsl`), documented like C1's: it returns 1 (lit) until R4 fills it. R4 returns how much of the world's key light (`FramePacket::sun`, whose maps it renders) reaches the point past every caster the key light's shadow maps hold, the environment's own and the characters' (unlike `cast_shadow` in the anime character shader, which sees environment casters only), times the per-character ground shadow on desktop and capsule shadows on mobile; sampled at `in.world_position` (offset along `in.world_normal` against acne) or, with a screen-space shadow mask, at `in.clip_position`. The shader reads it in one place, the terminator's input, so cast shadows take the terminator's distance-widened remap, the tinted keep and the band, and kill the key light's highlight; local lights cast nothing.
  - **Wetness inputs:** `FramePacket::weather` (`Weather`: wetness and puddle coverage, dry by default; nothing fills it yet) reaches the GPU as the frame uniforms' `weather`, appended. No shader reads them: the shader's header says where Lagarde's wet model (darker porous albedo, lower roughness, puddles, ripples) will apply, before any light; a porosity input joins the material then.
  - **Decision: no environment outlines in M1.** The environment family has no inverted hull (`pipelines::mesh_draw` never outlines it, whatever the flags): the spec's environment outline is the screen-space edge pass from the prepass's normals (a desktop option, the tiers' `screen_space_outlines`, later), and props that should read like characters use the anime character family, hull and all.
  - **Families on meshes.** A mesh's slots bind anime character or environment materials, mixed freely within a mesh; `upload_mesh` and `update_mesh` take both, and refuse foliage, sky and fog card materials (`RenderError::NotDrawable`). A surface variant is a coverage (opaque, alpha-tested, transparent) and a shading (`Shading::Character(part)` or `Shading::Environment`), nine in all, each a pipeline per position format built when a draw first needs it; an edit that changes an environment material's coverage moves its draws with one draw-list rebuild, as anime character permutations do. Each mesh family has a scene group of its own (group 2: the instances and its family's material buffer, `pipelines::MESH_FAMILIES`), which the main pass rebinds only when the family changes; the prepasses bind the anime character family's for every draw, reading no material.
  - **Draws sort by pipeline** (§5.12 "Sorting"): the draw list groups them by position format and surface variant, so families never interleave, whatever order instances and submeshes come in. In each coverage the anime character variants draw before the environment's, so depth rejects the world behind the characters; then the characters' outlines, the sky, and both families' transparent surfaces. Sorting by material and mesh within a group stays later work.
  - **Prepasses and TAA:** environment surfaces, opaque and alpha-tested, go through the scene prepass like any mesh (depth, normals and velocity, from their instance's transform, skinned positions where they have them, and the camera's). The character prepass draws the anime character family's skinned and morphed instances alone, so a skinned prop in environment materials (a flag, a swinging sign) never counts as a character for rim light, hair shadow or the responsive mask. Converged under TAA, a test scene of the world changes by a mean of 0.016 steps from frame to frame, one pixel of 16,384 by more than 2.
  - **Shared helpers** moved out of the anime character shader unchanged, so both shaders use one copy: `LUMINANCE`, `light_step`, `occluded` and `mapped_normal` into `frame.wgsl`, the light-list lookup into `lights.wgsl`. The sixteen earlier golden scenes render exactly as before (FLIP mean, p99 and max 0.000 on Metal).
  - **Hot reload (A8):** `environment.wgsl` is a `ShaderFile` and a mesh program of its own (`frame.wgsl`, `materials.wgsl`, `lights.wgsl`, then it); an edit rebuilds the environment pipelines built so far and nothing else, and `frame.wgsl` edits now rebuild six programs.
  - **Stats** (rule R9): `RenderStats::environment_draws` (of `draws`, which now counts every family's surfaces) and `environment_pipelines`, in the overlay's stats panel.
  - **Content and sandbox:** the pipeline's `samples::environment` writes `content/samples/environment/`: `courtyard.glb` (220 m of ground, flat around the character and rolling into hills from 45 m, boulders from 3 m to 60 m with vertex AO at their feet, a colonnade of nine pillars receding from the character's left, a plaster wall with a gap, and paper lanterns on posts and on the wall), one slot per material, bound by its sidecar to five environment material documents (ground, rock, stone, wall, lantern), a terminator band ramp and a painted ground map; all generated, nothing downloaded. The sandbox's `--environment` loads it by ID through the asset server and places it around the character, whose pedestal stands on its flat middle, with a camera pulled back 1.45× and lowered. The mannequin and its pedestal keep their anime character materials (decision: the pedestal is part of the character asset, and moving it would change every golden).
  - **Golden images:** three new scenes, Metal goldens only, under the whole sky with TAA (32 still frames), as the desktop tiers draw: `environment-day` (10:00), `environment-sunset` (18:30) and `environment-night` (22:00, with the demo lights).
  - **Tests (Metal, `tests/environment.rs`):** a CPU model of the shader predicts every pixel of analytic spheres and planes (each pixel's ray meets the exact surface) within 0.025 in linear color: wrap lighting, and a terminator that reaches N·L = −0.1 at 11 m (0.33 of its step) but not at 2 m (0.04); the shadow side keeping the frame's tint × the material's, never black, and the band warming the transition's middle; the cast-shadow hook leaving a square that faces the key fully lit (one facing away keeps the tint alone); distance desaturation turning an albedo at 40 m into its luminance in the key light's hue; each tier's specular (full, approximate, and off with a metal shading as diffuse), the clamp and the hemisphere's reflection, and the mask map's AO, cavity and roughness, a zero specular mask leaving the sphere matte under grazing light; emission on either side, untouched by desaturation; two lamps on a floor through both light paths (the same frame within 64 pixels) and a lamp's terminator on a sphere; both families in one mesh and in another, each through its own shader, a coverage edit building one environment pipeline per format drawn with one draw-list rebuild, alpha test and blending, and a sky material refused on a mesh; the scene prepass holding the world's depth wherever the frame drew it, a morphed environment prop's included and the character prepass empty, no velocity while still and its projected motion when moved, and temporal AA converging (frame to frame a mean of 0.016 steps, one pixel of 16,384 over 2); an `environment.wgsl` edit rebuilding the environment's pipelines alone and swapping only the world's colors. Twenty mutations of the shader each fail a test: wrap, the width's growth, either shadow tint, the band, desaturation, the specular model, the clamp, emission, local lights and their terminator, the hook returning shadow, ambient light, its reflection, metals without specular, the mask map, a masked texel's grazing Fresnel and sheen, transparency and the alpha test; and drawing a morphed environment prop into the character prepass fails the prepass test. Headless: `EnvironmentParams`, `FrameUniforms` (with `shadow_tint` and `weather`), `InstanceData` and `LocalLight` against the environment program's WGSL; its slot, flag and specular-model constants, group 3 bindings and material binding; every environment variant under every specular model specializes and validates; the draw list groups mixed families by pipeline; each tier class's specular; the sample content's IDs, slots and winding.
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench 3000`, 1920×1080, Desktop-High with TAA, Apple M5, release, three alternating rounds against 6e5ad54 on a machine loaded by other agents' builds, load average 12–33):
    - Without `--environment` the frame is the base's: CPU p50 124.8–128.3 µs against 124.0–125.6, 407.7 allocations a frame on both, the GPU frame 1,257–1,403 µs against 1,252–1,348 and the main pass 266–302 against 271–288, within the rounds' noise.
    - With `--environment` (five more draws in the main pass and five in the scene prepass, about 17,000 vertices and 30,000 triangles, 1.07 MB more in the mesh pool): CPU p50 126.2–137.0 µs and 412.7 allocations (five more: wgpu recording the extra draws, the environment pipeline and the scene group's rebind; the renderer's per-frame code allocates nothing new); the main pass 304–323 µs, about 40 µs more though the courtyard takes most of the pixels the sky shaded; the scene prepass 103–122 µs against 81–83; the GPU frame 1,426–1,465 µs. Against §3.3's Desktop-Low column at 1080p: forward opaque (the whole main pass, sky included) 0.32 of 3.5 ms, culling and prepass 0.12 of 1.5 ms, the frame 1.47 of about 13.5 ms.
    - With each mobile tier's settings on the same GPU (two rounds, 4× MSAA, no TAA): the main pass grows from 156–163 to 202–258 µs on Mobile-High (approximate specular, 1316×740) and from 88–90 to 127–135 µs on Mobile-Low (no specular, 1049×590), against Mobile-Low's 8 ms for forward opaque; phones are measured in X1.
  - **The alpha test at one exit** (M1, the discard audit): Apple's Metal compiler has miscompiled a per-pixel `discard` taken before a fragment stage's end (docs/conventions.md, "Fragment stages that cut pixels out"). The alpha test decides early (`kept`), after every derivative; 2 × 2 quads it cuts whole leave there, before the lighting (`quad_any`, which joins `frame.wgsl`'s shared helpers), and any other pixel it cuts at `fs_environment`'s exit, after the final color. Opaque and transparent permutations compile without either. Every golden scene is byte-identical, and the early test it replaced drew identically on every tier. Cost at 1080p, release, minima over 10 to 16 alternating rounds on a loaded machine (load average 7–45): environment-day's main pass 312 µs against 330 on Desktop-High and 114 against 115 on Mobile-Low, the prepasses 134 against 145 and 16.2 against 16.3, within the noise (the courtyard has no alpha-tested surface). `tests/cutouts.rs` keeps the holes showing what lies behind them on every tier.
- **As built (M1 R4):** the cast-shadow hook returns `shadow_visibility(in.world_position, normalize(in.world_normal))` (`frame.wgsl`, "Lighting" above): the cascades, which hold the environment's casters, times every character's own shadow (its map on desktop, its capsule on phones), offset along the normal against acne, and 1 with shadows off. Cast shadows thereby take the terminator's remap, the tinted keep and the band, and lose the highlight, as W4 planned. An environment-family surface on a character's instance would receive as environment, its character's own shadow included; characters draw with the anime character family, so the hook reads no character index (it would need one more flat varying).
- **As built (M1 W3, fog: `sapphire_render::fog`, the fog section of `shaders/frame.wgsl`, `shaders/fog_card.wgsl`; look pillar 3):**
  - **One function at every final color.** `fogged(color, position, share)` (`frame.wgsl`) is applied once, at the single final-color point of every world pass: `fs_environment` (lit color plus emission), the anime character shader and its outlines (a line fogs as its surface does, at its own position and share), and fog cards; the sky takes `fogged_sky`, the same fog as at `fog::SKY_DISTANCE` (10⁶ m). `Fog::ray` is the same function on the CPU, for tests and tools. A frame whose fog has no density skips it on a uniform branch, so it draws exactly as before fog.
  - **The model.** Two homogeneous distance layers, near haze and far aerial perspective, each from its start distance, and an exponential height layer, `density × exp(−falloff × (y − base))`, integrated analytically along the ray (its series where the exponent's argument is near 0, horizontal rays included; exponents held under 30), from a camera inside the layer or above it alike. Transmittance is `exp(−share × depth)`. The key light's inscatter is a cos⁸ forward lobe in `Fog::sun_inscatter`'s color, added where the fog is.
  - **Decision: colored by the sky.** The far layer's color is a tint on the sky's horizon (white is the horizon itself), so geometry far enough away takes the sky's own color, how WuWa's and Genshin's distance melts into the painted sky; near haze and height fog keep authored colors.
  - **Decision: the sky takes the same fog, with a ceiling.** The distance layers fill the air up to `fog::CEILING` (30 m) above the camera, a ray's path through them ending where it has risen that far: the sky takes them as a band along the horizon (all fog at and below it) and the zenith keeps its color, and tall peaks rise out of the haze. `sky_color()`, which ambient reads, is unchanged. The sky cube's faces take the fog too.
  - **Decision: colors mix over the stretch the camera sees.** The layers' colors mix by their optical depths over the ray's first `fog::MIX_DEPTH` (4) optical depths at the densities around the camera, not over the whole ray: a long ray's far end, behind fog the camera can't see through, has other proportions (a rising ray leaves the height layer; a sky ray runs on to the ceiling), and weighing it in colored distant geometry and the sky beside it differently, a seam along the horizon wherever the layers' colors differ. Shorter rays, all of them in light fog, mix over their whole length.
  - **Decision: characters take a share of the fog** (`Fog::characters`, keyed by time of day, so an art director sets it per hour and a cutscene per shot): the optical depth of the frame's listed characters and their outlines is scaled by it (`fog_share`, the per-instance character index), so a character where the world melts away still reads as a figure. The default day keeps 40–50%. Anime character surfaces that aren't listed (props) fog as the world.
  - **Fog cards** draw on meshes: `MaterialFamily::FogCard` joins `MESH_FAMILIES` (a scene group of its own, which also binds the depth cards fade against), `Shading::FogCard` and `SurfaceVariant::FOG_CARD` (always transparent, no hull, no shadow, both faces), ten surface variants in all. A card's density is its map's R (its shape, soft at the edges) × its G read where it drifts by `scroll` (whole repeats an hour, so it never jumps when the shaders' clock wraps), × its opacity; it is lit by the fog (its tint × the fog's light at its distance, the horizon's where the frame has none, plus `sun_scatter` × the inscatter) and then fogged; it blends with straight alpha and keeps scene color's alpha (post's character mask). `facing` places it as the mesh is, turned whole toward the camera, or upright about the world's up axis (one card per instance, around its origin, keeping its scale). Cards draw after every other transparent variant, back to front by their instances' bounds, sorted each frame without allocating.
  - **Decision: soft cards where the prepass runs.** Every tier fades cards near the lens (`camera_fade`); the depth fade, a soft particle over `depth_fade` meters of view depth, reads the scene prepass's depth where the frame has it, the desktop tiers under temporal AA, where it costs nothing more (the main pass reads it as a texture: under temporal AA it is another texture than the main pass's depth). Phones' MSAA in tile memory has no prepass, and reading the main pass's depth would cost a store and a resolve, so there cards fade near the lens alone and their maps' soft edges carry them (the pipeline constant `FOG_CARD_DEPTH_FADE` off, a 1×1 stand-in bound). The frame caches the card group with the prepass's depth and makes it again only when the depth texture or the buffers change.
  - **Hot reload:** `fog_card.wgsl` is a `ShaderFile` and a mesh program (`frame.wgsl`, `materials.wgsl`, then it; fourteen files, eleven programs): an edit rebuilds the fog card pipelines built so far, and a `frame.wgsl` edit now rebuilds eight programs.
  - **Stats** (rule R9): `RenderStats::fog` (`FogStats`: layers with a density, fog card draws, fog card pipelines), in the overlay's stats panel; the `day` panel shows the resolved fog with swatches (each layer's color, the far one as the horizon it tints, the inscatter) and its starts, falloff, base and the characters' share.
  - **Pins:** every golden scene from before fog passes `--fog off` (sandbox: no fog in any frame and no fog cards), and the 36 render byte-identical (FLIP mean, p99 and max 0.000 on Metal).
  - **Content and sandbox:** the pipeline's `samples::environment` adds `mist_sheet.glb` and `haze_card.glb` (a unit quad each, slots `Mist` and `Haze` bound by their sidecars), their fog card materials (world-placed mist fading in from 8 m with a 4 m depth fade; upright haze) and `mist.png` (a two-channel mask: R a soft bank of mist, G a tiling octave). Where frames draw fog, `--environment` places four mist sheets across the valley from 30 m to the hills' feet and three haze cards among the far pillars, each an instance of a unit card.
  - **Golden images:** seven new scenes, Metal goldens only, with plain post and no shadows so they show fog alone: `fog-day`, `fog-sunset` and `fog-night` (the courtyard at 10:00, 18:30 and 22:00 with the lanterns' lights), `fog-valley` (sunrise's mist from higher and wider), `fog-cards` (the cards' vista at 7:30, temporal AA and the depth fade), `fog-cards-mobile` (the same on Mobile-Low, without it) and `fog-characters` (the outline row on the stage's floor in sunrise's mist: the floor melts into the horizon while the characters still read).
  - **Tests:** unit tests (`fog`): the height layer against numeric integration from inside and outside it and along horizontal rays, the distance layers' starts and ceiling, distant surfaces taking the sky's fog in every direction, the mixing stretch across the horizon, the characters' share and the lobe, and sanitized values; GPU tests (`tests/fog.rs`, Metal): every pixel of a glowing floor, a wall and the sky against `Fog::ray` within 0.02 in linear color, no seam where a far floor meets the sky, zero density drawing byte-identical to no fog, characters' share against their unfogged pixels, the cards' camera fade and three facings, the depth fade under temporal AA (a quarter left 0.5 m before a wall) and none without, and back-to-front order.
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench 3000`, 1920×1080, `--environment` at 10:00, Apple M5, release, three alternating rounds against 5955ca4; the GPU was shared with other agents' runs, load average 15–30, so these are minima over the rounds):
