    - With `--fog off` the frame is the base's: the main pass 991 µs against 984 on Desktop-High and 233 against 245 on Mobile-Low, 484.2 and 304.9 allocations a frame on both.
    - The fog alone (the cards left out by a temporary switch): about 90 µs more main pass on Desktop-High (1,077 µs) and 15–25 µs on Mobile-Low (257), every pixel of the world and the sky taking a few exponentials; no allocations.
    - The courtyard's seven cards, sheets tens of meters wide covering much of the frame: about 100 µs more on Desktop-High (1,178) and 40 µs on Mobile-Low (297), about 15 µs a large card, and 4 allocations a frame (8 on Desktop-High, whose cards also read the prepass's depth): wgpu recording the draws and their bindings. The card group with the prepass's depth is made once (one miss in 60 frames).
    - CPU p50 within the rounds' noise: 161–195 µs against 154–185 on Desktop-High, 106–123 against 106–177 on Mobile-Low.
- **As built (M1 W6, foliage: `sapphire_render::foliage`, `shaders/foliage.wgsl`, `pipelines::{Shading::Foliage, MeshPass::{Foliage, FoliagePrepass, FoliageShadow}}`; look pillar 7):**
  - **The model.** Foliage is lit by the environment's model, so trees stand in the world's picture rather than the characters': the world key light on the cook-time volume normals (§5.27), pushed toward the shadow side by the cast shadow and the canopy's occlusion (`occluded`), through the terminator remap whose width grows with view distance (the material's width plus `terminator_growth` per meter, at least half a pixel's change), wrapped Lambert, time of day's shadow tint × the material's, C2's ambient hemisphere (or the probe grid's, with capsule AO and GTAO, where M1 W5 has them: "Lighting", the W5 note) and distance desaturation. `wrapped` and `distance_amount` moved unchanged from `environment.wgsl` into `frame.wgsl` so both shaders share one copy; the environment draws exactly as before. On top: the color gradient from the material's root color to its tip color along the cooked gradient coordinate (root to tip, the canopy's inside to its outside), canopy occlusion darkening the inside and pushing it into the shade, vertex AO and the mask map's occlusion, and translucency, `pow(saturate(dot(−L, V)), power)` on the side facing away from the light, × the mask's thickness weighted toward the canopy's outside, kept out of cast shadow, so a canopy against a low sun glows at its edges. Local lights light the diffuse through the same terminator and wrap. No specular. W3's `fogged` covers the final color.
  - **Shadows (R4).** Foliage receives as the environment does (`shadow_visibility`: the cascades and every character's own shadow) and casts into the cascades through casters of its own (`MeshPass::FoliageShadow`), displaced by the wind as in the main pass and cut out by the alpha test, so shadows on the ground are the leaves' shapes and sway with them. Caster culling pads a foliage instance's bounds by as far as the frame's wind can carry its vertices (`foliage::wind_margin`: its materials' reach × the wind's strength × its strongest gust), so a leaf swaying past its cooked bounds stays in the cascades it shades. **Decision: cached cascades stay cached with foliage in them.** Their casters redraw on the cascade's turn (every other frame on every tier), so their foliage shadows lag the leaves by up to a frame and move at half the frame rate: at the sample tree's breeze a frame moves a branch tip at most 1.6 cm and a fully fluttering leaf 3.4 cm (4 and 9 cm at 2.5 times the breeze), against those cascades' texels of about 2.7 and 9.4 cm on Desktop-High at the sandbox's 43° lens, under a five-texel tent, on plants 15 m and more away; in a gale the far dapple steps at half rate. Redrawing both cached cascades every frame would bring back all the caster work caching saves (a fourth cascade a frame on Desktop-High), which far foliage's shadows don't earn.
  - **Wind** (`FramePacket::wind`, `Wind { direction, strength, gustiness }`, calm by default; rule R4: the world resolves it, the renderer sees plain data). Three levels, each the material's response (`Wind { sway, bend, flutter, flutter_frequency }`, meters at unit strength) × the vertex's cooked weight × the frame's strength: the trunk leans downwind and swings slowly, each plant in its own phase; branches swing faster in their cooked phases; leaves flutter in a direction that wanders across the canopy; gusts swell and travel downwind. The offset is continuous in the vertex's position, weights and phase; the bake ramps bend in from the root and gives each clump, blade and trunk one phase (§5.27), so trunks stay planted and move whole. `foliage::wind_offset` is the same function on the CPU (frequencies rounded half to even, as WGSL's `round`), and `foliage::wind_reach` bounds it for culling. Time reaches the GPU wrapped to `WIND_PERIOD` (1,024 s) in `f64`, and every oscillator runs whole cycles in the period, so the wrap never jumps. The foliage scene group holds `WindUniforms` (this frame's and the last drawn frame's wind) at binding 2; every pass that draws foliage displaces it alike, and the scene prepass's velocity takes the last frame's wind as well as its transform, so temporal AA reprojects swaying leaves without ghosts; after a camera cut the wind counts as unmoved.
  - **Coverage.** Alpha-tested cards and blades are two-sided (no culling), lit alike from both sides by their volume normals. A material with `alpha_cutoff` 0 draws opaque (`MaterialFlags` without `ALPHA_TEST`): trunks, branches, solid canopies, which are closed, so the main pass and the prepass cull their back faces (`foliage::cull`; an opaque card must be modeled with both sides) and the opaque prepass reads no map; casters stay two-sided, as every family's do. Alpha-tested cards discard below the cutoff, their alpha scaled up with the map's mip level (Golus) so distant canopies keep their fullness; on the desktop tiers temporal AA's jittered history resolves the edges, and the scene prepass (`MeshPass::FoliagePrepass`, binding group 3) and the casters test alike, so every depth matches. Under MSAA (the phones) the main pass writes alpha-to-coverage instead, the tier's `alpha_to_coverage`, through `fs_foliage_coverage`: the coverage sharpened to a pixel, `(alpha − cutoff) / fwidth(alpha) + 0.5`, covers that share of the samples through the sample mask, a run of samples starting where a hash of the pixel and the triangle's first vertex picks: a dither, so 4×'s five levels don't band and two half-covering edges stacked in a pixel cover different samples rather than leaving the canopy see-through there. **Decision: a written sample mask, not the hardware's alpha-to-coverage,** which would also leave the coverage in scene color's alpha, where P2's character mask needs 1 for the world.
  - **Meshes.** The foliage stream (§5.10, vertex slot 5) lives in a buffer of the mesh pool's own, numbered apart from the slab's vertices (the draw record's `foliage` base), so other meshes spend nothing on it. **Decision: foliage draws on static meshes only.** `upload_mesh` refuses a foliage material on a mesh without the stream (`RenderError::NoFoliageStream`) or on a skinned or morphed one (`RenderError::DeformedFoliage`): wind displaces in the vertex stage, from the static mesh; skinned foliage (vines, banners) is later work.
  - **Families on meshes.** `upload_mesh` and `update_mesh` now take anime character, environment, fog card and foliage materials (the sky family alone stays `NotDrawable`). `Shading::Foliage` joins `Shading::SURFACES` after the environment, so in each coverage foliage draws after the world it stands on; its transparent variant is never used (foliage is never transparent), which keeps the grid regular. With C5's parts the grid is three coverages × six shadings (body, face, hair, eye, the environment, foliage) and the fog card, nineteen surface variants; `DEPTH_WRITING` is the first twelve, `SHARED_DEPTH` those ten that aren't foliage's (what the shared scene prepass and casters draw), `FOLIAGE` foliage's two, and C5's `CHARACTER_DEPTH_WRITING`, `OUTLINED` and `THROUGH_HAIR` are unchanged (foliage is never a character: it stays out of the character prepass). `MaterialFamily::Foliage` joins `MESH_FAMILIES` with a scene group of its own (instances, its material buffer, the wind). Foliage's prepass and casters draw after the shared prepass and casters, through their own pipelines (they bind group 3 and the foliage stream).
  - **Tiers** (`QualityTier::foliage`, `FoliageSettings`; the pipeline constants `FOLIAGE_WIND_LEVELS` and `FOLIAGE_TRANSLUCENCY`; alpha-to-coverage picks `fs_foliage_coverage` and passes the sample count as `FOLIAGE_SAMPLES`): `wind_levels` (0–3), `alpha_to_coverage` and `translucency`. Both desktop tiers and Mobile-High run every level with translucency; the phones cover cards' edges with alpha-to-coverage (the desktop tiers draw without MSAA, so they alpha-test); Mobile-Low keeps trunks and branches moving but drops the leaves' flutter, which a 590-row scene barely resolves, and translucency. A table without it reads the desktop tiers' foliage.
  - **Hot reload:** `foliage.wgsl` is a `ShaderFile` and a mesh program (`frame.wgsl`, `materials.wgsl`, `lights.wgsl`, then it; fifteen files, twelve programs): an edit rebuilds the foliage pipelines built so far (main, prepass and casters), and a `frame.wgsl` edit now rebuilds nine programs (with C5's character prepass always built, twelve pipelines in `hot_reload.rs`'s first scene and sixteen in its second).
  - **Stats** (rule R9): `RenderStats::foliage_draws` (of `draws`), `foliage_instances` and `foliage_pipelines`, in the overlay's stats panel (the draws row reads "… foliage on … plants"); the panel's GPU section gains rows for the culling and prepass and the forward main pass against §3.3's budgets (`sapphire_debug::budget`), where foliage's time lands with the shadows'.
  - **Content and sandbox:** the pipeline's `samples::foliage` (§5.27) writes a tree, a bush and a patch of grass with their maps and materials, all generated. The sandbox's `--foliage grove|backlit|ground` plants two trees, three bushes and nine grass patches in the courtyard (implying `--environment`) in a breeze (strength 1, gustiness 0.3) and frames the grove whole, the big tree against the key light, or the grass and a bush up close; `--wind <0..4>` sets the strength.
  - **Golden images:** five new scenes, Metal goldens only, with plain post and no fog so they show foliage alone, shadows on: `foliage-day` (Desktop-High, TAA), `foliage-backlit` (18:30 through the big tree's crown), `foliage-ground` (low over the grass by a bush), `foliage-mobile` (Mobile-Low: alpha-to-coverage under 4× MSAA, two wind levels) and `foliage-wind` (2.5 times the breeze, playing for the 32 frames up to tick 150). No earlier scene plants foliage and the default wind is calm, so the 49 earlier scenes (C5's six included) need no pin and render byte-identical (FLIP mean, p99 and max 0.000 on Metal). Rebased onto C5, the four scenes that show the character took its depth-offset rim: against their goldens from before, FLIP mean / p99 `foliage-day` 0.0006 / 0.0113, `foliage-ground` 0.0037 / 0.180, `foliage-mobile` 0.0007 / 0.0106 and `foliage-wind` 0.0007 / 0.0171, every pixel past one code value on the character and its anime-shaded pedestal (which lost C1's Fresnel rim, as in C5's scenes); `foliage-backlit`, with no character in view, is unchanged.
  - **Tests (Metal, `tests/foliage.rs`):** wind moves cards alike in every pass and the prepass's velocity follows the displacement between frames (a card swept 3.4 pixels); alpha-to-coverage softens card edges under MSAA where the tier asks (139 partly covered pixels on the phone tiers, none on the desktop tiers, which alpha-test); casters cut cards out (the shadow under a card's clear half matches open ground); the scene prepass cuts cards out exactly as the main pass draws them (3,766 drawn pixels, none mismatched); the frame's fog covers foliage as `Fog::ray` predicts (within 0.02); foliage needs its stream on a static mesh and is counted. Unit tests (`foliage`): the wind's state horizontal, unit and wrapped; calm, still tiers and roots never moving; each level adding its own motion within its reach; no vertex carried past its culling margin; continuity across the period's wrap; frequencies rounding half to even; velocity against the last recorded frame's wind; the tiers' settings. The shadow plan keeps swaying foliage in the cascades its margin reaches.
  - **Cost** (measured before W6 was rebased onto C5, which changed no foliage code; sandbox `--bench 3000`, 1920×1080, Apple M5, release, six alternating rounds against a560a84 on a machine shared with other agents' builds and GPU runs, load average 11–37, so these are minima over the rounds; GPU times are the passes' mean exclusive times):
    - Without foliage (`--environment`) the frame is the base's: on Desktop-High the main pass 1,115 µs against 1,136, the prepasses 56 against 63, the shadows 267 against 270, the GPU frame 2,537 against 2,575; on Mobile-Low the main pass 289 against 290 and the shadows 33 against 36; CPU p50 and allocations (493.2 and 308.9 a frame) unchanged.
    - The grove (`--foliage grove`: two trees, three bushes and nine grass patches, 14 plants in 16 draws) against the same camera with no plants (a temporary switch in a scratch build): on Desktop-High the main pass 1,595 µs against 1,156 (+439), the scene prepass 178 against 60 (+118), the shadows 698 against 242 (+456: 42 more caster draws over three cascades a frame) and the GPU frame 3,615 against 2,564, about 1.05 ms, 75 µs a plant on average (31 main, 8 prepass, 33 shadows), most of it the cards' alpha-tested overdraw. Against §3.3's Desktop-Low column the frame's forward pass is 1.6 of 3.5 ms, culling and prepass 0.18 of 1.5 and shadows 0.70 of 2.0, on a much faster GPU than that column's. On Mobile-Low (1049×590, 4× MSAA, alpha-to-coverage) the main pass 519 µs against 292 (+227) and the shadows 70 against 31 (+39), about 20 µs a plant, against 8.0 and 3.0 ms; phones are measured in X1.
    - CPU p50 within the rounds' noise (194 against 176 µs on Desktop-High, 117 against 111 on Mobile-Low). Allocations grow with the draws recorded, about one a draw as for W3's and W4's (wgpu's recording): 70.5 more a frame on Desktop-High (25.9 with shadows off, for the main pass's and prepass's 32 draws) and 29.1 on Mobile-Low.
    - Review fixes (opaque foliage culling its back faces and skipping the map in its prepass, the dithered coverage mask, the casters' wind margin; the bake's new phases cost nothing at run time): the grove in ten alternating rounds against 70f3dd1's build on the same content, minima, on the shared machine under heavier load than above. Desktop-High: the main pass 1,610 µs against 1,614, the prepasses 188 against 192, the shadows 740 against 727 with 62 caster draws in both, within the noise; Mobile-Low: the main pass 549 against 536 (the dither's hash, about 2%), the shadows 66 against 69 with two more caster draws (18 against 16: foliage padded into a cascade it can sway into). CPU p50 and allocations unchanged (184.6 against 184.5 µs and 581.7 a frame on Desktop-High, 126.5 against 122.5 and 393.0 on Mobile-Low).
  - **Later** (the `foliage` module's later milestones): the shadow casters are the grove's largest cost on Desktop-High, 698 µs against 242 without the plants (+456 µs: 42 caster draws over the three cascades drawn a frame, at 2,048²), most of it the cards' alpha-tested overdraw. Two ways to bring it down: casters without the leaves' flutter (a level that moves leaves by centimeters, under a cascade texel past the nearest cascade), and a caster LOD (fewer, larger cards, or the canopy's volume drawn opaque, in the farther cascades). Fine for M1 at the grove's size.
  - **Cutouts at one exit** (M1, the discard audit; docs/conventions.md, "Fragment stages that cut pixels out"): the main pass already cut at its exit and the casters have nothing after their cut; the scene prepass now computes its targets and then cuts, a plain late discard, since nothing after the test is worth skipping. Every golden scene is byte-identical. Cost at 1080p, release, minima over 10 to 16 alternating rounds on a loaded machine: foliage-day's main pass 734 µs against 741 on Desktop-High and 472 against 474 on Mobile-Low, the prepasses 656 against 727 and 9.0 against 9.4, within the noise. `tests/cutouts.rs` keeps a card's holes showing what lies behind them on every tier and the prepass cutting the same holes; `shadow_casters_cut_cards_out` keeps the casters' cut.
- **As built (M1, keyed emission: time of day's scale on the environment's emission; §5.21 has the keys):**
  - **The shader:** `fs_environment` multiplies a material's emission by `frame.emission_scale` mixed toward 1 by the share the material keeps steady, `emission × mix(scale, 1, steady)`, then by its emission map, before `fogged` as before. At a scale of 1 with no steady share, as in every existing scene, the factor is exactly 1, so every existing golden is byte-identical.
  - **The material:** `EnvironmentMaterial::emission_steady` (0 to 1, default 0, validated) is the share of its emission that shines the same at every hour. 0 leaves all of it to time of day, as paper lanterns and lit windows want; 1 keeps it as authored, for a sign or a crystal that glows by day too. It packs into `EnvironmentParams::emission.a`, which held 0, so the block's layout and every material cooked before it are unchanged: no Material version, no recook, no new binding. Documents write it only when it's set, so those from before it read and write as they did.
  - **Decision: one steady share per material, not a curve per material.** One global scale can't keep a beacon lit by day while the lanterns around it dim, and the share is the least that can: a number per material in a slot the block already had. A curve per material would put time-of-day keys in materials, or a timeline per material, for no case M1's content has; a place whose emission keeps hours of its own (a night market) is a zone that overrides the scale there.
  - **Who doesn't follow it:** characters' emission (C7's layers): a character's glow is part of their design, the same at noon and at night, and characters' bloom already fits it to the frame. Foliage and fog cards have no emission; the sky's discs and stars have keys of their own. Local lights are the app's: the look-dev stage's lamps still follow `day_night`.
  - **Tests (Metal, `tests/environment.rs`):** `time_of_day_scales_emission_but_for_its_steady_share` draws three squares, all keyed, half steady and all steady, under scales of 1, 0.1, 0 and 2, against the CPU model; the frame uniforms' tests hold the sanitized scale and its offset, and the packet's tests its default and reset.
  - **Cost:** one uniform read and a `mix` per environment pixel. Sandbox `--bench 3000` at 1920×1080 on an M5, release, in alternating rounds against 3f0f89e's build in its own target dir, on a machine at load average 20–40 (minima): the courtyard (`--environment`, nine rounds) main pass 1,417 µs against 1,465, GPU frame 3,616 against 3,682; the look-dev town (`--look-dev wide`, three rounds) main pass 2,022 against 2,054, GPU frame 5,256 against 5,416. The courtyard's main pass alone spread from 1.4 to 2.7 ms between runs, so nothing is measurable. Allocations are the base's (555.2 and 690.2 a frame), and the cooked packs are byte-identical.

**Post-processing**

- **Bloom:** a downsample chain from 1/2 to 1/32 resolution, with an emissive-driven bloom mask.
- **Tonemapping:** a custom tone curve and grading baked into a 32³ LUT. No ACES look. Two LUTs can blend (time of day, zones), and a user filter-strength slider scales the grade, as both target games offer.
- **Anime compositing:** a wide "diffusion" glow on top of bloom, a character bloom mask, 2–4 screen-space gradient ("para") layers and sprite lens flares.
- **Gameplay-triggered effects:** finish-freeze, halftone, radial blur and chromatic aberration, triggered from `PostParams`.
- **Desktop only:** depth of field in cutscenes and optional camera motion blur.
- **Mobile:** bloom composite, LUT, vignette and gameplay effects are fused into one full-screen pass to save bandwidth.
- **As built (M1 R3, `sapphire_render::post`):**
  - The main pass renders unclamped linear HDR into an `Rgba16Float` scene-color transient, MSAA resolving into it. `Renderer::new` fails cleanly if the device can't render, filter and resolve that format. The target keeps encoding sRGB on write.
  - Bloom is a dual-filter chain (Bjørge, SIGGRAPH 2015) of frame-graph passes on pooled transients: a prefilter at ½ (exposure, soft-knee threshold, Karis average), five-tap downsamples to 1/32, and tent upsamples back to ½, each replacing `scatter` of its own level. `RenderSettings::bloom_levels` (5; at most 6) sets the length for the tiers. Levels are `Rg11b10Ufloat` where the device renders it. Bloom follows brightness alone until the emissive and character masks (C7, P2).
  - The tone curve is GT (Uchimura, CEDEC 2017) per channel: linear midtones and a short, late shoulder. A hue-preserving shoulder was tried and dropped: it couples the channels near white more than a 32³ table can follow (about two display steps of ripple along the grey axis).
  - The curve and grade are baked on the CPU when `PostParams` change: about 2.6 ms of CPU, 0.5 ms across the job pool on an M5. The LUT is 32³ `Rgb10a2Unorm` holding sRGB-encoded display color, indexed through a log shaper (ε = 1/32, maximum 16). Scene-referred grading is per channel (white balance as RGB gains, filter, contrast); saturation, lift, gain and gamma act on display values. The table then stays within 1 display step of direct evaluation for a neutral grade, 1.5 on the lit sky, and 2.5 for saturated colors at white.
  - Two LUTs blend per pixel. A key already baked in either slot is reused (least recently used is replaced), so a time-of-day transition costs one bake. Filter strength is baked as a blend toward the neutral grade, so 0 is exactly the tone curve alone.
  - One fused pass does exposure, bloom, the vignette, the shaper and both LUTs (the second fetched only while blending) on every tier.
  - Cost against M1 F3 (sandbox `--bench 3000` at 1920×1080 with 4× MSAA, Apple M5, release): the graph grows from 2 to 12 passes, which crosses the executor's parallel threshold (6 command buffers instead of 1). CPU p50 goes from 29 to 91 µs, allocations from 70 to 294 per frame, and GPU wait from about 255 to 460–515 µs. Post's own code allocates only the graph's boxed closures (bind groups are cached per pass); the rest is wgpu, about 13 allocations and 6 µs per render pass on Metal, plus the parallel encoders. Recording all 12 passes serially allocates 205 per frame but takes longer, so the per-pass cost hint the graph lists as later work is the lever. Pass timestamps put most of the post GPU time in the half-size passes (prefilter, last upsample) and the full-size composite; smaller levels cost about 20 µs each, mostly fixed per-pass overhead.
- **As built (M1 P2, `sapphire_render::post`, `shaders/post.wgsl`; look pillars 3 and 5):** anime compositing's layers over R3's post, all in the fused pass on every tier (phones need one pass; desktop draws the same), driven by `PostParams` from time of day's keys (§5.21) and the player's settings.
  - **The diffusion glow** (`Diffusion`): the bloom chain's widest smooth level, the up level just above the smallest (1/16 of post's input with five levels, 1/8 with Mobile-Low's four: about the same share of the frame at each tier's own size), through a soft threshold of its own (its knee half the threshold, so nothing below half of it glows), `color × (1 − exp(−intensity × light))`, screened over the graded frame's sRGB-encoded values. One bilinear tap; the chain runs for it even with bloom off. On a lone bright sphere its light 3–6 radii out is 1.31 of what it has 1.5–3 radii out, against bloom's 0.87: wide and flat where bloom falls off fast. Screening lifts what is dark around bright light and never pushes past white, so it can't blow out a face.
  - **The character mask** (`CharacterBloom`): characters bloom with a threshold and a strength of their own, as multiples of the world's (the default day: ×1.3–1.5 and ×0.4–0.6), in the bloom prefilter, so faces lit near white hold while the world glows. **Decision: scene color's alpha, on every tier,** holding the world's share of each pixel: the anime character shader writes 0 for the instances the frame lists as characters (`FramePacket::characters`) and 1 for the rest; every other pass writes 1 already (the clear, the sky, outlines, and W4's environment and any later opaque pass without knowing of the mask), and transparent surfaces keep what is under them. MSAA's resolve averages it into partial edges. Under TAA the history's alpha holds R7's accumulation weight, so the prefilter reads the mask from the frame's scene color before the resolve, at the same coordinates (jittered by under a scene pixel, which half-size bloom doesn't show). R7's responsive mask is not used: it exists only with TAA, marks skinned and morphed instances rather than the frame's characters (the sandbox's mannequin is a character but static), and can't hold the fractional values C7's breathing emission will write into alpha.
  - **Gradient ("para") layers** (`GradientLayer`, at most four): linear from any edge or radial around a point (round on screen), ramped by a smoothstep from full opacity at `start` to none at `end`, in normal, multiply, screen, overlay, soft-light (the W3C formula) or add mode, composited in order over the graded frame's sRGB-encoded values, as compositing tools blend. The four modes affine in the frame's value are folded on the CPU into a scale and an offset, so the shader has three cases (affine, overlay, soft light) rather than six. The player's filter strength scales their opacity: they are the frame's other color filter.
  - **Lens flares** (`LensFlare`): the default lens's eight elements (`post::layers::DEFAULT_LENS`: a warm ring and an anamorphic streak on the sun, colored disc and hexagon ghosts and a far ring along the axis from the sun through the middle of the frame), added to the scene's light before the vignette and the grade, so they are graded with the frame; a pixel outside a box around an element (and inside a ring's hole) skips it. **Decision: occlusion is measured on the frame itself, on every tier.** The fused pass's vertex shader samples post's input at five points within half the sun disc's radius and compares their exposed luminance with half the disc's own, once a frame (a flat varying), so a character, the world or a cloud in front of the sun dims the flare by how much of the disc it hides, and a sun off screen draws none. Phones have no depth to read (the MSAA depth stays in tile memory and the scene prepass doesn't run), and depth would miss the clouds, which draw at the far plane. The disc's place and size on screen come from the main view's unjittered projection, the packet's `Celestial` and the sky's disc (`SunOnScreen`, on the CPU).
  - **The vignette** is R3's, extended with a `color` the corners multiply toward (black is R3's own); the default day tints it navy, violet or a warm dark at night and sunset.
  - **Sharpening** (`PostParams::sharpening`, the player's: `post.sharpening`, `--sharpening`): RCAS (FSR 1's robust contrast-adaptive sharpening, written from AMD's published algorithm) on exposed light compressed by its luma, five taps at the output's resolution (bilinear from a smaller scene, so below render scale 1 without TAA it sharpens the bilinear upscale). **Off by default, after measuring where it helps:** the sandbox at 640×360 at 10:00 with the layers on, against a 16-sample reference (4× MSAA at twice the size, averaged down), mean display steps / p99 / pixels more than 32 steps off, strength 0 → 0.5 → 1: native 4× MSAA 0.64 / 10 / 261 → 0.74 / 10 / 314 → 1.14 / 19 / 624; native TAA 0.73 / 10 / 253 → 0.74 / 10 / 237 → 1.05 / 17 / 269; TAAU at two-thirds 0.91 / 13 / 417 → 0.91 / 13 / 391 → 1.17 / 17 / 473; TAAU at half 1.25 / 19 / 1,238 → 1.24 / 19 / 1,202 → 1.46 / 21 / 1,210; the bilinear upscale phones use without TAA at 0.7 1.37 / 22 / 1,375 → 1.35 / 22 / 1,334 → 1.51 / 23 / 1,375 and at 0.5 2.03 / 34 / 2,487 → 2.00 / 34 / 2,506 → 2.00 / 36 / 2,574. So it helps nowhere measurably: at 0.25–0.5 on upsampled frames (TAAU, or the bilinear upscale) it trims the mean by at most 0.03 steps and the badly-off pixels by 2–4%, which reads as crispness rather than accuracy; at native resolution and at full strength it is worse everywhere, ringing up to 18 display steps past hard edges (R7's halos). It stays a player option, for upsampled frames.
  - **Variants.** Pipeline-overridable constants pick each frame's variants from what it draws (`post::layers::Variant`): `POST_LAYERS` and `POST_SHARPEN` in the fused pass (`fs_composite`, with `vs_composite` measuring the flare), `POST_CHARACTERS` in the prefilter. Every variant is built with the renderer (eight post pipelines, all rebuilt by a `post.wgsl` edit), so a layer turning on never compiles mid-game (since post's lazy pipelines, below, unless the renderer's settings leave the layers out). The gradient layers and the flare's elements are unrolled calls, not loops: wgpu bounds every WGSL loop with a counter, which keeps Metal from unrolling it (R7's finding), and the loops cost about three times as much. Parameters without layers (`PostParams::plain`: a timeline from before them, `--plain-post`) draw through the plain variants, which are R3's shader code: the 19 earlier golden scenes, pinned to `--plain-post`, are pixel-identical to their goldens.
  - **Data.** `PostParams` gains `diffusion`, `character_bloom`, `gradients`, `lens_flare` and `sharpening`, and `Vignette::color`; their defaults draw nothing, so `PostParams::default()` is still R3's look exactly. `PostUniforms` appends the layers' constants (now 768 bytes; a headless test checks every field's offset against the WGSL, and every combination of the three constants specializes). Group 1 of the fused pass gains the diffusion level (five textures), visible with the constants to its vertex shader. `RenderStats::post` (`PostStats`, rule R9) says which layers drew and whether the bloom chain ran; the overlay's stats panel shows it, with a "Bloom + tonemap + LUT" row timing `post::PASSES` against this section's budget, and the time-of-day panel the resolved layers.
  - **Tests** (Metal, `post::gpu_tests`): the diffusion glow is exactly off at 0, grows with its intensity, centers on its source, holds its light far out where bloom falls off, and lets less through as its threshold rises, nothing at all far below it; two equally bright spheres, a character and the world, bloom alike with neutral settings (1.000 with 4× MSAA, 1.005 with TAA) and the character far less with its own (0.175 and 0.160 of the world's; half the strength gives 0.54 and 0.53), the world's unchanged; every blend mode matches its formula at the layer's edge and halfway down, from the top, the right and a radial center, layers compose in order, and filter strength 0 leaves the frame exactly plain; the lens flare's ghosts sit along the axis within 1.5 px of where the lens puts them, a sphere covering the sun puts the flare out (under 1%), one covering part of it dims it, and a sun off the frame draws none; the vignette halves the corners or tints them blue and leaves the middle exactly; sharpening steepens an edge at render scales 1 and 0.5, leaves flat regions, rings by less than the edge's own contrast, draws the plain frame when off and next to it at 10⁻⁶; with every layer on, Mobile-Low's settings (590 rows of 1080, four bloom levels) draw a frame 0.56 mean steps from Desktop-Low's where their plain frames are 0.45 apart, while the layers move the frame by 7.6; an edited `post.wgsl` rebuilds the eight pipelines and draws the same pixels. Headless: the layers' packing (variants, character thresholds, gradient directions and ramps, the sun's place and size on screen) and the uniform layout.
  - **Golden images.** The 19 earlier scenes pin `--plain-post` and are pixel-identical, W4's three environment scenes among them (their lavapipe and WARP goldens stay as committed). Three new scenes, Metal goldens only, each identical run to run: `post-day` (10:00 through TAA, 32 still frames: the clouds' soft glow, the sky deepened at the top of the frame, the ground's lavender shade at the bottom, a soft pool of light on the character, whose skin and face keep their values), `post-sunset` (18:30 through Mobile-Low's settings: a warm, dreamy glow over the clouds and the horizon, violet from above, the ground in deep violet shade, the character crisp in front) and `post-flare` (18:30 looking into the sun: its warm halo and streak, colored ghosts and a far ring down the axis, the glow around the sun and the character's lit edge). Checked by eye for the anime look: the glow is soft and wide without blowing out faces (skin pixels keep their values to within 5 steps, and a sunset face clipped at 255 in red comes back to 251), the gradients read as painted tints rather than haze, and nothing looks filmic.
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench 3000` at 1920×1080, release, Apple M5, three alternating rounds against 6e5ad54, before rebasing onto W4 and A9, on a machine shared with four other agents' builds and GPU work, so the spread is wide and the quietest rounds, those whose main pass ran at its usual 270–300 µs, are quoted first; post's passes are summed only over frames whose timings hold all of them and the main pass, which the bench now does: some frames come back with passes' timestamps missing, which left their time in the next pass's):
    - **Desktop-Low** (TAA, five bloom levels) at 10:00 with the default day's layers (the glow, three gradients, characters' bloom; the sun behind the camera): post, the bloom chain and the fused pass, takes 461 µs against 221–224 µs (the fused pass 262 against 74–75, the chain 200 against 146–149, mostly the character mask's five extra taps in the prefilter), and 695–859 µs against 353–434 under load: inside §3.3's 0.7 ms for "Bloom + tonemap + LUT", on an M5 rather than its GTX 1060-class reference. Looking into the sunset sun, the flare drawn too: 662–707 µs against 352 (754–1,038 against 407–561 under load), at the budget's edge on this GPU.
    - **Mobile-Low's settings** (a 1049×590 scene, four bloom levels, 4× MSAA, the fused pass at the 1080p output): 474–660 µs against 231–238 (the fused pass 335–479 against 112–118), and 481–915 against 190–322 with the flare, against §3.3's 2.5 ms for an A14-class phone. The M5's GPU is several times a phone's, and a phone's fused pass runs at its native resolution (about 3 megapixels), so on Mobile-Low the layered fused pass will be near or over that budget: device measurements (X1, M2) decide whether Mobile-Low drops the flare or a gradient layer, which the variants make free to do per tier.
    - **By layer** (a scratch harness timing the fused pass alone at 1080p, p10 over 190 frames, on the same loaded machine): the plain pass 75–110 µs; the layered variant with nothing to draw about +30; the diffusion glow +60–100; three gradient layers +80–130; the flare with the sun in view +120–170; sharpening +140; everything but sharpening +260. Before the gradient and flare loops were unrolled, the flare alone cost about +480 and three gradient layers +300.
    - With `--plain-post` the branch draws R3's code and matches the base: the fused pass 124–125 µs against 132 in matched rounds.
    - CPU p50 moves within the rounds' spread. Allocations drop from 407.7 to 385.7 a frame on the desktop tiers (Mobile-Low stays at 264.4): post's bind-group caches keep two entries now, so TAA's alternating history no longer rebuilds them every frame.
- **As built (M1 P3, `sapphire_render::post`, `shaders/post.wgsl`; look pillar 8):** combat's screen effects in post's fused pass on every tier, as resolved values from the world's effect stack (§5.21's P3 note), the hooks M2 authors real effects with.
  - **Data.** `PostParams::effects` (`PostEffects`) holds one value per kind: `RadialBlur` (center, strength: the share of the way toward the center a pixel's streak reaches), `ChromaticAberration` (center, strength), `Inversion` (amount), `Duotone` (amount, dark and light colors, luma threshold and softness) and `SpeedLines` (opacity, center, count, width, clear radius, how far the tips scatter past it, color, seed), the three color effects each with an `EffectCoverage` (their weight on the world and on characters). `PostEffects::NONE`, the default, draws nothing; `PostParams::plain` keeps the effects, which are gameplay's rather than the look's. `PostUniforms` appends `GpuEffects` (160 bytes, 928 in all); the headless layout test checks every field's offset against the WGSL. Hit points may lie off screen: centers are clamped to −1–2 of the frame, strengths to their working ranges (chromatic aberration's to 0.25), counts to 1–1024.
  - **The lens effects** replace the scene's texel, before exposure, and replace sharpening (a streaked or split frame has nothing to sharpen). **The radial blur** averages the frame along the line from the pixel `strength` of the way toward its center over the tier's taps (`PostEffectSettings::radial_blur_taps` in `tiers.ron`: 16 on Desktop-High, 12 on Desktop-Low, 8 on both phone tiers), unrolled in groups of four behind the pipeline constant `POST_RADIAL_TAPS`; each pixel's taps start at an interleaved gradient noise offset, so few taps read as fine grain rather than copies of the frame. **Chromatic aberration** samples red nearer its center and blue farther, by a split growing with the distance from it (in half frame diagonals), so fringes grow toward the corners: three taps, green the pixel's own. Under a radial blur each channel weighs the blur's taps by a tent over its own window of the ray instead, so the colors spread along the streaks for no extra taps.
  - **The color effects** act on the display values after P2's gradient layers, in order: the inversion; the duotone (luma through a smoothstep ramp from the dark color to the light one; with both, the duotone of the inverted frame); speed lines in their own color. The lines radiate from their center outside a clear radius, one per slot of a circle cut into `count` slots, each line's width, place in its slot and tip from PCG hash bits of the slot and the seed, a wedge from a point at its tip to its full width half a frame height out, its edges box-filtered over one pixel. The stack redraws them by changing the seed at the layer's rate.
  - **The character mask.** A color effect's coverage weighs characters and the world apart, so an impact frame turns the world two-tone and keeps characters colored, or speed lines draw behind them. It is P2's mask, scene color's alpha, bound as the fused pass's sixth texture only when an effect covers the two differently (`DrawnEffects::character_mask`; otherwise post's input is bound there again). **Decision: no sharp mask at the output's size, and nothing through temporal AA's resolve.** Under TAA the fused pass reads the mask bilinear from the jittered scene color before the resolve; along a row through a character against the resolved frame, no pixel lands on the wrong side of its edge by more than a pixel, and none is partial (with 4× MSAA, two partial pixels across its two edges, the resolve's antialiased edge). So P1's resolve carries nothing new. Below render scale 1 the mask is as sharp as the scene it comes from, as everything upsampled is. Cut-ins' hard-edged character cut-outs (M2) may need a mask carried through the resolve.
  - **Variants, decided by cost on Mobile-Low's settings.** Two pipeline constants, `POST_LENS_EFFECTS` and `POST_COLOR_EFFECTS`, make 12 fused-pass variants (`post::layers::Variant`: with or without the layers and the color effects, reading the scene as it is, sharpened or through the lens effects), 16 post pipelines in all, built with the renderer (since post's lazy pipelines, below, the effects' when the app prewarms them). Within each, an effect is a branch every pixel takes alike. Measured with a scratch harness timing the fused pass alone at 1080p on Mobile-Low's settings (M5, configurations interleaved frame by frame on a loaded machine): with a single variant for all five effects, a lone chromatic aberration's fused pass took 13–15% longer than with the lens effects in a variant of their own, a lone radial blur 2–13%, and a lone color effect about 4% longer than in a color-only variant. Compiling only the effect drawn would save a lone color effect a further 5–13% (each effect's code costs the others even with its branch untaken), but per-effect constants make 2⁵ variants for every combination of the layers and sharpening, and the effects of a hit mostly play together anyway (an impact frame needs all of them).
  - **An empty stack is free:** with no effect the frame draws P2's variants and constants, so every earlier golden scene is pixel-identical without pinning anything, and the GPU test draws the same bytes as before effects existed. Stats (rule R9): `RenderStats::post.effects` (`DrawnEffects`) lists the effects that drew and whether they read the mask; the overlay's stats panel shows it with the stack's depth.
  - **Tests** (Metal, `post::gpu_tests::effects`): no effect draws exactly the frame from before effects, and effects at weight 0 draw nothing; the inversion and the duotone map display values as their formulas say, including on the inverted frame; the mask keeps a character's colors within a step while the world's sphere and the sky turn light, or the reverse, with 4× MSAA and with TAA (above); chromatic aberration at strength 0.2 moves a sphere's red image outward by 5.0 px and its blue inward by 3.7 px near the frame's side (4.4 and 3.8 expected from the split), its green the frame's own to the byte; the radial blur streaks a sphere along its ray (the light's variance along the ray grows 4.5× and across it 1.5×) and moves its light outward, and 4 taps draw within 0.2 mean steps of 12; speed lines stay outside their clear radius exactly, redraw with the seed and repeat with it, and pass behind a character; Mobile-Low's effects draw within 0.1 mean steps of Desktop-Low's while they move the frame by 51 steps. Headless: packing and clamping, taps validation, the layout, and every combination of the five switches with every tap count specializing.
  - **Golden images** (Metal only): three new scenes, each identical run to run, push the sample presets held on one frame. `effects-blur-lines`: the radial blur and white speed lines over the default day through TAA; the frame zooms toward its middle, the lines radiate from outside a clear middle, and the character (with its pedestal, part of its mesh) stays in front of them. `effects-impact-frame`: `impact_frame` 0.03 s in, through TAA; the world flat cream under black speed lines, the character in its own colors in front of them, softened and fringed by the blur and chromatic aberration, as Zenless Zone Zero's two-tone hit frames read. `effects-chromatic-inversion`: through Mobile-Low's settings, the inverted frame with colored fringes growing toward the corners. No earlier scene needed pinning: all 32 are pixel-identical (FLIP max 0.000).
  - **Cost** (Apple M5, release, 1920×1080):
    - **The fused pass alone** (the scratch harness, p10 of 200–1,500 frames per configuration, interleaved; over P2's layers, then over plain post): Desktop-High (16 taps): the radial blur +303 / +412 µs over a 222 / 78 µs pass, chromatic aberration +22 / +29, the inversion +47 / +37, the duotone +46 / +37, speed lines +109 / +95, the impact frame (all but the inversion) +393 / +445. Mobile-Low's settings (8 taps, a 1049×590 scene): the blur +333 / +254 over 213 / 80, chromatic aberration +34 / +27, the inversion +55 / +26, the duotone +61 / +25, speed lines +84 over plain post, the impact frame +436 / +488. The blur's taps cost about 70 µs per four at 1080p (+150, +210, +280 and +365 µs for 4, 8, 12 and 16 over the layers on Desktop-High's settings); the per-pixel noise offset 30–45 µs of that, in texture cache misses.
    - **The sandbox** (`--bench 3000`, three alternating rounds against a release build of 5955ca4 on a machine shared with other agents' builds and GPU work; the quiet rounds quoted, those whose main pass ran at its usual 347–354 µs): on Desktop-High (TAA, 16 taps) at 10:00 with the default day's layers, post takes 433.6–433.9 µs with an empty stack against the base's 433.6–434.8 (the fused pass 248.3–248.4 against 248.3–248.9). With one preset held: chromatic aberration 460.9 µs, the inversion 481.6, the duotone 481.6–483.1, speed lines 553.8–564.3, the radial blur 736.6 (the fused pass 545.9) and the impact frame 820.9–850.0 (the fused pass 635.2–656.4); the bloom chain stays at 185–194 µs. Desktop-Low (12 taps) had no quiet round: its quietest give the impact frame 795.0–811.9 µs (the fused pass 583.6–595.9) and the empty stack 466.6.
    - **Against §3.3's 0.7 ms for "Bloom + tonemap + LUT":** the effects are transients of a few tenths of a second per hit, but while they play the radial blur and the impact frame take post past the budget on Desktop-High's settings, by 37 µs (the blur) and 121–150 µs (the impact frame); the color effects alone stay inside it. On Desktop-Low the impact frame is over by about 100 µs, and the blur alone, about 70 µs cheaper with its 12 taps, sits at the budget's edge. An M5 is not §3.3's GTX 1060-class reference, so the desktop tiers' taps are for M2's device measurements to settle; each four taps is about 70 µs at 1080p on the M5. Mobile-Low's budget is 2.5 ms on an A14-class phone: on the M5 the impact frame about triples the layered fused pass, and P2 already found that pass near or over budget on phones, so device measurements (X1, M2) decide the phones' taps (4 save 60–90 µs a frame on the M5 against 8) and which effects Mobile-Low keeps; the variants make dropping one per tier free.
    - **On Mobile-Low's settings the sandbox bench can't time post** on this machine: the post passes' timestamps came back on 0–17 of each run's 240 timed frames (the bench sums post only over frames with all of them, P2's rule), so the fused-pass harness above stands in. CPU p50 is 90–96 µs against 92–94 for the base (101 and 107 in two loaded runs), and allocations stay at 265.4 a frame. On Desktop-High they stay at 458.8 with or without effects; CPU p50 is the base's (133–137 µs) with an empty stack and 134–156 µs with effects in the quietest round, moving with the GPU's load (the effects' own CPU work is resolving the stack, 0.03 µs, and packing 160 bytes).
- **As built (post's lazy pipelines, after M1 P3; `sapphire_render::post::variants`):** post builds only the variants its frames can draw, as W5 and the WARP cost fixes did for theirs.
  - **Why.** Every golden scene is a process of its own, and WARP and lavapipe compile every pipeline on the CPU in each one: all 16 of post's, built with every renderer, cost about 1.3 s a desktop process on WARP (found by the light and ambient cost fixes), screen effects or not, and on the M5 (loaded) about 2.5 s with Metal's cache cold.
  - **Sets of switches.** `PostVariants` holds one switch per pipeline constant (`layers`, `sharpening`, `character_bloom`, `lens_effects`, `color_effects`); a set's variants are every combination of its switches, the lens effects in sharpening's place, and the bloom chain's pipelines come with a chain (none at 0 levels, the down and up passes from two). `RenderSettings::post_variants` is built with the renderer: by default `PostVariants::COMPOSITING`, P2's three switches, which time of day's keys and the player turn on at any hour (8 pipelines); `NONE` builds the plain fused pass and the chain (4), which the sandbox asks for under `--plain-post`, since none of its frames draws a layer.
  - **Decision: an explicit prewarm, with a counted fallback.** `Renderer::prewarm_post(PostVariants)` builds, synchronously, every combination of what it is given with what was set before. A game calls it at load with the variants of the effect presets it may push (`sapphire_world`'s `EffectPreset::post_variants` and `EffectLibrary::post_variants`: each layer at full weight on its own, so a layer that can draw counts even where a later layer of its kind would hide it), so a perfect dodge's desaturation shows on the frame it fires. A frame drawing a variant nothing built builds it as it is prepared (in `Renderer::prepare`, through `checked_build`, so after a shader edit it is checked like any pipeline built later): the same pipeline, so the same pixels, but a stall, counted in `PostStats::first_use_builds` and warned of in the log, so a missing prewarm shows. The stall is real: on the M5 (loaded) a layered or effect variant of the fused pass takes 170–210 ms to build with Metal's cache cold (the plain pipelines about 100), 0.5 ms warm, and the software backends have no cache. Rejected: building on first use alone (that stall, on the frame that fires the effect), and building on a job thread: wgpu's device builds pipelines from any thread, but the frame that fires the effect needs the pipeline either way, so a worker only moves the prewarm off the loading thread. That belongs with the warm-up above, shared by every feature's pipelines (M4's PSO warm-up).
  - **The sandbox** prewarms the presets it can fire whenever it makes a renderer (at start, on a tier change, after device loss) and when hot reload or live link replaces the presets (`Scene::effect_variants`): every preset's in `dev` builds, whose overlay fires any, otherwise those `--post-effect` pushed. A golden scene builds effect variants only if it pushes an effect, and then before its first frame.
  - **Hot reload and live link** rebuild only the pipelines built so far and keep the edited `post.wgsl` module, so the variants built later come from the edit too: a `post.wgsl` edit rebuilds 8 pipelines on a default renderer (16 before), and `tests/hot_reload.rs`'s counts, which `post.wgsl` isn't part of, are unchanged.
  - **Stats** (rule R9): `PostStats::pipelines` (built so far, of 16) and `first_use_builds`; the overlay's "Post layers" row shows both.
  - **Counts** (release sandbox, M5; post's pipelines, then the process's render pipelines by wgpu's report, against dd118d1): `turntable` (`--plain-post`) 4 and 16 against 16 and 28; `post-day` (the default day's layers) 8 and 21 against 16 and 29; `effects-impact-frame` 16 and 30, as before. Compute pipelines are unchanged.
  - **Startup** (release, M5, five alternating rounds against a dd118d1 release build in its own target dir, minima; Metal's cache cold for post's pipelines through a fresh entry-point argument per run, warm for the rest; the machine loaded by other agents' builds): the stage and renderer, all before the first frame (the bench's `startup_ms`), take 460 ms against 2,575 on `turntable`, 1,300 against 2,641 on `post-day` and 2,831 against 2,782 on `effects-impact-frame`; the golden capture's whole process 495 ms against 2,689, 1,437 against 2,804 and 2,828 against 3,315. With the cache warm (three rounds): 74 against 80 ms, 72 against 80 and 78 against 77.
  - **A frame** costs what it did: the same pipelines draw the same passes, and the frame's own work adds a branch and a check of the four pipelines it draws with. Sandbox `--bench 1000` at 1920×1080 (seven alternating rounds, minima, very noisy on the loaded machine): CPU p50 165–217 µs against 219 at 10:00 with the layers, 174 against 175 on plain post, 186 against 170 with the impact frame held; allocations a frame equal (475.7, 434.7, 475.7); post's GPU time within the rounds' spread.
  - **Tests:** headless, every set's pipelines (4 plain, 8 compositing, 16 in all, the lens effects alone with P2's adding 2) and a frame's; presets' variants in `sapphire_world`; the sandbox's (the scene's effect variants with and without `dev`, `--plain-post`'s settings). Metal (`post::gpu_tests`): a renderer builds its settings' variants and no effect's; a frame with an effect nothing prewarmed builds its one variant, counted once, and draws exactly what a prewarmed renderer draws; a prewarm builds the 8 effect variants once, wgpu's own count agreeing; a `post.wgsl` edit rebuilds the 8 built, a variant built after it comes from the edit (an inversion edited to paint red paints red), and going back rebuilds 9 and inverts as a renderer that never saw the edit.
  - **Golden images:** no scene is new or changed. All 75 Metal captures are pixel-identical to their goldens (`cargo xtask golden`, each capture also decoded and compared byte for byte): a variant is the same pipeline whenever it is built. lavapipe and WARP draw with the same pipelines, so CI's per-scene WARP times are where the change shows.

**Anti-aliasing and upscaling**

- **Desktop:** TAA with a responsive mask, or DLSS (NVIDIA + Vulkan via `dlss_wgpu`) or MetalFX temporal (via `objc2-metal-fx`).
- **Mobile:** MSAA 2× / 4× on the main pass if wgpu can keep MSAA attachments on-tile (**Verify**). Otherwise use FXAA in the fused post pass. Upscale with your own spatial upscaler, an FSR1-style EASU + RCAS port in WGSL, or MetalFX spatial on iOS.
- **As built (M1 R7, `sapphire_render::temporal`, `shaders/temporal.wgsl`):**
  - **Who gets what.** `RenderSettings::temporal` turns temporal AA on. The desktop tiers resolve with it and MSAA off; the phone tiers keep 4× MSAA without it. TAA's jittered history resolves edges inside a surface as well as silhouettes and outlines (painted shadow-bias steps, the unfiltered material-ID borders of control maps, thin highlights), where MSAA resolves only geometry. Its scene prepass already holds single-sampled depth and velocity, so 4× MSAA on top would quadruple the main pass's attachments for edges TAA already resolves. TAA with MSAA still works (MSAA resolves first), at the cost of the hull handling below.
  - **Jitter.** Every geometry pass rasterizes the same jittered samples: the view's matrices into clip space carry a Halton(2, 3) offset from its second element, and the sky follows through `world_from_clip`. The sequence is 8 phases at native resolution and 8 × the area ratio when upsampling (FSR 2's rule; 18 for 4K from 1440 rows), at most 64. It follows the renderer's frame count, so captures repeat exactly. The sky cube's six face views (W2) are never jittered. `ViewUniforms` appends `unjittered_clip_from_world` and the jitter. The scene prepass's velocity compares unjittered positions, so geometry that holds still has exactly zero velocity.
  - **Inputs.** The scene prepass (depth, velocity) and the character prepass now run under TAA; R2's graph culled both. A `responsive mask` pass writes `PrepassTargets::responsive` (`R8Unorm`, the scene's size): 1 where a character (a skinned or morphed instance) is the nearest surface, its prepass depth at or in front of the scene's. With the HDR scene color, the jitter, post's exposure and a reset flag, these are what DLSS, MetalFX temporal and FSR 2/3 take (`TemporalInputs`), so one of them can replace the resolve. The main pass's depth, below, is the resolve's own.
  - **The resolve** (`taa` pass) runs at the output's size on HDR scene color, before bloom and the tone curve, blending in a space compressed by exposed luma (`c / (1 + L)`), so a bright sample can't dominate a pixel. WuWa found that resolving after bloom and tone mapping amplified edge noise.
    - **A compute pass,** written for its cost. Each 8 × 8 workgroup gathers the scene pixels its outputs' 3 × 3 neighborhoods cover into workgroup memory once (2 × 2 `textureGather`s, color already compressed and in YCoCg), the 3 × 3 is unrolled, and textures are read through samplers. A first fragment-shader version cost 1.2 ms at 1080p on an M5: wgpu bounds every WGSL loop with a counter, which keeps Metal from unrolling the 3 × 3 loops, and each `textureLoad` carries a bounds check of three texture queries. The history is written as a storage texture; workgroup memory isn't zero-initialized, since every thread writes its share before any reads it.
    - **Reconstruction:** `exp(-4 d²)` over the 3 × 3 scene pixels around each output pixel, `d` in output pixels. The filter's sum is the frame's confidence, and the history's alpha accumulates it, so a frame counts by how close its samples landed. That makes the resolve an upsampler (TAAU), and the converged image this Gaussian. Wider filters (Unreal's `exp(-2.29 d²)`) visibly softened outlines; sharper ones (6, 9) converged less far in 48 frames.
    - **History:** fetched through a five-tap Catmull-Rom filter, clipped toward the mean of a YCoCg variance box (γ 1.5; at 1.0 one-pixel outlines lost visible darkness against 4× MSAA), and clamped back into RGB's gamut.
    - **Velocity:** a pixel follows its own surface unless one at least 10% nearer covers part of its 3 × 3, which then lends its velocity. Plain closest-depth dilation dragged edges ahead on curved, turning surfaces, whose fronts move fastest. Where no geometry is, the camera's motion at infinity reprojects the sky from depth (`previous_clip_from_clip`, computed in f64 and exactly the identity for a still camera).
    - **Outlines:** the inverted hull draws no velocity until C4, and at 1080 rows its default width of 5.5 px reaches far beyond any 3 × 3 dilation; ghost lines trailed every moving outline without handling. So the main pass keeps its depth under TAA, and where it is at least 10% nearer than the scene prepass's, the pixel is a hull pixel. It takes the velocity of the nearest surface matching its depth, searching three rings of eight taps out to 8 px at 1080 rows, and counts as responsive. So does any pixel with a hull pixel in its 3 × 3 in front of its own surface: the outline's antialiased edge, which a frame's jittered hull may miss, otherwise kept a still history of what the outline moved over (a moving outline's leading edge kept 75% of 4× MSAA's darkness, against 85% with the rule). On a body turning about its own axis the outline holds still while the surface slides, and no surface pixel carries that, so such outlines soften in motion until C4 writes the hull's velocity.
    - **Disocclusion:** what the main pass drew this frame (hulls included) against the farthest of last frame's drawn depths in the 3 × 3 around where the pixel came from, with a 10% margin. A single pixel's depth flips across an edge as the jitter moves its sample, and testing it alone rejected the history of still edges once per cycle (they flickered).
    - **Weights:** the history counts at most 32 frames' worth while still, 4 at 2 px a frame or when resampled by half a pixel. Resampling loss is `4f(1 − f)` for an offset of f; Catmull-Rom halves a one-pixel line's peak at f = 0.5, so thin lines in slow motion faded under long histories. Responsive pixels moving that fast keep half, with a γ 1.0 box. Characters that hold still accumulate like everything else, since their thin outlines need the full history.
  - **History lifetime.** The history is a pair of `Rgba16Float` textures at the output's size; the kept depth is a pair at the scene's size (the main pass's depth, or the scene prepass's under MSAA). Both are persistent and imported into each frame's graph. The history is dropped when a renderer starts (device loss or a tier change makes a new one), when the output's size changes, and on a camera cut: `ViewData::cut`, new, which also counts the camera as unmoved for velocity and carries over from a prepared frame that was never recorded.
    - **Deviation from the plan:** a render-scale change keeps the history. It is at the output's size, so it stays valid while dynamic resolution moves the scene's, as FSR 2 and DLSS keep theirs. Dropping it would flash aliased edges at every dynamic-resolution step: in a GPU test a step from scale 1 to 0.85 changes the converged frame by a mean of 0.01 steps (2 of 129,600 pixels by more than 32), a reset by 0.41 (625 pixels).
  - **As built (M1 P1):** outlines in the resolve were reworked ("Outlines", P1's note, has the decision and the measurements), replacing the **Outlines** bullet above. There is no hull search, and the main pass's depth is no longer kept: a hull pixel carries its line's velocity from the scene prepass, a pixel with a line in its 3 × 3 also tries the camera's motion alone and no motion and keeps the reprojection whose history matches best (`line_motion`), and lines are fully reactive. **Disocclusion** compares the scene prepass's depth (hulls included, bit for bit the main pass's), kept as the persistent pair at the scene's size with or without MSAA; the main pass's depth is a transient again, discarded on tile. **The responsive mask** holds reactivities, the share of the moving cap a pixel gives up: 0, characters' 0.5 (stored 128) and lines' 1 (255); a moving pixel's cap is scaled by 1 − reactivity × motion, and any reactive pixel in motion takes the γ 1.0 box. **The resolve's interface** (for later packages and the upscalers): it binds six textures and its storage target, seven bindings from eight (binding 4, this frame's drawn depth, is gone; history is 4, last frame's scene depth 5, the resolved frame 6); `TemporalUniforms::motion` (the search's reach) became `outlines` (lines' reactivity, the threshold between the two reactivities, whether the camera moved), and `response.y` is characters' reactivity. No channel was added: P3 reads the character mask from scene color's alpha before the resolve, within a pixel, and only M2's cut-ins might need it carried through.
  - **Upsampling** restores §3.1's caps (Desktop-High 1440 rows, Desktop-Low 1080). Jitter and resolve follow each frame's scene size, so dynamic resolution keeps working. Against native 4× MSAA, a converged TAAU frame at two-thirds scale differs by a mean of 0.74 steps (505 pixels over 32), against 1.69 (1,266) for the old bilinear upscale; at half scale 0.95 (522) against 2.57 (1,706).
  - **Sharpening measured, not added.** RCAS (FSR 1's, 0.2 stops, in compressed HDR) in the fused pass moved TAA further from 4× MSAA in every case on the sandbox: mean 0.51 → 0.64 steps and p99 8 → 13 when still, TAAU 0.68 → 0.80, in motion 1.01 → 1.15 (measured on the fragment version of the resolve, whose output the compute one matches within a FLIP mean of 0.0003). It also drew light halos along the dark outlines. P2 can offer it as a player setting.
  - **As built (M1 P2):** RCAS is a player setting in post's fused pass (`PostParams::sharpening`), off by default; P2 measured it against a supersampled reference on every path, native, TAAU and the phones' bilinear upscale, and it helps nowhere measurably ("Post-processing", P2's note).
  - **Mobile.** The 4× MSAA color and depth attachments were already `TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT`, cleared and discarded on tile (since M0). A GPU test now holds the mobile tiers to that: 4× samples, exactly `RENDER_ATTACHMENT | TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT`, and no TAA or prepass running. **Verify** in M2's device testing: the tile-memory saving and the cost on Mali and Adreno, and on Apple GPUs (`transient_saves_memory` is logged at startup). The iOS and Android targets build; the Simulator wasn't run (only W2 runs it in this batch).
  - **Stats (rule R9):** `RenderStats::temporal` (`TemporalStats`) has the scene and output sizes, the jitter's index and phase count (`TemporalStats::jitter`), and history resets with the last reason. The overlay's stats panel shows them, and a "TAA / upsampling" row adds the two passes' GPU time (`temporal::PASSES`, F5's timings) against §3.3's 0.8 ms. The bench logs `gpu_taa_us` and the prepasses' and post's GPU time.
  - **Sandbox:** `--aa off|msaa|taa` (`--msaa` still sets the count), `--spin <rad/s>` for the turntable, and `--capture-frames N [--capture-step S]` to draw N frames before a capture, S ticks apart (0: still, for convergence).
  - **Tests (Metal):**
    - The jitter moves an edge as the sequence says, in x and y, for two whole cycles, and the stats report it.
    - Still geometry has exactly zero velocity under jitter; a quad moving 3 px a frame has 3 px within 0.005.
    - A still scene converges: against a 64-sample reference (4× MSAA at 4× size), one sample's mean is 1.27, 4× MSAA's 0.33 and TAA's 0.43 (p99 12 against MSAA's 12). After convergence the frame-to-frame change is at most 0.06 steps, with at most 143 of 129,600 pixels moving more than 2 where a jitter phase misses a thin feature's end.
    - Under camera motion, panning and turning over a comb with a sky, geometry reprojects without ghosts (1,148 pixels over 48 against 4× MSAA, mostly edges; 6,050 with velocity ignored).
    - Disoccluded pixels and the pixels a moving outline leaves restart their accumulation (history weight ≤ 1), while untouched ones keep theirs.
    - The responsive mask marks visible characters only. Moving, a character keeps half the history weight other geometry does; still, the same.
    - A moving outlined sphere keeps 85% (leading edge) and 102% (trailing) of 4× MSAA's outline darkness over a jitter cycle, centered within 0.13 px of it; without the hull handling the leading edge keeps 50%. A single frame varies with the jitter phase (57–89% on the leading edge), so the test averages a cycle.
    - Resets happen on start, resize and cut, but not on a render-scale change; upsampling is described above. TAA runs at any size from 1 × 1, at any scale, with and without MSAA.
    - W2's night sky (stars and the moon's disc, which write no velocity and reproject from depth at infinity): held still, the frame's light holds within 0.4% from frame to frame and 4% of 4× MSAA's, with at most 13 pixels changing by more than 4 steps (no shimmer under the jitter). Turning by 1.3 px a frame, the light is within 1% of MSAA's, and the stars line up with MSAA's: the squared difference is 156, against 618–635 for MSAA's moved a pixel either way (46 still; the history's resampling softens them a little). Without the sky's reprojection the stars keep 35% of their light.
    - Hot reload (M1 A8): `temporal.wgsl` is a `ShaderFile` and its own program; an edit rebuilds the mask's and the resolve's pipelines (and changes what the resolve writes), a resolve that validates but doesn't build leaves the old ones drawing, and the compiled-in source draws the first frames again exactly.
    - Breaking disocclusion, the responsive mask, the hull search, the kept depth or the sky's reprojection each fails a test.
  - **Golden images:** the thirteen earlier scenes (C2's `turntable-night`, C3's `faces` and W2's three sky scenes included) pin `--aa msaa` (`--aa off` for `turntable-msaa-off`), and their captures are unchanged (FLIP mean, p99 and max 0.000). Three new scenes, Metal goldens only, deterministic run to run, with W2's `--plain-sky` as the other character scenes have, so they stay what they were before the sky's layers:
    - `turntable-taa`: still, after 32 converging frames. Outlines and light splits are crisp, close to 4× MSAA.
    - `turntable-taa-motion`: the turntable at 3 rad/s for 32 frames. No ghost trails or smears; outlines are thinner than 4× MSAA's at this resolution (1.8 px), with a faint second line beside the legs' outline. At 1080 rows the spinning head's outline is visibly fainter than 4× MSAA's: the body turns about its own axis (see "Outlines" above).
    - `turntable-taa-upsampled`: 427×240 resolved to 640×360, nearly indistinguishable from native.
  - **Cost** (sandbox `--bench 3000`, release, Apple M5):
    - **1920×1080, native,** four alternating rounds against 877fb16 (A8, with W2's sky) on a quiet machine: the TAA passes (mask and resolve) take 598–599 µs of GPU time, against §3.3's 0.8 ms. The prepasses TAA runs add 73–74 µs; the main pass, now without MSAA, takes 273 µs against 289 (the sky draws in it); post 74 µs and the sky cube 31–33 µs on both. The timed GPU frame is 1,193–1,196 µs against 471–488. CPU p50 is 119–121 µs against 102–104 (p99 201–235 against 141–173), with 407.7 allocations a frame against 309.6: the four passes TAA records (two prepasses, the mask, the resolve) cost wgpu's per-pass recording and the graph one more command encoder. With `--aa msaa` the branch is the base again: 309.6 allocations, CPU p50 102 µs, GPU frame 496 µs.
    - **3840×2160 from 2560×1440,** three alternating rounds against b349915 (C2) on a machine shared with other agents' builds and GPU work, so the ranges are wide: TAA takes 2,306–2,542 µs and post 347–384 µs at the output's size; the GPU frame is 3,410–3,969 µs against 844–1,715 for the base's native 4K with 4× MSAA. CPU p50 145–172 µs against 100–133.
    - The fragment-shader resolve took about 1.2 ms at 1080p and 4.7 ms at 4K; the compute pass halved both. Bisecting it at 4K: gathering the tiles about 0.5 ms, the 3 × 3 about 0.5 ms, the history's five taps 0.46 ms and disocclusion 0.18 ms. §3.3 budgets Desktop-Low at 1080p only; at 4K output the resolve is well over 0.8 ms, and MetalFX temporal or DLSS replace it where they run.

**Materials, shaders and PSOs**

- **Source:** WGSL with WESL imports (`wesl` 0.6), validated and reflected with naga. Parameter structs are laid out with `encase` (through glam's `encase` feature) / `bytemuck`. They are written by hand and checked against naga's reflection in tests. `wgsl_bindgen` is out: it is built on the archived `naga_oil` and can't read WESL imports.
- **Permutation inventory:** the cooker writes, per pack, the list of `(family, permutation bits, vertex layout, render state)` it needs.
- **Warm-up:**
  - At boot, compile the global pipelines behind the splash screen.
  - At pack load, compile the listed pipelines on workers. An object stays hidden until its pipelines are ready.
  - On Vulkan, persist the pipeline cache.
  - On Metal, warm up behind load screens (**Verify** whether wgpu exposes Metal binary archives).
- **Hot reload:**
  - Desktop: file watch triggers an async recompile and a pipeline swap.
  - Devices: live link pushes the WGSL text. Shaders are translated at runtime anyway, so this works on phones too.
- **As built (M1 A8, `sapphire_render::shaders`, `Renderer::{set_shader_sources, update_mesh}`):**
  - **Sources:** `ShaderFile` names the renderer's WGSL files (eleven since R7's `temporal.wgsl`) and holds each one's compiled-in text; `ShaderSources` is the text of every file, compiled in by default, any of it replaceable (`set`); a program (one shader module) is its files joined by newlines, `frame.wgsl` and `materials.wgsl` first, exactly the concatenation before (every golden is pixel-identical). The concatenated constants became one table (`Program::files`), so a shader file is named in one place. The renderer only ever gets text: development builds read edited files and hand them over, live link (A9) will push them, and shipping builds use the compiled-in files alone (rule R5).
  - **Rebuilding:** `Renderer::set_shader_sources` finds the programs whose files changed, checks each with naga as wgpu compiles it, with no optional capabilities (as the headless shader tests always have, so an edit can't use what phones lack), and maps naga's location in the program back to the file and line it is in (`sky.wgsl:32:12: expected identifier`; `ShaderError::Invalid` also carries naga's full report). It then builds the new modules and every pipeline built so far from their programs' old versions, each on the pipeline layout it was built with so every bind group stays valid (the mesh programs per position format, surface variant (coverage and part, C3) and outline coverage, the character prepass, the sky's six (W2: four layer variants into the scene and two into the sky cube, which then draws all six faces again), post's four, the light-cluster and skinning passes), inside wgpu validation and internal error scopes, so a program naga accepts but wgpu refuses (a renamed entry point, a binding the layouts don't have) is `ShaderError::Pipeline` rather than an uncaptured, in debug builds fatal, error. Only if everything succeeded do the new pipelines replace the old ones, from the next recorded frame; otherwise the renderer draws on with what it had. Unchanged programs keep their modules and pipelines; pipelines built later (a new permutation, f32 positions, the first skinned mesh) use the current sources, inside the same error scopes while they are edited ones: if wgpu refuses one of those (an edit that renamed a permutation constant no pipeline built so far set), the renderer goes back to the compiled-in shaders, rebuilding every pipeline from them, and logs why, rather than draw with an invalid pipeline. `RenderStats::shader_rebuilds` counts applied edits (rule R9).
  - **Synchronous, on purpose for now:** the frame that applies an edit compiles on its own thread. A code change to the anime character shader rebuilt its two pipelines in 118–148 ms (sandbox with `--swatches --skinned --lights`, release, M5: naga's check, wgpu's own, and Metal compiling the uber-shader cold), and one to `sky_color` in `frame.wgsl` the five programs that include it and nine pipelines in 179–183 ms (both measured before W2's sky, whose six pipelines a `frame.wgsl` edit now rebuilds too); an edit whose generated code Metal has compiled before (a comment, a file restored) takes 3–6 ms. So the frame that applies an edit hitches by up to about 0.2 s, once, in development builds only. Compiling on workers and swapping when ready belongs with the warm-up above (crate docs, later milestones).
  - **New shader files** (R7's TAA) join `ShaderFile` (name and `include_str!`) and their program's `Program::files`; a new program also needs its pipelines' owner to build from `ShaderSources` and rebuild in `set_shader_sources`, as the mesh pipelines, the sky (`sky::Sky`, W2, which kept `sky.wgsl`), post, the light clusters and skinning do.
  - **Meshes replaced in place:** `Renderer::update_mesh(handle, mesh, assets)` replaces a mesh under its handle, as `upload_slot_materials` and `upload_mesh` would upload it: instances draw the new mesh from the next frame (skinned ones from their bind pose until posed again: their outputs are made anew, since the vertex count may differ), per-object light lists are chosen again for its new bounds, and the materials it embeds rewrite the slots the old mesh's embedded materials had (slot by slot where the family matches) instead of taking new ones. The mesh pool's buffers now hand out ranges first fit (the skinning outputs' `RangeAllocator`) instead of only appending, and a replaced mesh's ranges go back: replacing a mesh again and again keeps the pool's size and grows no buffer. Ranges are freed right after the new ones are written, which queue order makes safe for frames already submitted. Materials were already updated in place (A3); a texture replaced on the app's side is bound again by updating the materials that use it, which the asset server sends again for that (§5.10).
  - **Tests (Metal):** editing the sky rebuilds its six pipelines and only the sky changes; editing the anime character shader to swap red and blue rebuilds its one built pipeline and swaps the sphere's channels; appending a comment to `frame.wgsl` rebuilds the five programs that include it and their ten pipelines and draws the same pixels; with opaque, alpha-tested and transparent bodies and a face drawn, comments in the anime character shader, the outline shader and `frame.wgsl` rebuild four, two and fourteen pipelines and draw the same pixels; going back to the compiled-in sources draws the first frame again. A syntax error is reported at its file and line, a type error at its line in `sky.wgsl`, a renamed entry point as a pipeline error, and after all three the renderer still draws the same pixels and then takes a fixed edit. A new texture through `update_material` recolors a sphere uploading one slot; a mesh replaced by one of half the radius draws at its new size under the same handle; replacing it four more times leaves the pool's bytes and buffer growths unchanged; a mesh's embedded material replaced keeps the material count; an unresolvable slot and an unknown handle fail without changing the frame; an edit good for the pipelines built so far but not for an alpha-tested surface uploaded afterwards sends the renderer back to the compiled-in shaders, and the surface draws. Headless: the program table composes the compiled-in files into the same text as before, and naga's line numbers map back to files.
- **As built (M1 A3, `sapphire_render::materials`, `shaders/materials.wgsl`):**
  - **Material buffers:** one parameter buffer per family, an array of its block, bound as group 2 binding 1 by that family's pipelines. R2's character and scene prepasses share group 2 but leave group 3 out, so only the main pass binds each draw's material, and only when it changes. One shared array would pad every slot to the 896-byte anime character block, and a pipeline only reads its own family, so the per-stage storage-buffer count is unchanged. `Renderer::upload_material` gives a material a slot for the renderer's lifetime (a `MaterialHandle`); draw records name it by that slot. Slots are written through a CPU mirror and the frame's uploads pass sends the changed ones through the staging belt, in runs. `Renderer::update_material` rewrites one slot and rebinds its textures: nothing else is uploaded and the draw list isn't rebuilt, which is the hot reload (A8) and live link (A9) path for a material edit. A GPU test scribbles over every slot behind the renderer's back, updates one, and reads the buffer back: only that slot was rewritten. `RenderStats` counts materials, their bytes and the slots each frame uploads.
  - **Group 3** per family: texture slot `i` at binding `i` (filterable 2D, fragment-visible), a trilinear repeating sampler at 8 and a clamped linear one for ramps at 9; unbound slots get a white 1×1 texture. The anime character family takes 7 of the 16 sampled textures and 2 of the 16 samplers a fragment stage has on Vulkan 1.1 phones, and group 3 is the last of their 4 groups. The renderer checks each bound texture's swizzle against the one the material was cooked with. Every family uploads and binds its textures now; their shaders come with W2 (sky), W3 (fog cards), W4 (environment) and W6 (foliage), and until then `upload_mesh` accepts only anime character materials.
  - **The anime character family drives the anime character shader (`anime_character.wgsl`) and its outlines:** base color × base color map × vertex tint, region 0's shade, rim tint and outline color, the split's threshold and softness, the rim's width and strength and the outline's width (times region 0's scale) all come from the block (the rim width was a shader constant). The shaders read the block field by field from the buffer rather than copying all 896 bytes, which a per-pixel region index would otherwise force into scratch memory on mobile GPUs. Textures are sampled with explicit gradients taken once per pixel, so unbound slots are skipped on a branch, and through the slot's swizzle code. Ramp and three-band diffuse, per-region reads of the control map, faces, hair and eyes are C1–C7's, over data already in the block; alpha-tested and transparent materials draw opaque until C1's permutations.
