An envelope is a `Pulse` (attack, hold, release; smoothstep rises and falls), a `Sustain` held until released, or up to eight `Keys`, linear between them, where two keys at one time cut, for impact frames that flash on exact frames. A layer names exactly one effect; unknown fields are errors (a misspelled `hlod` fails rather than vanishing), and everything is checked however it is made: times within 60 s and in order, weights and parameters in range, a preset's name unique.
  - **The sample presets** (`default_screen_effects.ron`, compiled in until packs carry it; development builds hot reload it, A8, and live link pushes it, A9): one per effect (`radial_blur`, `chromatic_aberration`, `inversion`, `duotone`, `speed_lines`) and `impact_frame`, all of them at once: the world in a near-black and cream duotone for six frames of real time, black speed lines behind the character redrawn at 20 Hz, a radial blur and chromatic aberration fading over 0.4 s.
  - **The stack** (`EffectStack`, an ECS resource) holds the layers playing, in push order. `push` (or `push_at`, centered on a hit's point on screen) returns a handle to `release` a sustained layer, `stop` the push or `hold` it at a time (for captures and benches); `tick_screen_effects` advances it in `FixedFirst` after the clocks and drops the layers that ended. Once a frame `resolve(alpha)` evaluates each layer's envelope at its interpolated time and blends the layers bottom to top, kind by kind, into the packet's `PostParams::effects`: a layer mixes the strength from what the layers below resolved to by its weight, and the center and colors by its share of the result, so a new hit shows over what was playing as it fades in, and an effect fading in over nothing keeps its own center and colors. Speed lines change their seed at the layer's rate, which redraws them. The renderer sees those values only (rule R4); an empty stack resolves to none.
  - **Fixed capacity, 16 layers** (four impact frames): a heavy moment is a few overlapping hits' presets on top of an ultimate's sustained layers, and layers of a kind blend toward the top one anyway. A full stack drops the layer with the least time left, or the new one if it would end first, and counts it. Layers are `Copy` (an envelope's keys are a fixed array), so pushing, ticking and resolving allocate nothing (a test, with the stack overflowing every other frame for a minute).
  - **Clocks** (§4.5): the stack's `LocalClock` is issued by the world clock. A `World` layer (the default) plays on the world's time, so pause holds it and slow motion stretches it; a `Real` layer counts fixed steps at full speed while the world runs, as hit-stop counts down, so an impact frame authored as three frames lasts three frames in a perfect dodge's 0.2× too. **Hit-stop (M2)** freezes the combatants' own clocks, never the world's, so effects pushed with a hit play on through it, which is the point; M2 pushes the hit's preset in the step it applies hit-stop, with its impact frames as `Real` keys as long as the hit-stop. A finish freeze that stops the whole world holds both clocks: M2 decides with the freeze whether effects then need a clock of their own.
  - **Stats** (`EffectStackStats`, rule R9): depth (layers playing), active (with weight this frame), pushed and dropped. The overlay's stats panel shows them beside the effects post drew, and its effects panel lists the layers playing (§5.20).
  - **Sandbox:** `--post-effect <preset>[@<seconds>]`, repeatable, pushes a preset at the start, held that far into it with `@` (an unknown name fails with the presets' names). Effects resolve after `--plain-post`, so they draw over plain post too.
  - **Post variants** (post's lazy pipelines, after P3; §5.12): `EffectPreset::post_variants` and `EffectLibrary::post_variants` name the post pipeline variants presets draw through (`sapphire_render::PostVariants`), each layer at full weight on its own, alone or with the others playing, for the renderer's prewarm at load (`Renderer::prewarm_post`), so a preset's first push shows on the frame it fires. The sandbox prewarms its library's in `dev` builds, whose overlay fires any preset (again when hot reload or live link replaces it), and otherwise the presets `--post-effect` pushes.
  - **Tests:** the sample presets load, validate and round-trip; validation names the field (an envelope typo, keys out of order, a layer with two effects or none); pulses, sustains and keys, cuts included; world and real clocks under slow motion (1.2 s against 2.0 s over two seconds at 0.2×), pause and hit-stop, interpolation and held layers; delays and ends; blending order, centers and colors, masks, `push_at`; the speed lines' redraw rate; a full stack's drops, releases and stops; no allocation.
  - **Cost:** ticking 0.02 µs a frame and resolving 0.01 µs with nothing playing, 0.03 µs with an impact frame's four layers and 0.11 µs with all 16, on an M5 (release).
- **As built (M1 W3, fog keys):**
  - `FogKey` gains `characters` (0 to 1, unset 0: keys written before it leave characters clear of the fog), validated; the far layer's color is a tint on the sky's horizon.
  - **Blending:** fog layer colors blend in OkLab weighted by the layers' densities, as premultiplied colors blend, so a layer only one key has keeps its color as it thins away rather than darkening toward the other key's unused color; densities, starts, falloff, base and the share blend linearly, the inscatter in OkLab.
  - **The default day** sets fog at every key for the renderer that now draws it: far tints white or nearly; morning mist thickest at sunrise (height fog 0.025 per meter, falloff 0.4), a light haze by day, peach and violet at sunset, lavender in the twilights and deep blue at night with the moon's faint inscatter; characters take 40–50%. Its 10:00 key is the renderer's default look but for that haze, which the default leaves out, so scenes from before fog pin it off.
  - **Tests:** the default day fogs every hour, with a share and a far tint near white; density-weighted OkLab blending, exact on the keys; zones and cutscenes override the fog (inside, outside, through the packet, eased in and out).
- **As built (M1 W5, `TimeOfDay::probe_keys`):** the bounce keys of an ambient probe grid (§5.12, "Lighting") for the frame, from the hours the grid was baked at. For the clock's hour, and a playing cutscene's own hour by the track's weight, it takes the two baked keys the hour lies between: by the base timeline's own eased weight when the grid was baked at that timeline's keys (the cooker bakes every key of the default day), so the bounce moves with the rest of the look, and linearly by the hour otherwise, wrapping at midnight. Zone volumes don't change it: the bake lit the base timeline. It allocates nothing. Tests: keys at and between baked hours, the eased weight, midnight, a cutscene's crossfade, and no grid.
- **As built (M1, keyed emission: a W1 and W4 follow-up for X1's look):** time of day decides how much of the world's authored emission shows.
  - **Why:** the environment's emission (W4) was added at one level all day. X1 part 2's Wuthering Waves target wants paper lanterns and lit windows pale cream by day and glowing amber at night, and exposure can't separate the two: it lifts a lantern with the light around it, so one bright enough to bloom at night glows visibly at noon.
  - **Documents:** keys gain `emission`, an optional group (`TodGroup::Emission`): a scale on the world's authored emission, finite and not negative, 1 as authored and above 1 a boost. Like the sky's and post's layers the base timeline may leave it out (`TodGroup::OPTIONAL`, which `TodGroups::REQUIRED` now leaves out); a timeline that does keeps 1 at every hour, so every document written before it loads, validates and draws as it did, with no schema version. `default_day.ron` and `lookdev_day.ron` don't set it. Every key that sets it still needs the same groups as its neighbors.
  - **Evaluation:** it blends like every scalar, by the first key's ease, exactly a key's value on that key, wrapping at midnight. Zones and cutscenes override it like any group: a festival street whose lanterns burn all day is a zone that sets it alone. `TodLook::emission` writes `FramePacket::emission_scale` (§4.6), and the overlay's time-of-day panel shows it. Hot reload and live link carry it in the day's document like every other group.
  - **Who follows it:** the environment family's emission (§5.12, "Environment"), but for the share a material keeps steady (`EnvironmentMaterial::emission_steady`), and the probe bake's (§5.27, W5): each key's emission is lit by that key's scale, steady share included, so a lantern dark by day lights nothing in the day's keys. **Decision:** characters' emission (C7's layers) never follows it, since a character's glow is part of their design at any hour; §5.12 has the rest.
  - **Adopting it:** a day that keys emission sets it at every key, authoring lanterns' emission for the night and keying it down by day. In a trial capture of the look-dev town (not committed), 0.08 from 9:00 to 16:00, rising through 0.3 at 18:00 and 0.8 at 19:00 to 1 from 19:45 to dawn, left the lanterns' glass pale cream and the windows' interiors dark at 13:00, and at 19:30 drew within 8 code values of the town as authored. The look-dev stage's lamps (local lights) follow `day_night`; a day that keys emission can light them by its scale instead, so glass and light come on together.
  - **Tests:** a key that sets only the scale names its group, which the base timeline may leave out; out-of-range values name the field; timelines without it (the default day, one written before the sky's layers) give 1 at every quarter hour, through the packet too; keyed, it's exact on keys, eased between them and wraps at midnight, and the packet carries it with every other value the default day's; it round-trips as a document, and a key without it next to one with it is an error; zones (inside, blending out, outside) and a cutscene (in, easing, gone) override it; a day pushed over live link with it in every key replaces the old one, and the sandbox's packet follows an edited day.
  - **Cost:** one more scalar blended: the resolve took 1.3–3.3 µs a frame against the base's 1.8–3.1 µs in the same benches (§5.12, "Environment"), on the loaded machine, against the 0.05 ms budget, and ticking and resolving still allocate nothing.

### 5.22 `sapphire_gameplay` (L4)

**Character controller**

- A kinematic capsule with grounded, air, slope, step-up and ledge handling.
- Root-motion integration.
- A combat movement mode that disables step-up jitter during attacks.

**Combat core** (all inside the fixed step, in this order)

1. **Intents:** a ring buffer 8–15 ticks deep, with expiry and priority per intent.
2. **Cancels:** the move graph checks cancel windows. Buffered inputs fire when a window opens; instant inputs only fire if pressed inside it.
3. **Advance moves:** each move advances on its local clock. Its frame-data timeline has tracks for startup, active and recovery, hitboxes, hurtboxes and invulnerability, perfect-dodge and parry windows, cancel windows, root motion and warp, and cues.
4. **Movement:** apply root motion and the controller.
5. **Hits:** run `HitQuery` and queue `HitEvent`s.
6. **Resolve:** apply attributes, effects and tags. This step handles damage, poise and stagger (in the spirit of ZZZ's Daze meter) and hit reactions (stagger, launch, knockdown).
7. **Hitstop and time scale.**
8. **Cues:** VFX, SFX, camera shake, rumble and post effects. Cues are presentation-only, so the simulation runs headless.

**Ability system (GAS-like)**

- **Attributes:** HP, ATK, energy, and so on.
- **Effects:** instant, duration or periodic, with stacking rules.
- **Tags:** hierarchical tags compiled to bitsets.
- **Abilities:** activation conditions, costs, and cooldowns on the owner clock.

**Party**

- Three slots. Swapping is an ability, and swap-in assists and chain attacks are moves.
- Off-field characters keep ticking for cooldowns and energy.
- The party's resources are pinned in `sapphire_assets`.

**Camera director**

- A rig stack:
  - Explore follow.
  - Combat framing (player plus target).
  - Lock-on.
  - Sequencer takeovers for ultimate-skill cinematics.
- Collision by sphere cast, smoothing by critically damped springs.
- The camera runs on its own clock. Shakes are cues, scaled by the player's shake setting.

**Targeting:** soft lock weighted by input direction, plus auto-target on touch.

**Enemy AI**

- An enemy's "brain" outputs intents into the *same* move system the player uses, so bosses are authored in the same timeline editor.
- `statig` hierarchical state machines handle behavior states; utility scores pick attacks.
- An encounter director hands out attack tokens and manages waves. Boss phases are data plus Luau hooks.

**Tests**

- The headless `sapphire_testkit` world replays recorded input files and asserts outcomes: damage dealt, final positions, a checksum of the simulation state.
- These replays run in CI on all three desktop OSes and are the combat regression suite.

### 5.23 `sapphire_sequencer` (L4)

- **Timeline asset tracks:**
  - Camera.
  - Character animation and transforms.
  - VFX and audio events.
  - Post-process parameters.
  - Subtitles and dialogue.
  - Time scale.
  - Gameplay events.
- **Used for** cutscenes and in-combat ultimate cinematics. Each timeline chooses its clock, blends in and out with the gameplay camera, and can be skipped.
- **Dialogue graphs:**
  - Each line has a localization key, a VO event and a speaker. Graphs support choices and conditions (Luau expressions) and set quest flags.
  - They feed ZZZ-style dialogue panels in `game_ui`.
- **Editor:** scrubbable, with a live preview (§5.28).

### 5.24 `sapphire_script` (L4)

- **VM:** Luau via `mlua` 0.12 with the `luau` feature and **no JIT** on any platform (iOS forbids it, and determinism benefits). One sandboxed VM per world, with read-only globals.
- **Calls are event hooks only:** encounter start, boss phase change, move start and hit, dialogue conditions and quest steps. Scripts never run every tick.
- **Bindings** are generated from the `sapphire_reflect` registry, plus a `.d.luau` type-definition file for editor autocompletion.
- **GC:** incremental, stepped once per frame with a time budget (0.2 ms desktop, 0.4 ms mobile) at a fixed point outside the simulation hot loop.
- **Determinism:** the `os`, `time` and `random` globals are removed. The engine provides seeded RNG and clock functions.
- **Hot reload:** reload the chunk and rebind its hooks. On devices, pushed through live link.
- **iOS policy:** scripts ship inside the app. Any downloadable script update needs legal and App Review sign-off first (App Store guideline 2.5.2).

### 5.25 `sapphire_save` and `sapphire_loc` (L4)

**`sapphire_save`**

- **Settings:** RON in `user://`.
- **Progress:** `postcard` with a versioned header and migrations.
  - Writes are atomic: write a temp file, fsync, then rename.
  - Two rotating slots, each with a checksum.
- **Cloud saves:** *later*, through your own backend, Steam Cloud, iCloud or Play Games.

**`sapphire_loc`**

- Fluent `.ftl` bundles per locale, cooked into binary tables.
- ICU4X (`icu` 2.4) for plurals, numbers and dates.
- **Font fallback chains** per script: Latin, CJK, Cyrillic, Thai, and so on.
- A **pseudo-localization mode** with expanded, accented strings for layout testing.

### 5.26 `sapphire_patch` / `sapphire_online` (L4, later)

**Patching**

- Content-addressed chunk manifests, signed with `ed25519-dalek`.
- **Download:** CDN downloads with resume and blake3 verification.
- **Swap:** atomically switch the manifest when a patch completes. Only changed chunks are downloaded.
- **HTTP:** `reqwest` + `rustls` + `rustls-platform-verifier`, so certificates are checked with the OS trust store on iOS and Android.
- **iOS background downloads** need a native `URLSession` shim.

**Online services**

- Accounts, gacha and inventory are **server-authoritative**. The client only displays results.
- Telemetry is opt-in.

### 5.27 `sapphire_pipeline` (Tools)

**How**

- **Pipeline:** a library plus a `cook` CLI plus an optional daemon used by the editor. It runs as a separate process, so a crash in a third-party encoder can't take down the editor.
- **Stages:** importer (source → engine-neutral intermediate) → cooker (intermediate → platform blob) → packer.
- **Cache key:** blake3 of the source bytes, the importer and cooker versions, the settings, the target cook (`desktop` / `mobile`) and the dependency keys. The local cache comes first; a shared cache can be added *later*.
- **Identity:** every source asset has a `.meta` sidecar holding its GUID and import settings, and references always go by GUID.
- **Parallelism:** `rayon` is fine here.
- **As built (M1 A1, `sapphire_pipeline::{meta, cache, cook}`):**
  - A sidecar is `<source file name>.meta` (`content/samples/mannequin.glb.meta`): a `sapphire::assets::AssetMeta` RON document holding the `id` and the `importer`, an enum whose variant names the importer and holds its typed settings (`Gltf((outline: (weld_tolerance: 0.0001)))`). Left-out settings take their defaults.
  - A cook creates missing sidecars and never rewrites an existing one. `--locked` (xtask's, so CI) fails instead, because an ID made there would be thrown away. Two sidecars with one ID are an error, and so is a source without a sidecar next to a sidecar without a source: a move that left the sidecar behind would otherwise lose the ID.
  - The cache key is blake3 (derive-key mode) over postcard-encoded inputs: the source's hash; the recipe (importer and cooker names and versions, and the cooked format's version); the import settings; the cook; and the dependencies as a set, files the importer reads (glTF external buffers) by relative path and hash and assets by ID and key. A new kind of asset adds an importer variant and a recipe without changing the key.
  - The key leaves out the source's path, its ID and the pipeline binary, so moves, copies and pipeline rebuilds are hits. The price: a stage whose output changes for the same input must bump its version. A cooked format's version is in the key, so a format bump recooks on its own.
  - The local cache is `target/content/cache/`, one file per key with a header (magic, format, length, blake3 of the output), so damaged entries are misses. Outputs are written only when their bytes change, so a file watcher sees only real changes, and stale cooked files are pruned. This replaces xtask's tree stamp, which recooked everything on any change.
  - Timings on the M5: `cargo xtask content` takes 0.26–0.27 s cold, warm, or after touching or changing the one sample mesh. Nearly all of that is cargo checking that xtask and the pipeline are built; the pipeline itself takes 11 ms cold and 8 ms warm. On 200 distinct meshes (27 MB of sources) it takes 0.46 s cold, 0.06 s warm or with one source changed (1 cooked, 199 up to date), and 0.10 s to restore every output from the cache.
  - Cooked outputs keep mirroring their source paths; addressing them by ID waits for packs (A4, see §5.10).
  - Later: a shared cache behind the same key, pruning old entries (deleting the directory is always safe), a stat cache so unchanged sources aren't rehashed, and parallel cooking.
- **As built (M1 A2, `sapphire_pipeline::{import::image, texture}`):**
  - PNG, TGA and EXR import through `image` 0.25, built with only those decoders, into linear `f32` RGBA that remembers the source's channel layout. Integer samples are decoded as the texture class says (sRGB for color, ramp and HDR classes, linear for data maps), float samples are always linear, and non-finite samples are errors.
  - The class is a typed import setting in the sidecar (`importer: Texture((class: Normal((flip_y: true))))`). New PNG and TGA sidecars default to color, EXR to HDR. The formats per class and cook are the table below: masks pick BC4, BC5 or BC7 by channel count, SDF maps are 16-bit on desktop by default and 8-bit on mobile, and mobile HDR is RGB9E5 by default (RGBA16F keeps alpha). ASTC blocks are 4×4 by default, configurable per color, normal and mask texture, and fixed at 4×4 for ILM maps.
  - The pipeline generates mip chains itself. Each level is the area average of level 0 over its footprint, a box filter of the exact scale factor, so odd sizes stay aligned and nothing rings or haloes. Averaging is in linear light: color weights by alpha, normals are averaged as vectors with Z rebuilt and renormalized, and everything else is averaged per channel.
  - Each level then goes through its own `ctt::convert` call with a named encoder (bc7enc-rdo for BC7, Intel's prebuilt ISPC kernels for BC4, BC5 and BC6H, astcenc for ASTC), so ctt's broken BC7 mip chains (its issue #114) never come into play. ctt is told the levels are premultiplied on both sides: its float path otherwise premultiplies straight alpha and divides it back out, which destroys data packed into alpha.
  - Textures plug in as a recipe (importer `image` 1, cooker `ktx2 (ktx2 0.5.0, ctt 0.6.0)` 1, format `TEXTURE_VERSION`) without changing the cache key. Two cooks of one source are byte-identical (serial encoders, no timestamps). A test ties the cooker's name to the versions of every `ctt*` crate and of `ktx2` in `Cargo.lock`, so upgrading an encoder (each is its own crate on a caret range) or the descriptor generator the reader checks against recooks every texture.
  - Timings on the M5 (release, encoders serial): a 2048² color texture cooks in 1.2 s to BC7 and 0.5 s to ASTC 4×4, and a 1024² normal map to BC5 in about 30 ms.
  - A GPU test cooks every class for both cooks, uploads the KTX2 and samples every level back on Metal, down to the 1×1 levels, within each format's tolerance.
- **As built (M1 A5, `sapphire_pipeline::{import::gltf, bake, optimize, mesh}`):**
  - The glTF importer reads **skins**: every joint of every skin in the scene plus their ancestors (so a non-joint armature node still transforms its joints), depth-first, and a palette entry per distinct joint and inverse bind matrix. Skinned primitives stay in bind space; static parts of a skinned scene (props, bone-parented weapons) bind rigidly to their nearest ancestor joint, or joint 0. It reads every `JOINTS_n`/`WEIGHTS_n` set, merges repeats of a joint and keeps the four heaviest, renormalized; further influences are dropped and their weight goes to the four in proportion. A vertex with no positive weight binds wholly to its skin's first joint.
  - **Morph targets** are named by `extras.targetNames` (or `<mesh>/<index>`), merge by name across nodes and meshes (a face, lashes and brows exported apart share one `blink`), and keep only vertices that move by over 1 µm or turn by over 1e-4. Normal deltas are the difference of transformed, normalized normals, exact at full weight.
  - An authored **`_OUTLINE_NORMAL`** attribute (VEC3 float, or normalized byte or short, in `NORMAL`'s space) wins over the welded bake vertex by vertex.
  - **`meshopt` 0.7** (meshoptimizer 1.3): submeshes merge by material; each LOD level simplifies the last kept one, per submesh, with normals weighted and material-seam vertices locked so submeshes stay sealed; a level that saves under 10%, or keeps no triangles, is dropped, and a part that simplifies away still counts toward its level's error; each LOD is reordered for the vertex cache, then overdraw (threshold 1.05); vertices are reordered coarsest LOD first, so every LOD reads a prefix. The crate's vertex-fetch remap wrapper truncates its table when vertices go unused (as when the mobile cook drops LOD0), so that remap is done in Rust.
  - Sidecar settings: `lods: (levels: [(triangle_ratio, max_error), …], mobile_skip)`, by default ½, ¼ and ⅛ of the triangles within 0.2%, 1% and 3% of the extent, with the mobile cook dropping the first level; `levels: []` turns LODs off. `position_format: Unorm16 | Float32`. All are part of the cache key; importer, cooker and format are at version 2.
  - The mannequin's chain: 6,188 / 3,130 / 1,603 / 837 triangles, with error estimates of 0 / 2.8 / 8.1 / 20.9 mm; 3,675 vertices desktop, 2,080 mobile. A cold cook of the sample content still takes about 10 ms. Cooks are deterministic: the same source writes the same bytes.
- **As built (M1 A7, `sapphire_pipeline::character_maps`):**
  - The anime character maps are generated from RON documents, each a source with a sidecar like any other, known by a compound extension: `<name>.facesdf.ron` (threshold masks per light angle), `<name>.ramp.ron` (color stops per material ID) and `<name>.ilm.ron` (a mask per ILM channel). The sidecar's importer is `CharacterMap(…)` with the cook settings (face SDF: mips and desktop precision; ramp: width 256, row height 4, no mips; ILM: mips). What the texels mean is `sapphire_assets::character_maps` (§5.12).
  - They plug in without changing the cache key: each kind's importer stage reads the document and its input images and generates the map as an image, and A2's texture cooker cooks that image as its class (SDF R16/RG16 on desktop and R8/RG8 on mobile, ramps RGBA8 sRGB, ILM BC7 or ASTC 4×4). The input images are file dependencies, by the path the document names them with, so editing a mask recooks exactly the maps that read it and moving a document with its inputs is a cache hit. Stages that decode masks carry the image importer's version in their low 16 bits.
  - Input images are part of their document's asset, as a glTF's buffers are: the cook leaves every image a document names out of its sources, so masks get no sidecar and no texture of their own. An input inside the content tree must name a scanned file exactly, case included, so every file system claims the same files, and must not have a sidecar of its own, which would make it an asset that claiming would silently drop; both are errors. This is the one change to the cook loop beyond registering the recipes.
  - **Face SDF generator:** an exact Euclidean distance transform (Felzenszwalb and Huttenlocher, linear time, separable) of each mask; distances to an edge are measured from texel centers less half a texel. A texel lit in mask *i* and shadowed in mask *i + 1* turns at `aᵢ + (aᵢ₊₁ − aᵢ) · d₀ / (d₀ + d₁)`, with *d₀* its distance to mask *i*'s edge and *d₁* to mask *i + 1*'s lit region; texels shadowed in the first mask hold 0 and texels lit in every mask hold 1. Masks may sit at any increasing angles, two or more per channel. Thresholded at a mask's angle, the map reproduces it exactly (a moving edge, nested circles and a cooked R16 tree in the tests), except that nothing reads as lit with the light at exactly 180°; halfway between masks, the edge lies halfway, within a texel. A band without an edge on one side (after a mask with no shadow, or before one with nothing lit) turns at once, at the later mask's angle. Each consecutive pair is checked for texels lit again; the cook fails naming both masks, the count and the bounding box, unless the document allows that many per pair (`allowed_violations`), and allowed ones follow the first mask that shadowed them. Masks are binarized at 50%, so their resolution sets the edge's precision. Generating a two-channel 1024² map from 26 masks takes 1.6–2.7 s on the M5 (release, with other builds running), mostly its 48 distance transforms.
  - **Ramps:** stops at positions 0 to 1 in sRGB hex, blended in OkLab by default, or `Linear`, `Srgb` or `Step`, chosen per document, ramp or stop. Each material has a day ramp and an optional night ramp (the day ramp when left out); IDs past the listed materials repeat the last, so every row the shader can read holds a ramp. Texels sample the ramp at their centers.
  - **ILM packer:** each channel is one channel of an image mapped onto a `range` (so an AO bake becomes a shadow bias with `range: (0.0, 0.5)`), or a constant; channels left out take their neutral values. Material IDs come from a constant or from painted gray levels snapped to the nearest stored level (or to listed levels, for Genshin-style 0 / 0.3 / 0.5 / 0.7 / 1), so they are flat regions compression keeps exact. One image may feed several channels.
  - `sapphire_pipeline bake-map <document> <png>` writes a document's map before compression, for checking a map without cooking the tree.
  - **Blender recipe** ([blender-mask-bake.md](blender-mask-bake.md), MIT script `tools/blender/bake_character_masks.py`): bakes object-space normals of a face (custom normals and modifiers included, armatures at rest) and turns them into threshold masks per angle that are monotonic by construction, plus AO and material-ID masks per body mesh, and writes the documents. It works on copies with temporary materials and never saves the `.blend`. Tested headless on Blender 5.2 LTS: 26 masks of 1024² in 3.5 s, Blender's start included.
  - The look-dev character's maps come with its import (A6): the tools are ready, and no character sources are in the repository yet.
- **As built (M1 A3, `sapphire_pipeline::{material, cook, import::gltf}`):**
  - Material documents are sources named `<name>.material.ron`, recognized by that suffix so other RON sources keep `.ron` free; their sidecars say `importer: Material(())`. They cook to `<name>.smat` beside the source's other outputs (recipe: importer `material` 1, cooker `smat` 1, format `Material::VERSION`).
  - A material reads the cooked textures it references, so `cook_dir` cooks every other asset first. Before the cache is consulted, on every cook, each referenced texture must be an asset of the content tree, cooked as a texture of a class its slot accepts (color for base color, ILM for the control map, ramp for ramps, SDF for face shadows, color or HDR for matcaps, emission and stars, masks or ILM for masks; A7's character maps count as the class they cook to), and each becomes a `Dependency::Asset` of the material's key by the texture's key. Editing, re-classing or deleting a texture therefore recooks or fails the materials that use it, cached or not. The cook records each texture's swizzle from its cooked KTX2 (so the mobile cook's two-channel masks record `ra01`), checks that a ramp is a whole number of A7's 16 rows tall (a day and a night row per material ID), packs the block and reads the bytes back through `Material::read`.
  - The glTF importer (version 4) turns each glTF material into a material slot with a generated anime character material: `baseColorFactor`, `alphaMode` and `alphaCutoff`, and the Blender extras `shade_color`, `outline_color` and `character_shading` (light-split threshold, softness, rim strength, outline width). Slot names are the material names, `#<index>` for unnamed materials and `#default` for glTF's default material, made unique by appending `#<index>` (again, if a literal name matches a made-up one). A cutoff above 1, which glTF allows, is clamped. Generated materials reference no textures, and packing one that does is an error. A mesh sidecar binds slots to material documents by name (`materials: {"Hair": "<id>"}`); every binding must name a slot of the source and a material document of the same tree, checked on every cook.
  - The cooked mannequin grows from 230,092 to 235,168 bytes (desktop): its five embedded materials, about 1 KB each. The cache key's encoding of mesh settings gained the bindings, so every existing key changes once.
- **As built (M1 A6, `sapphire_pipeline::{import::vrm, extract}`, sub-assets in `cook`):**
  - **VRM import.** `.vrm` sources, VRM 1.0 or 0.x, have sidecars saying `importer: Vrm((mesh: (…), version: Some(V1)))`: glTF's mesh settings (bindings included) and a note of the file's VRM version, read from the file when the sidecar is created. The note is checked, so a 0.x file re-exported as 1.0 fails until its sidecar says so: its materials and rig come out differently, and `V0` tells a reader the file is migrated. The extensions are read through pixiv's `vrm-spec` 0.1.1 (Apache-2.0; `VRMC_vrm`, `VRMC_materials_mtoon`, `VRMC_springBone` and VRM 0.x), and `VRMC_node_constraint`, which it has no types for, through the pipeline's own. Quirks: the crate doesn't build without its `gltf_index` feature, it turns on gltf's default `import` feature (base64, urlencoding, PNG and JPEG decoding; tool-only), and it types two indices as the wrong kind, which the importer reads as what they are.
  - The **mesh** goes through A5's glTF importer, with every node the VRM's data names (humanoid bones, spring joints and centers, colliders, constraint nodes) made a joint of the skeleton with its ancestors, skinned or not. MToon replaces the importer's generated materials; vertex colors are ignored, as MToon ignores them, and the outline-width texture (G; R in 0.x) is sampled bilinearly at each vertex's UV, with the texture's wrap modes, into the outline-width scale the vertex alpha carries. VRM's `meta` (licensing stays in `SOURCE.md`, M1 plan §3), first-person mesh annotations, `VRMC_vrm_animation` and `VRMC_springBone_extended_collider` are dropped.
  - **MToon → anime character materials** (the full table, field by field, is `import::vrm::mtoon`'s): band mode with two tones; the light split's threshold is `−shadingShift` and its softness `1 − toony`, exactly MToon's `linearstep`; region 0's shade color is `shadeColor / baseColor`, exact when the shade texture is the base color's image, as exporters write it; the shading shift texture becomes the control map's shadow bias (until it is extracted, its mean offset, measured on import, joins the threshold, so a 0.x grade texture's folded-in `−rate` doesn't push a generated material into shadow); the parametric rim's strength is its brightest channel and its width the N·V where MToon's falloff is at half (`1 + lift − 0.5^(1/power)`); the matcap keeps `1 − rimLightingMix` of its strength in shadow; outline widths in screen fractions are `× 1080` pixels, and world widths are converted at 2 m through 45°, since Sapphire's outlines are screen-constant. Unlit materials map as MToon with the shade side equal to the lit one. Dropped for want of fields in the family (C1–C7's to add): the rim multiply texture, emission, UV animation, GI equalization, outline lighting mix, render queue and Z-write controls, `doubleSided`, normal scale, `KHR_texture_transform` and `texCoord`; the face, hair or eye surface is the artist's to set on the extracted document.
  - **VRM 0.x migration** (as UniVRM's and three-vrm's, both MIT): the model turns half a turn about Y to face +Z, node by node, with joints' rest transforms and inverse bind matrices conjugated, and 0.x's Unity-space vectors (collider offsets, gravity, the first-person offset) get Z negated first. MToon's Unity properties are decoded (sRGB colors to linear; `toony = lerp(_ShadeToony, 1, ½ + ½·_ShadeShift)`, `shift = −_ShadeShift − (1 − toony)`; the shading grade texture becomes a shading shift texture with scale `_ShadingGradeRate`, linearized at the split; outline widths in centimeters or half-screen percent). Blend shape groups become expressions (`joy` → `happy`, `sorrow` → `sad`, `fun` → `relaxed`, `a`/`i`/`u`/`e`/`o` → `aa`/`ih`/`ou`/`ee`/`oh`, weights ÷ 100, `_MainTex_ST` to a UV transform with V flipped); the thumbs' proximal and intermediate become metacarpal and proximal; each secondary-animation root becomes one spring per branch (first children to a leaf, other children starting springs of their own) ending in a 7 cm virtual tail, which is how UniVRM 0.x swings them.
  - **Sub-assets.** A source may cook to more than one asset (`Recipe::sub_assets`): a VRM cooks to its mesh (`alicia.smesh`, the sidecar's ID) and its rig (`alicia.srig`, recipe `vrm`/`srig`, ID `meta::sub_asset_id(id, "rig")`: blake3 of both laid out as a version 8 UUID, so it needs no storage and never meets a random version 4 ID). Each is planned, keyed, cached and reported as an asset of its own, so the packer (A4) sees both. Each imports the file itself; the rig's import skips the materials and the vertex work. The VRM importer's version carries the glTF importer's in its low 16 bits.
  - **Decision: `extract-materials` is an explicit command**, never part of a cook, as A3 decided: cooks write no sources and rewrite no sidecars, so keys hold and `--locked` CI cooks change nothing, and extracted files are the artist's from then on. `sapphire_pipeline extract-materials <source> [--all]` writes, for `characters/alicia.vrm`, into `characters/alicia/`: the images its MToon materials use as `textures/<image>.png` with sidecars choosing the class by use (color for base, shade, matcap and emission; normal; masks for rim, outline-width and UV-animation textures; one file per class when an image has two), each shading shift texture as `maps/<image>.png`, without a sidecar, as the input of `maps/<image>.ilm.ron`, an ILM document putting the texture in G over `[0.5, 0.5 + scale/2]` with the other channels neutral, and each slot's document as `materials/<slot>.material.ron`, binding them; then it binds the slots in the source's sidecar. By default it extracts the slots whose documents bind a texture (the ones a generated material can't draw as authored); `--all` extracts every slot, glTF sources' too. PNG images are copied byte for byte, JPEG ones decoded and written as PNG (`image` gained its `jpeg` decoder). File names are decided over every slot and image of the source, whatever one run extracts, so a name always stands for the same slot, texture or map; two mesh sources sharing a stem in one directory can't both extract. It never overwrites a file, keeps every existing ID, and leaves slots the sidecar already binds alone; a slot whose document exists but isn't bound is bound to it again. Images that the family can't bind yet are still extracted, so nothing authored is lost.
  - Test VRMs are built in code (`import::vrm::samples`): one small character as VRM 1.0 and as UniVRM 0.x exported it (facing −Z, its data in the `VRM` extension), with textured, untextured and unlit materials, PNG and JPEG images, merged expressions, a branching hair chain, colliders and a twist constraint. The migrated 0.x import matches the 1.0 one vertex for vertex. No third-party VRM is in the repository or downloaded.
- **As built (M1 A4, `sapphire_pipeline::pack`):**
  - The packer is the stage after cooking, in the same process: `sapphire_pipeline cook --packs <dir>` packs the cook's assets, which the `CookReport` gives by ID, cooked file and cache key. `cargo xtask content` (and the mobile cook, for iOS and Android bundles) writes loose cooked files to `target/content/loose/<cook>/`, which tools read and hot reload (A8) will watch, and packs to `target/content/<cook>/`, the content root apps bundle and desktop runs read, which now holds only packs: stale packs, temporary files and the loose files shipped before packs are removed from it.
  - Each asset goes in exactly as cooked, its kind from its cooked format's extension and its dependencies the references the runtime's own check reads from its bytes; a dependency no pack holds fails the pack. Packs are deterministic: assets sorted by ID, serial encoders, no timestamps.
  - **Grouping rule** (spec §5.10's per zone, per character, per encounter, plus shared): zones, characters and encounters will be root documents (M2, M3); each root's pack holds what it reaches through dependencies, and an asset two roots reach goes in the shared pack, which apps mount first, so a group pack depends only on itself and the shared pack. Until roots exist, everything goes in `shared.spack`.
  - **Codec policy:** zstd at level 19 for textures and meshes, the bulk of a pack, loaded at zone and character transitions where install and download size matter most (the mannequin packs at 0.37 of its size; BC7 and ASTC textures at 0.66–0.69, against lz4's 0.86–0.88); lz4 for materials, small and decoded mid-play when hot reload and live link swap one, and for rigs, loaded with a character swapping in. Sub-assets (a VRM's rig, §5.27's A6 note) are packed and addressed by their derived IDs like any other asset. Level 19 encodes at 13–20 MB/s a core, which the cache absorbs; decoding speed barely depends on the level.
  - **Caching:** each asset's encoding is a content-cache entry keyed by the cooked bytes' hash, the packer's stage and the codec and level, so an encoding can't outlive the bytes it was made from and identical cooks share one; a cooked file edited after its cook is refused, and a pack directory that holds cooked files is refused rather than pruned; each pack records its file's hash under a key over its assets' IDs and encoding keys, so an unchanged pack is checked by hashing the file, without reading any encoding. A changed asset re-encodes alone, and an asset that recooks to the same bytes (a material whose texture changed but not its swizzle) reuses its encoding, and a pack deleted or edited by hand is rebuilt from cached encodings. The packer's stage name carries its encoders' versions (`spack (zstd 1.5.7, lz4_flex 0.14.0)`), which a test ties to `Cargo.lock`.
  - Timings on the M5, under load from parallel builds: `cargo xtask content` takes 0.45–0.68 s cold or warm, nearly all of it cargo checking that xtask and the pipeline are built. The pipeline itself takes 40 ms cold (19 ms without packing: zstd 19 on the 235 KB mannequin is the difference) and 8.3 ms warm (7.8 ms without packing); rebuilding a deleted pack from cached encodings takes about as long as a warm run. The desktop pack is 86,397 bytes, the mobile one 45,111.
- **As built (M1 C3, `sapphire_pipeline::{texture, samples::face}`):**
  - A one-channel SDF map cooks with the swizzle `r101` (`character_maps::face_sdf::ONE_CHANNEL_SWIZZLE`), so it reads G as 1 and the face shader treats it as a two-channel map whose vertical channel never shadows. The texture cooker's version is 2, so every texture recooks once; only one-channel SDF maps change.
  - `samples::face` writes the face test head's content into `content/samples/face/` (§5.12): the head as a glTF whose sidecar binds its face slot to a face material document, the material, a base color, the face mask, the expression map, and a face SDF document with its 26 threshold masks, all generated in code and committed. The desktop pack grows from C2's 92 KB to 305 KB.
- **As built (M1 C4, `sapphire_pipeline::{import::gltf, import::vrm::mtoon, samples::hard_edges}`):**
  - The glTF importer is version 6 (generated materials carry outlines v2's settings) and meshes are format 7 (§5.10), so every mesh recooks. The bake and authored outline normals are unchanged; the mesh encoder stores them in the tangent frame.
  - **MToon:** outline widths map onto the curve too. Screen widths (`factor × 1080` pixels) hold from the camera to the cutoff (`near` at 0, `far` at the cutoff, both at scale 1); world widths, converted at 2 m through 45° as before, put all three knots at 2 m, so the line keeps its width in meters at every distance and lens, as MToon draws it. `outlineLightingMixFactor` (0.x `_OutlineLightingMix`) becomes `outline.lighting`, no longer dropped.
  - **The hard-edged sample** (`samples::hard_edges`, `content/samples/hard_edges.glb` and its sidecar, generated and committed): a cube, a hexagonal prism, a triangular prism and an eight-sided gem in a row, each turned so its edges meet the camera at different angles, with every edge split, in four materials with dark color lines. The sandbox's `--hard-edges` loads it by ID; the golden scene `outlines-hard-edges` shows it.
- **As built (M1 C5, `sapphire_pipeline::{import::gltf, import::vrm::mtoon, samples}`):**
  - The glTF importer is version 7 and the VRM importer's version `(2 << 16) | 7`: generated materials carry the depth-offset rim, and material extras may set `rim` (an object with any of `width`, `strength`, `threshold`, `fresnel`, `light_gate`, `back_light`; unknown keys are errors) and `rim_regions` (`[[strength, width, overlap], …]` from region 0, each region past the first a copy of region 0 but for them, picked by `_MATERIAL_ID`).
  - **MToon:** `parametricRimFresnelPowerFactor` and `parametricRimLiftFactor` give the N·V where MToon's falloff is at half, as before, now the rim's Fresnel gate (`rim.fresnel`; it was the band's width); `rimLightingMixFactor` becomes `rim.light_gate` as it is, besides the matcap's shadow strength; `rimMultiplyTexture` is still extracted and dropped (the rim is masked per region).
  - **Samples:** the face test head's bangs, brows, hair, bangs and brow materials and hair mask, and the mannequin's arm region and outfit back-light weighting (§5.12); the sample documents are version 2. The desktop pack grows from 27 to 31 assets.
- **As built (M1 W6, `sapphire_pipeline::{bake::foliage, meta, samples::foliage}`):**
  - **Foliage import settings:** a mesh sidecar's `foliage: (normals: …, slots: […], blend: …, canopy_from: …)` (`FoliageSettings`, written out only when set, so other sidecars stay as they were) makes the cook run the foliage bake after the outline bake and before optimization, and the mesh cooks a foliage stream (§5.10); optimization carries the data through its reorder and LODs. Loading checks the settings (`blend` and `canopy_from` within 0–1, 1–64 clumps), and a slot name the source lacks fails the cook, naming the slots it has.
  - **Normal transfer,** chosen per mesh (`NormalTransfer`): `Sphere` (radial normals from the ellipsoid of the slots' bounds), `Clumps { count }` (the slots' cards, their connected pieces welded by position, clustered by deterministic k-means, each clump its own ball), `Hull { count }` (the gradient of a smooth field over the same clumps, a metaball proxy, so normals turn smoothly from clump to clump), or `Up` (grass: it lights like the ground). Cards' normals are flipped toward the volume's before the blend, a face at a time (the transferred triangles connected through shared vertices) by the sign of their sum against the volume's, so a two-sided card lights alike from either side and a card edge-on to the volume isn't flipped corner by corner; `blend` keeps a share of each card's own facing (0.8 for the sample tree).
  - **Wind and gradient data** for every vertex, wood included: sway grows as the square of the height fraction; bend with the distance from the vertical axis, eased in from 0 at the root over the lowest `BEND_RISE` (30%) of the height, so a trunk's foot stays planted (grass: the height); flutter is 1 on cards and 0 on wood (grass: the height); and the gradient runs from the canopy's center to its surface (wood and grass: the height). The phase comes from a field, the azimuth around the axis plus 0.31 cycles a meter outward, so branches pointing different ways swing in their own time; around the axis the field turns a whole cycle, so anything wrapped around it would move its sides in antiphase, squashing and stretching. A canopy's clump (the whole canopy for `Sphere`), a blade and a piece of wood around the axis (a trunk) each take the field at their centroid; branches keep it per vertex, swinging into their clump's phase at the tip. Sway and bend are continuous in the position, so vertices that meet sway and bend alike; flutter and the phases change from piece to piece, so cards are modeled as pieces of their own, as card kits and the samples do. The module docs have the table.
  - **Blob canopies.** Stylized kits model a canopy as a closed low-poly shell, often in the trunk's material (Kenney's fantasy town trees put both in one `colormap` slot): `canopy_from` leaves out the slots' pieces whose lowest point is below that fraction of the mesh's height, which keep their normals and never flutter, so the canopy alone rounds off. Pieces weld at the mesh's own weld tolerance (its sidecar's `outline.weld_tolerance`), so a kit whose split vertices drift further than the default 0.1 mm keeps its trunk whole. Checked on the kit's `tree.glb` (trunk 0–0.96 m, tiers above) with `normals: Sphere, canopy_from: 0.15` and its slot bound to a foliage material, in a scratch cook; the committed Kenney sidecars are unchanged (X1 places the kit's trees).
  - **Samples** (`samples::foliage`, `content/samples/foliage/`, generated and committed): `tree.glb` (a tapering trunk and five branches in the `Bark` slot, opaque, and 336 leaf cards in six clumps in `Leaves`: `Hull(count: 6)`), `bush.glb` (90 cards, `Sphere`), `grass.glb` (blades in a 2 m disc, shorter and sparser toward its edge, `Up`), their four foliage material documents (bark opaque with `alpha_cutoff` 0; leaves, bush and grass alpha-tested with translucency and wind) and two card maps, `leaves.png` (34 leaves on golden-angle turns) and `grass.png` (seven blades), clear at their borders so mips don't bleed. Their sidecars turn LODs off: the simplifier would collapse cards (impostors and card-thinning LODs are M2's). `cargo xtask content` lists them among the sample sources.
  - **Tests:** a sphere fit gives radial normals; the blend keeps a share of each card's facing on both sides; the hull is smooth across cards where clumps split; clumps light as their own balls; grass faces up, its tips bending and fluttering; wood sways with its leaves and never flutters; a blob canopy in its trunk's slot rounds off while its trunk stays wood; a card edge-on to the volume flips as one; pieces weld at the mesh's tolerance; unknown slots and bad settings are errors; k-means is deterministic. The samples import with their roots at the origin, wood facing outward, bake into one LOD with a foliage stream and bound materials, and generate deterministically; the sample tree's foot ring neither sways nor bends and keeps one phase, its trunk shares that phase, and its six clumps take a phase each.
- **As built (M1 W5, `sapphire_pipeline::probes`, `samples::environment`):** ambient probe grids, cooked from `*.probes.ron` documents (`ProbeGridDocument`, schema 1: the box the probes fill, spacing, visibility order and rays per cook, surfel rays and spacing, bounces and their strength, the timeline whose keys light the bounce, and the meshes the grid sees by ID, each placed) to `.sprobe` (§5.10). **Decision:** a document names its meshes; the bake never takes everything in the content tree, which shares one pack with third-party content (X1).
  - **The cache key** holds the document, every mesh as cooked and every material its slots name (by key), and the keys as the bake reads them, resolved: each key's hour, light, shadow tint and sky radiance by their bits (blake3), so a change to the timeline or to the code that resolves it (time of day's evaluation, its key light, the celestial sphere, `sky_radiance`) bakes again, which the timeline's text alone missed. A changed mesh, material, color map or key bakes again, and an unchanged tree takes the grid from the cache. Meshes cook first (`Cook` orders grids after them).
  - **What the bake leaves out:** fog cards (M1 W3's family). A card is a soft sheet of fog, not a surface: it neither hides the sky from a probe nor reflects bounce, so a submesh whose material is a fog card, embedded or referenced, is skipped. The courtyard's document names the courtyard and the arcade, whose submeshes hold no cards (its mist and haze are meshes of their own), so its grid is unchanged.
  - **Decision: foliage's cards bake as they are drawn** (M1 W6). Alpha-tested foliage hides the sky and bounces light through its leaves alone: a ray reaching a card reads its coverage (the color map's alpha times the base color's, nearest texel of the first level) at the hit's texture coordinates and passes below the cutoff, as the renderer's alpha test, prepass and shadow casters cut it, and the bake's own shadow rays cut out the same way. Leaving cards out, as fog cards are, would light the ground under a canopy as open sky, where the anime look wants a cooler, darker shade; baking them as solid quads would shade it under every hole and, seen from behind, mark the probes inside a canopy as inside geometry. Cards are two-sided, as drawn: a ray meeting a card's back meets leaves, and the leak test's rays pass cards (a leaf is no wall between two probes, so lookups inside a canopy keep their corners). Surfels sit where a card is drawn, facing the side its volume normal does, colored by the covered texels' mean through the middle of the material's gradient; opaque foliage (wood, closed canopies) bakes solid and one-sided like the environment, and a material nothing of which is drawn bakes as none. The bake is version 2; grids without foliage, the courtyard's among them, bake to the same bytes.
  - **The bake** (CPU, every core, deterministic: scalar `f32` in a fixed order, one ray set turned per probe by its index, every result in its own slot): bricks kept where a triangle passes within a cell of them; surfels every 0.5 m over the geometry, lit per key through W4's shading (wrapped Lambert, a shadow ray, the shadow tint × the material's kept) plus the key's sky through their own visibility, times albedo (the base color times its map's mean), plus emission; one more bounce between surfels; then each probe's rays (256 desktop, 128 mobile) give its sky visibility (L2 or L1) and, from the surfels they hit, its bounce per key projected to L1 irradiance, and six axis rays its free distances. Probes that see backfaces (inside geometry) take their open neighbors' mean and stay marked invalid. The rays walk a BVH that splits at the middle of the centroids' bounds, and at the median past depth 24, so clustered triangles can't deepen it past 56 levels and the walk's fixed stack always holds it.
  - **The sample:** `samples::environment` writes the courtyard's arcade (a gateway through the far wall and a roofed loggia along it, `arcade.glb`) and `courtyard.probes.ron` (both meshes, desktop 1.5 m with L2, mobile 3 m with L1, the default day's eight keys). `cargo xtask content` bakes it.
  - **Keyed emission** (M1, a W1 follow-up; §5.21): each key's emission is lit by that key's emission scale, but for a material's steady share, as the renderer draws it (`BakeMaterial::emission_under`), and the scale joins the resolved values in the cache key. The default day keeps 1 at every key, so the courtyard's grid bakes the same bytes.
  - **Cost:** the courtyard's bake takes about 0.5 s (1.9 s of CPU) for the desktop cook and 0.4 s (0.9 s of CPU) for the mobile cook on an M5's ten cores, release; an unchanged tree takes it from the cache. The desktop grid holds 6,973 probes in 84 of its 128 brick positions (1.53 MB cooked, 2.19 MB on the GPU), the mobile grid 1,445 in all 16 (303 KB, 383 KB); the packs grow by 1.37 MB and 275 KB with the arcade's mesh.
- **As built (M1 C6, `sapphire_pipeline::{import::vrm, samples::face}`):**
  - **VRoid's eyes map onto anime eyes' layers** without hand edits (`import::vrm::vroid_eye_layer`, from the parts of VRoid Studio's material names, `<model>_<Part>_<nn>_<GROUP>`): `EyeWhite`, `FaceEyeline`, `FaceEyelash` and `FaceBrow` are the eye itself, a blended one alpha-tested at 0.5 instead; `EyeIris` the iris, its ellipse the span of its submeshes' texture coordinates and its parallax depth 0.1 of its height; `EyeHighlight` the highlights at the default motion. All draw through the bangs at the default opacity and without an outline, and `extract-materials` writes the same mapping out. The face, mouth and everything else stay as before. VRM importer `(3 << 16) | 7`, so VRM files recook. X1's sample (`VRM1_Constraint_Twist_Sample`, every material OPAQUE) maps unedited: `EyeWhite_00_EYE`, `FaceEyeline_00_FACE` and `FaceBrow_00_FACE` become the eye, opaque; `EyeIris_00_EYE` the iris, blended, over the ellipse centered at (0.748, 0.509) with half-extents (0.108, 0.246) and 2.6 mm deep; `EyeHighlight_00_EYE` the highlights, blended. X1's look-dev character binds its own documents to those slots (`content/lookdev/character/materials`, extracted and edited), which override the import's; C6 moves their eye parts onto the same layers, and a test holds every eye document to `vroid_eye_layer`'s layer for its name. Seen close in the hero shot the eyes read as before (brown irises with a pupil, the lash line, two small highlights), now clipped to the eye, the iris shifting a little behind the cornea and the highlights living.
  - **Samples:** the face test head's eyes are meshes of their own (one `Eyes` mesh, six slots: eye white, the lid's shadow, iris, highlights, lashes, lids), on the anime eye outline (a raised outer corner, a broad upper lid, a winged lash thickening outward), with generated iris, shadow and highlight textures, and six morph targets on the eye vertices: `LidsHalf` and `LidsClosed` (applied in two steps, so the lid follows the curved head without its chord sinking in), `LookLeft`, `LookRight`, `LookUp` and `LookDown`. The face's painted eyes and its draw through hair are gone (the eyes draw through themselves). The desktop pack gains nine assets: six materials and three textures. `gltf_writer::set_mesh_field` writes the targets' names into the mesh's extras.
- **As built (M1 C2 follow-up, VRM region GI, `sapphire_pipeline::import::vrm`):**
  - **VRoid's names give the stylized GI's region weights** (`RegionGi`, §5.10; the GI, §5.12). The import left them at (0, 0) on every material, so no imported character took the head's sphere or the skin tint without hand edits. `import::vrm::vroid_region_gi` reads the group VRoid Studio's material name ends in (`<model>_<Part>_<nn>_<GROUP>`, hair numbered after it, as `N00_000_Hair_00_HAIR_01 (Instance)`): `HAIR` (the strands, the bangs among them, and `HairBack`, the cap under them) takes the sphere, (sphere 1, skin 0); `SKIN` on the `Face` part (`Face_00_SKIN`) the sphere and the skin tint, (1, 1); `SKIN` on any other part (`Body_00_SKIN`) the skin tint, (0, 1). Everything else keeps (0, 0), the GI its normals give it: clothes (`CLOTH`), eyes (`EYE`), and VRoid's `FACE` group, which holds what it draws on or inside the face rather than its skin (brows, lash line, lashes, and the mouth's inside, teeth and tongue). The face sample's documents (C2) carry the same weights: face and lids (1, 1), hair and bangs (1, 0), eye parts (0, 0).
  - **Decision: from the name.** Neither VRM 1.0 nor MToon has a field saying which material is a face, skin or hair, and the import has no shading family to read (it keeps every material a body but VRoid's eyes, since faces and hair need maps an artist authors), so VRoid Studio's names are the only signal, as for C6's eyes. A name it doesn't place (another exporter's, or one an artist renamed) keeps (0, 0) until the weights are set on its extracted document. MToon and unlit materials take the weights; a plain glTF material keeps what the glTF importer generates.
  - `extract-materials` writes the weights into the documents it generates. VRM importer `(4 << 16) | 7`, so VRM files recook; no Material or Mesh bump (the field and its packing are C2's). X1's VRM binds all 13 slots to its look-dev documents, which override the import, so its cooked mesh and every golden are byte-identical. A fresh extraction of its model writes `Face_00_SKIN` (1, 1), `Body_00_SKIN` (0, 1), `Hair_00_HAIR` and `HairBack_00_HAIR` (1, 0) and the other nine (0, 0); the look-dev documents have the face and body at (0, 0) and the hair at (1, 0), left for X1 part 2's look tuning.
- **As built (M1 C7 follow-up, MToon onto the layers, `sapphire_pipeline::{import::vrm::mtoon, character_maps::layer_mask, extract}`):**
  - **Every MToon parameter, audited.** C7 gave the anime character family the layers MToon's emission and matcap need, and the A6 mapping (above) still dropped emission. The full mapping now, VRM 1.0's names (0.x's properties take the same paths through their 1.0 counterparts, `Mtoon::from_v0`); `import::vrm::mtoon`'s docs hold the same table:

    | MToon (1.0) | Sapphire | How |
    |---|---|---|
    | `baseColorFactor`, `baseColorTexture` (glTF) | `base_color`, `base_color_map` | As they are |
    | `alphaMode`, `alphaCutoff` (glTF) | `alpha` | Opaque, `Mask { cutoff }` (clamped to 1), `Blend` |
    | `normalTexture` (glTF) | `normal_map` | As it is; its `scale` is dropped |
    | `shadeColorFactor`, `shadeMultiplyTexture` | band mode with two tones, `regions[0].shade_color` | `shadeColor / baseColor` per channel; exact when the shade texture is the base color's image, as exporters write it, else approximated by it |
    | `shadingShiftFactor`, `shadingToonyFactor` | `light_split` | Exact: threshold `−shift`, softness `1 − toony` |
    | `shadingShiftTexture` and `scale` | `control_map` G | An ILM document over the texture; a generated material takes its mean offset |
    | `giEqualizationFactor` (0.x `1 − _IndirectLightIntensity`) | none | Not mapped: the stylized GI flattens per character (C2's camera bias) and per region by name (sphere and skin weights), not per material |
    | `matcapTexture` | `matcap.map`, `regions[0].matcap` (color, added) | MToon's matcap is C7's region default; added in the key light's color |
    | `matcapFactor` | `matcap.strength`, `regions[0].matcap.color` | Strength its brightest channel, the tint the color over it × the rim multiply texture's hue (held by the layer mask) or mean (not) |
    | `rimLightingMixFactor` | `rim.light_gate`, `matcap.shadow_strength` | As it is; the matcap's shadow side takes `strength × (1 − mix)` |
    | `parametricRimColorFactor` | `rim.strength`, `regions[0].rim_color` | The color × the rim multiply texture's mean color: brightest channel and tint |
    | `parametricRimFresnelPowerFactor`, `parametricRimLiftFactor` | `rim.fresnel` | The N·V at MToon's half falloff, `1 + lift − 0.5^(1/power)`, as the depth-offset rim's Fresnel gate (the decision below) |
    | `rimMultiplyTexture` (sRGB) | the layer mask's R; its mean on the rim | Masks the matcap where the layer mask holds it; the depth-offset rim takes no texture |
    | `outlineWidthMode`, `outlineWidthFactor` | `outline.width`, `outline.curve` | Screen: `× 1080` px, held to the cutoff; world: converted at 2 m through 45°, all knots at 2 m (C4) |
    | `outlineWidthMultiplyTexture` (G; 0.x R) | vertex color G | Baked per vertex (A6) |
    | `outlineColorFactor` | `regions[0].outline_color` | As it is |
    | `outlineLightingMixFactor` (0.x: `_OutlineLightingMix` in mixed mode, else 0) | `outline.lighting`, `outline.albedo` | Both as it is: MToon multiplies the line by its surface's lit color, albedo × light; exact at 0 and 1 |
    | `emissiveFactor` (glTF) × `KHR_materials_emissive_strength` (or `VRMC_materials_hdr_emissiveMultiplier`); 0.x `_EmissionColor` | `regions[0].emission`, `emission.period` 0 | C7's glow, unlit and steady, blooming as the world's light, × the emissive texture's hue (held) or mean (not) |
    | `emissiveTexture` (sRGB); 0.x `_EmissionMap` | the layer mask's B | Paints the glow where the layer mask holds it |
    | `uvAnimationMaskTexture`, `uvAnimationScroll{X,Y}SpeedFactor`, `uvAnimationRotationSpeedFactor` | none | Not mapped: the family's texture coordinates never move (§5.12's later milestones); the mask is extracted |
    | `transparentWithZWrite`, `renderQueueOffsetNumber` | none | Not mapped: no sort control |
    | `doubleSided`, `KHR_texture_transform`, `texCoord` (glTF) | none | Not mapped: no cull mode, one UV set, no UV transform |

  - **The layer mask.** MToon's rim multiply texture masks its matcap and its emissive texture paints its glow: C7's layer mask's R and B, one channel each where MToon's are colors. `Mtoon::layer_mask` decides what a material's holds: a body's (eyes bind their own mask in that slot), the rim multiply texture where it has a matcap, the emissive texture where it glows, the emission alone where the two differ in size (a layer mask's inputs share one). The import measures each such texture (`LayerTexture`, decoded from sRGB): the mask takes its brightest channel by mean, and the layer's color the rest of its hue, `mean / mean[channel]`, so a texture of one hue (gray, as VRoid Studio writes its hair's emission, or tinted) maps exactly and a many-colored one keeps its brightest channel's shape. Where no layer mask is bound (the generated material, an eye), each texture's mean color multiplies its layer's color instead, as the shading shift texture's mean joins a generated material's threshold.
  - **Decision: a layer mask document** (`<name>.layers.ron`, `character_maps::LayerMaskMap` v1, sidecar `CharacterMap(LayerMask((…)))`, importer `layers` `(1 << 16) | image`), rather than extraction baking a mask image: like the ILM documents A6 writes for shading shift textures, it packs the source images, copied byte for byte into `maps/` as its inputs, so the derived file is a short document, not pixels, and the artist can repaint an input or add a metallic or stocking mask by editing it. It names an input per channel (R matcap, G metallic, B emission, A stocking), each one channel of an image mapped onto a range or a constant; a channel left out is 1, what an unbound mask reads. It cooks as a four-channel mask (BC7, ASTC 4×4). Its channel inputs are the ILM documents', which gain `encoding: Srgb` (sRGB-encoded channels decoded to linear light first; linear, the default, isn't written, so ILM documents read and cook as before).
  - **Decision: MToon's parametric rim stays on the depth-offset rim, approximately** (C5's mapping). MToon's is a Fresnel glow over the whole surface whose width on screen follows the surface's curvature, thinning on a close-up and swamping a distant character; C5 replaced exactly that with a rim of a fixed pixel width at the silhouette, as the reference games draw theirs. A matcap could hold the Fresnel falloff (it is sampled by the view-space normal, and MToon adds the two together), but it would bring back the N·V rim C5 removed, needs a texture a generated material can't bind, and takes the one matcap slot, which MToon's own matcap fills.
  - **Fixes on the way:** MToon multiplies its outline by the lit surface color, so `outlineLightingMixFactor` now sets `outline.albedo` as well as `outline.lighting`; VRM 0.x's `_OutlineColorMode` 0 (a fixed color, its default) migrates to a lighting mix of 0, as UniVRM's and three-vrm's migrations do (it read `_OutlineLightingMix` regardless); `rimMultiplyTexture` is sRGB, so extraction writes it as a color texture where no layer mask holds it. Unlit materials take no glow.
  - `extract-materials` writes each layer mask document (`maps/<images>.layers.ron`, named after its inputs' images, R's then B's) and binds it as the document's `layer_mask`, with the glow and the matcap's tint in its region; a material with a layer mask counts as textured, so it extracts by default. VRM importer `(5 << 16) | 7`, so VRM files recook; no Material or Mesh bump (the fields are C7's).
  - **X1's look-dev character is unchanged:** its VRM binds all 13 slots to its own documents, which override the import, so its cooked mesh is byte for byte the base's and every golden is byte-identical. Its `Hair_00_HAIR` is the one material with a glow (factor (0.708, 0.254, 0.044) through `Hair_00_spe`, VRoid's painted hair highlight, gray); a fresh extraction would bind a layer mask of that image's R to it, glowing in the factor's orange, where X1 made the same image the hair highlight's mask instead.
  - **Tests:** VRM 1.0 and 0.x samples edited in their JSON (the 1.0 sample gains glows through its green outline image at strength 4, a 16² emissive image for the size rule, the HDR multiplier and an invalid strength; the 0.x sample `_EmissionColor` and `_EmissionMap`) check each parameter's destination, the hue and the mean, the layer mask's rule, unlit and eye materials, and that materials without glow or matcap keep every layer off; extraction writes and binds the masks and the tree cooks them; layer mask documents pack in order, unpainted at 1, decode sRGB inputs and fail on mismatched sizes.

- **As built (M1 X1 part 1, `sapphire_pipeline::{assembly, lookdev}`):**
  - **Assemblies.** `<name>.assembly.ron` (`sapphire::pipeline::Assembly` v1, sidecar `importer: Assembly((…))`, glTF's mesh settings) places a kit's glTF pieces (`"kit/model"`, from directories the document names), groups of placements (`"@group"`, nested) and three builtins (`#box`; `#quad`; `#ground`, a disc for open land) by position, turn about +Y and uniform or per-axis scale (negative mirrors, winding kept), and cooks them into **one static mesh** (`town.assembly.ron` → `town.smesh`): recipe `assembly`/`smesh`, the importer's version the glTF importer's in its low 16 bits (`(2 << 16) | 7`), no new format. The ground builtins come in cells of at most 2 m where cameras stand (`#quad` in a grid of them, at most 128 a side; `#ground` in rings 2 m apart out to 40 m from its center, then 1.25 times wider each, 128 segments round, without T-junctions): a triangle a camera's near plane clips has vertices far off screen, and WARP dropped a 2.4 km ground quad and shifted a 37 m one's texture coordinates where Metal and lavapipe drew both. Builtins' texture coordinates wrap by blocks of 64 tiles, so a disc kilometers across doesn't spread the mesh's unorm16 coordinates over centimeters. The pieces are file dependencies of the cache key, by the path the document names, with their external buffers.
  - **Surfaces.** Every triangle draws in one of the document's slots, picked by rules on the piece's name (`*` patterns), the palette cell its texture coordinates fall in, its glTF material and the placement's variant (inherited down groups); later rules win. A triangle no rule claims fails the cook, naming the piece, cell, material and variant, so no texel of a kit's palette reaches a frame. The sidecar binds slots to material documents, as a glTF's. Each placement may force a slot (builtins need one).
  - **Texture coordinates** are made anew in meters, `tile` meters a repeat per slot: planar on each face's own plane (U level, V down the slope) or boxed onto the nearest axis plane, level faces from above. **Occlusion** goes into the vertex color's R, which the environment family reads: non-level faces darken toward the ground (`ground`, `ground_height`), and gradient palette cells' place from top to bottom becomes shading (`gradient`). Identical triangles from overlapping pieces (shared corner posts), on positions quantized to 0.1 mm, are kept once; degenerate ones are dropped.
  - **`gen-lookdev`** paints the look-dev town's tiling maps in code (`lookdev::files`: plaster, ashlar, setts in running bond, roof tiles, timber, grass, foliage, rock, the terminator band), byte for byte deterministic and seamless, and derives the look-dev character's hair mask (`lookdev::hair_mask_png`, C5's layout) from the model's painted highlight layer: R the band as drawn, no shift, no occlusion. xtask writes them when missing, as it does the samples.
  - The look-dev town (`content/lookdev/town/`, 134 placements of 28 kit pieces, 78,122 triangles and 130,649 vertices in 21 slots, Float32 positions, no LODs) cooks in 0.09 s on the M5 to a 4.6 MB mesh; the desktop pack grows to 13.2 MB with it, the town's maps and materials and the character. Its sidecar binds the 21 slots to the town's environment materials.
  - **The look-dev character's materials** (`content/lookdev/character/materials/`, anime character documents version 2, bound by the VRM's sidecar) are `extract-materials`' output with deliberate edits, listed in the model's `MODIFIED.md`: outline widths that read; the parts MToon can't name (a face shaded by an SDF map, A7's Blender bake with ellipsoid normals, casting the bangs' shadow; two hair materials; eyes, brows and the lash line always lit, all but the whites drawn through the bangs), so the character prepass's IDs tell them apart (§5.12); C5's default rim, the model's MToon rim being black; and on the strands a painted band over the head's sphere, revealed by the derived hair mask. The model's images they read are extracted byte for byte into `content/third_party/vrm1_constraint_twist_sample/extracted/`, with Sapphire's sidecars.
  - **Later milestones:** zones (§5.21) place prefabs as culled, streamed instances; an assembly is one mesh, the right shape for a slice seen whole but not for a world (no per-part culling or LOD). Painted vertex tints and per-piece texture offsets wait for the environment family to read an albedo tint from the vertex.
- **As built (M1 X1 follow-up, the coplanar check in `sapphire_pipeline::assembly`):**
  - **What fails the cook.** Kit pieces that meet share planes, and two placements' triangles facing the same way in one plane z-fight, differently per backend and frame to frame (X1's banner and gable roofs). After placing and before packing, every assembly cook finds each pair of triangles from different placements (a placed piece or builtin; a piece's own faces never count) whose normals are within 2.5° and whose planes are within 1 mm over a region more than 2 mm across. Faces in one slot count only where they shade differently there: occlusion more than 4 of the vertex color's 255 levels apart (ambient 1.6% apart), normals more than 0.5°, or texture coordinates more than 1/1024 of a tile apart, less whole tiles. Back-to-back faces never fight (environment back faces are culled). The first fight in the document's order fails the cook (`AssemblyError::Coplanar`), naming both pieces, their placements' paths (`place[8] > gate[14]`), their slots, a point where they meet in meters, and how many more pairs of placements fight. The 1 mm is margin: reverse-Z `Depth32Float` orders faces 0.01 mm apart at a kilometer. In the town every overlapping pair is either exactly coplanar or at least 4.8 mm apart (X1's hand fixes; the closest are the gatehouse planks' ends behind its corner posts), so the tolerance decides nothing there.
  - **The search** is sound and deterministic: buckets of normals 0.1 wide in normal space, centered on the axes so level and upright faces each fall in one; each face is also a guest in every bucket its 2.5° neighborhood reaches, and each pair is tested in exactly one bucket. Within a bucket, slabs of overlapping offsets along its normal (one placement's slab, the ground disc's, is skipped) are swept along an axis in its plane. The exact test clips one triangle by the other in the first's plane, then by the band where the planes are within 1 mm, and measures the region's narrowest width; same-slot shading is compared at the region's corners (attributes are linear over it). A unit test checks the buckets against testing every pair, with 600 faces crowding a corner where buckets meet; removing the guests fails it. On the town (88,732 triangles, 2,167 overlapping pairs found) the check takes 13–15 ms in debug and release cooks alike, of a 60–75 ms import.
  - **`allow_coplanar`** (`CoplanarAllowance`, optional; the document stays version 1): entries let through the overlaps matching every filter they give: `pieces` (two placement names, `*` patterns, either way round), `within` (both placements inside one placement of a group), `facing` (a direction, within 2.5°) and `region` (a box holding the overlap's middle). Each must give pieces, a group or a region (a direction alone would let a whole facing through) and a non-empty `why`. An entry that lets nothing through fails the cook (`StaleAllowance`), so stale or misspelled entries can't linger and the list stays minimal.
  - **The town** cooks clean with six entries, all hidden, each checked once (a scratch test, not kept) by casting 96 rays from 28–132 of the overlaps it lets through against the town's own triangles, and lines of sight to the stage's three cameras: undersides on the paving (facing down, within the town's footprint at y = 0) and the mountains' feet 12 m under the fields, seen only from under the ground; attic floors (interiors' tops flush with their top storey's walls, under the roofs); the fountain's two basin floors under their opaque water; and the gatehouse's floor planks where they cross its arches' walls (their tops in the arches' tops, their sides in the arches' ends, under the storey's walls), in a region that stops 5 cm inside the gate's front and back faces so the planks' ends can't hide behind it.
  - **Two fights the check found, fixed.** X1's slot-only scan couldn't see them: same-slot overlaps the kit's gradient shades two ways (occlusion 30 or more levels apart). The gatehouse planks' ends met the arches' corner posts at the gate's front and back, timber on timber in the stage cameras' line of sight; the planks are now 0.3% shorter, so each end stops 4.8 mm behind the posts' faces. Each house's back roof segment reached into the front segment's back bargeboard and sat 2.6 cm low, so that its eave's underside, which lies in the plane of the board's underside, cleared the board; that sank its foot into its storey's walls on the houses' backs and outer sides. It now sits on the walls like the front segment, 4.5% shorter along its ridge, running from the back wall's face to the front segment's back bargeboard and stopping there. The board's back face covers the segment's whole cross-section (the segment's ridge 0.11 tile under the board's top, its eave's lower corner on the board's underside), so the board closes the joint at the ridge and the eaves with no overlap and no added cap, and the two meet back to back. Restoring either old placement fails the cook naming it (the planks against the corner posts, timber on timber; the roof against the round-window storey, 84 pairs of placements). Headless captures before and after, from close cameras on a temporary patch (not kept) and the stage's three shots at 2560×1440: on Metal the fights read within 1–3 code values (a band of the corner posts showing the plank ends' shading; the wall-top band); after, the posts and walls read as themselves, and the roof joint shows no gap from behind and above, along the side rows' eaves or in any stage shot.
  - **Cooked output:** the check alone changed none (the town's mesh cooked byte-identical to X1's while the two fights stood allowed). The fixes move geometry, so the town's mesh and the look-dev goldens change; no format or stage version changes (the town's document changed, so it recooks).
- **As built (M1 X1 part 2a, the look, `sapphire_pipeline::{lookdev, samples::sky}`):**
  - **The stage's own day** is a source document the cooker doesn't read: `content/lookdev/lookdev_day.ron`, a `TodTimeline` (§5.21) that the sandbox compiles in and loads only with `--look-dev`, its dev builds hot-reloading it. Its ten keys, their sun path and why its shadow tints lean blue are in the document's header; the m1-plan X1 row has the look it aims at.
  - **Plants as foliage meshes.** The foliage bake (W6) sways and bends a mesh about its own origin and vertical axis, so a town-wide assembly can't carry foliage: each plant is its own mesh, rooted at its origin, and the sandbox places it. `gen-lookdev` writes W6's sample tree, bush and grass geometry once per look-dev plant (`lookdev::PLANTS`, `foliage/*.glb`), so each file's sidecar binds its slots to a foliage material of its own (a sidecar binds materials per source file): green, blossom and autumn trees, green and lavender bushes, grass. The kit's two tree pieces become foliage through single-piece assemblies (`foliage/kit_tree*.assembly.ron`): an Assembly sidecar carries the same `foliage` settings as a glTF's, the assembly re-materials the trunk and crown into `Bark` and `Leaves` slots, and the bake runs on the cooked result.
