  - **The night's Milky Way** is painted by `samples::sky::milky_way_png_in` from a `MilkyWayPalette` (the band's cool and warm edge colors, its clumps and their tint, its brightness). `milky_way_png` is the sample palette, byte for byte the map it wrote before; `lookdev::MILKY_WAY` paints the look-dev sky's teal band (`sky/milky_way.png`), which its own sky material (`sky/sky.material.ron`, otherwise the sandbox sky's maps) reads.
  - **The town's assembly** loses its tree placements and their `foliage` and `bark` slots, and gains `planter` and `pot` groups (`#box`es of stone topped with grass) where the sandbox plants beds and potted plants.
  - **The look-dev character's GI weights** are the import's now: X1 part 2 set `Face_00_SKIN` to (1, 1) and `Body_00_SKIN` to (0, 1), and a test holds every look-dev character document's weights to `import::vrm::vroid_region_gi` for its name.
- **As built (M1 A10, FBX import, `sapphire_pipeline::{import::fbx, meta, extract}`):**
  - **FBX sources.** `.fbx` sidecars say `importer: Fbx((…))`, with glTF's mesh settings (outline weld, LODs, position format, material bindings, foliage). Files import through the `ufbx` crate 0.11.5 (MIT or Unlicense: the single-file C library ufbx 0.23.1, built with `cc`, tool-only) to the same `ImportedMesh` glTF's importer makes, so the outline and foliage bakes, LODs, optimization and packing are shared. The importer reads the file alone (`load_external_files` off), so its bytes are its whole cache key; its stage is `fbx (ufbx 0.11.5)` 1, which a test ties to `Cargo.lock`, so a ufbx upgrade recooks every FBX.
  - **Space.** ufbx converts each file on load to engine space (+Y up, meters, right-handed, facing +Z), folding the conversion into the top-level nodes' transforms, so the ×100 Blender puts on top-level objects cancels and skeletons come out in meters at unit scale. Left-handed files mirror in Z with their winding turned, geometric transforms join each mesh's geometry-to-world matrix, and Maya's segment scale compensation becomes plain inheritance. Every node with a mesh adds it in that space, as glTF's nodes do; ufbx triangulates polygons by ear clipping.
  - **What matches glTF.** Each material a node uses is a slot named as glTF's are (the shared `import::slot_names`), holding a generated anime character material: base color times factor, opacity as alpha (blended below 1). Corners merge into vertices where normal, first UV set (V flipped) and first color set (as stored: data) agree, and missing normals become flat ones. A skin is one per mesh: the skeleton holds the clusters' bones and their ancestors, depth-first. Each cluster's palette entry is `geometry_to_bone · G⁻¹` with `G` the mesh node's geometry-to-world matrix, so the mesh skins exactly as the file binds it, whatever pose it was bound in. The four heaviest weights are kept, and static parts bind rigidly to their nearest joint. Blend shape channels become morph targets named by channel and merged by name. Each keeps its full-weight shape, scaled by Blender's per-vertex shape weights, within glTF's epsilons; in-between shapes are dropped.
  - **Not imported:** animation (M2 cooks clips to ozz through ufbx), authored outline normals and material IDs (FBX has neither: outlines are baked, IDs are 0), tangents, second UV and color sets, cameras, lights, NURBS and LOD groups.
  - **Textures.** Generated materials reference none, as glTF's don't. `extract-materials` on an FBX writes each slot's material document and binds its base color and normal maps:
    - an external PNG, TGA or EXR the FBX names, relative to itself, is bound where it lies. A missing sidecar is created with the class of the image's use, and the cook's default color class is switched to normal for a normal map; a sidecar of any other class is kept and reported;
    - embedded images, and images in other formats, are written as `<stem>/textures/<name>.png` with sidecars;
    - an image found nowhere fails the extraction.
  - **Sources that don't cook.** A sidecar may say `cook: false`, written only when false. The source keeps its ID and settings, and `--locked` still needs its sidecar, but no cook imports, writes or packs it. Its stale outputs are pruned, `CookReport::uncooked` lists it, and a reference to it fails as a missing asset's would. This is for sources that feed other sources rather than the game. The pavilion FBX under `content/third_party/` is one: X1 converts it in Blender to a glTF piece of its own, and cooking it as well would add 1.3 MB (9%) to the desktop pack, and about a second to every cold cook, for an asset nothing reads. Its cooked mesh is 4.4 MB on both cooks: its normals are split at nearly every face, so A5's LOD search finds nothing worth dropping. Leaving cooked assets that nothing reaches out of packs is pack roots' job (§5.10, M2).
  - **Tests.** `tools/blender/fbx_fixtures.py` (MIT) runs headless and never saves the `.blend`. It builds three scenes:
    - a static mesh in three materials, with float vertex colors, a non-uniformly scaled part and a mirrored one, exported as FBX and glTF;
    - a skinned tube on a three-bone armature, with three shape keys (one at weight 0.25) and a bone-parented prop, exported as FBX and glTF;
    - a textured crate, exported as FBX twice, with its images beside it and embedded, for the extraction tests.
  - **Parity.** Each FBX fixture must import to its glTF's mesh: triangles matched per slot within 2e-5 m in position, 1e-4 in normals (Blender's glTF exporter rounds them to four decimals), one color step and 1e-4 in weights, with joints, rest poses and morph deltas compared too. Morph normals are compared away from hard edges only, since FBX offsets a vertex's normal, not a corner's. Blender 5.2's glTF exporter writes vertex colors only for its first material's primitives, which the script patches for its own exports. Other tests hand-write ASCII FBX for concave polygons, missing normals and materials, and Z-up centimeter files.
  - **The pavilion** (VVayToyek's, Blender 3.2's FBX, centimeters) imports in meters, standing on its origin: 70 meshes, 22 Phong materials whose Chinese names become slot names, 76,926 triangles, 35.7 m tall and 38.9 m wide. It has no textures.
  - **Timings** on the M5 (release, the minimum of seven runs): the pavilion imports in 27 ms, and its whole `cook-mesh` takes about 1 s (outline bake 44 ms, LOD search and optimization 180 ms, the rest packing and writing). Each fixture imports in 0.2–0.4 ms as FBX and 0.1–0.2 ms as glTF.

| Asset | Source | Importer | `desktop` cook | `mobile` cook |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Meshes (static and skinned) | Blender → glTF 2.0; FBX via `ufbx` | `gltf` 1.4, `ufbx` 0.11 | Quantized vertex streams; `meshopt` 0.7 vertex-cache / overdraw / fetch optimization; LODs; outline normals, either authored (an `_OUTLINE_NORMAL` vertex attribute, from M1) or baked by welding (M0: angle-weighted, sliver- and cancellation-safe, 0.1 mm weld) | Same, with a lower-detail LOD0 and fewer LODs |
| Kit assemblies | `.assembly.ron` placing glTF kit pieces (M1 X1) | serde, `gltf` 1.4 | One static mesh as above, slots re-materialed by rule, texture coordinates in meters, occlusion in vertex color | Same |
| Characters | VRM 1.0 / 0.x (`.vrm`) | `gltf` 1.4, `vrm-spec` 0.1 | A mesh as above with MToon mapped onto anime character materials, plus an rkyv `.srig` rig (humanoid, expressions, look-at, spring bones, constraints) | Same |
| Color textures | PNG / TGA / EXR | `image`, `exr` | KTX2 (`ktx2` 0.5) BC7 via `ctt` 0.6 | KTX2 ASTC 4×4–8×8 per texture class, via `ctt` |
| Normal maps / masks / HDR | same | same | BC5 / BC4, BC5 or BC7 by channel count / BC6H | ASTC; HDR as RGBA16F or RGB9E5 |
| Ramps, ILM maps, SDF face maps | RON documents: color stops, and masks painted or baked in Blender | serde, `image` | Uncompressed RGBA8 (ramps), BC7 (ILM), R16/RG16 (SDF) | RGBA8, ASTC 4×4, R8/RG8 |
| Animation | glTF / FBX | `ufbx` / `gltf` | ozz archives plus root-motion track and curves | Same, with stronger compression |
| Materials | `.material.ron` documents, one type per family | serde | rkyv `.smat`: the family's GPU block and texture references | Same, with the mobile textures' swizzles |
| Move graphs, frame data, effects, UI, tiers | Editor documents (RON) | serde | rkyv | rkyv |
| Zones | Blender glTF + custom-property extras, plus editor documents | `gltf` | Cells, instances, probes, navmesh tiles (`rerecast`) | Same, with lower density and fewer probes |
| Ambient probe grids | `.probes.ron` documents naming the zone's meshes | serde | rkyv `.sprobe`: sparse bricks, L2 sky visibility, L1 bounce per time-of-day key | Same, at twice the spacing with L1 visibility |
| Shaders | WGSL / WESL | `wesl`, `naga` | Validated WGSL plus permutation inventory | Same, with mobile tier flags |
| Audio | FMOD Studio project | FMOD Studio build | `.bank` (desktop) | `.bank` (mobile codecs) |
| Fonts | TTF / OTF | `skrifa` | Subset font plus MSDF atlas for display text | Same |
| Strings | `.ftl` | `fluent` | Binary tables | Same |

- **Blender as the level and modeling tool:** export glTF with custom-property extras (the pattern Bevy's Skein uses). The in-engine editor then only needs to cover gameplay data, combat tuning, VFX timing and cinematics.

### 5.28 `sapphire_editor` (Tools, desktop only)

**How**

- **Document model first** (after The Machinery's "The Truth"):
  - All editor state is typed objects with GUIDs and reflected properties.
  - Every edit is a change record `(object, property, old, new)`.
  - Undo and redo, copy and paste, dirty tracking and live-link sync all fall out of that one model.
  - Documents save as RON files that are friendly to git.
- **UI:**
  - `egui` 0.37 with `egui_dock` or `egui_tiles` for docking.
  - `transform-gizmo` 0.11 for manipulators and `egui-snarl` 0.12 for graphs (move graphs, animation graphs).
  - `egui_plot` for curves.
- **Platform:** the editor runs on `sapphire_platform` (SDL3) too. The viewport is the real engine renderer drawing into a texture that egui displays, so the editor and the game share one input and windowing stack.
- **Play-in-editor:** creates a fresh runtime world from a snapshot of the current documents. Game code never mutates documents.
- **Combat timeline editor**, the highest-value tool:
  - Tracks for hitboxes, hurtboxes and invulnerability, cancel windows, root motion and warp, and cues.
  - Frame-accurate scrubbing with a preview character and an enemy dummy.
  - "Play from frame N."
  - A hitbox-gizmo editing mode on bones.
- **Other panels:**
  - Asset browser.
  - Inspector.
  - Material editor with live preview of ramps, ILM and outlines.
  - VFX editor.
  - Sequencer.
  - UI preview at the 4:3, 16:9 and 21:9 aspect ratios.
  - Cvar panel.
  - Profiler (Tracy connection).

### 5.29 `sapphire_livelink` (a dev-build runtime half plus its desktop tool)

- **Transport:** TCP, with postcard-framed messages. The device dev build listens; the editor connects over Wi-Fi or USB (`adb reverse` / `iproxy`).
- **Editor → device:** change records, recompiled shaders, cooked assets, cvar sets and replay files.
- **Device → editor:** stats, logs, screenshots and Tracy (connected directly).
- **Why it matters:** combat feel and touch controls have to be tuned on the phone, not on the Mac.
- **As built (M1 A8, what A9 builds on):** the device half applies what it receives through the same calls desktop hot reload uses. A pushed pack goes into a `MemoryPackSource` (or a source that serves pushed packs over the platform's) and `AssetServer::remount` reloads what changed behind the handles, which the app's uploader replaces in place by generation (§5.10); pushed WGSL text goes to `Renderer::set_shader_sources`, which checks it and keeps the old pipelines on an error (§5.12); a pushed document goes to its owner (`TimeOfDay::set_timeline`). Pushing only the changed assets, as a pack mounted over the shared one, is A9's to add.
- **As built (M1 A9, `sapphire_livelink`, minimal live link):**
  - **One crate, two halves.** `sapphire_livelink` is `dev_only` (`xtask/layers.toml`): the device half, which apps link through `dev` on all five platforms, and the desktop tool, a binary of the same crate that `cargo xtask link` builds for the host and runs with `--content target/content`. Shipping builds link none of it (rule R8). The tool's own dependencies (anyhow, A8's `FileWatcher` and source paths) are built into dev device builds too, which already have `sapphire_hot_reload`; `notify` comes only on desktops, as for the sandbox.
  - **Protocol, version 1.** Each side first writes an 8-byte preamble, the magic `SPLK` and the protocol version as fixed bytes (not postcard), so builds of any two versions read each other's: a device of another version answers with its own and closes, and the tool says which versions met ("build the app and the tool from the same source"). Then every message is a frame, a little-endian u32 length and the postcard message, at most 4 MiB, refused unread beyond that. The tool sends requests with IDs (status, cvar, document, shaders, and a pack in three steps), the device answers each with a reply of the same ID or an error, after which it keeps what it had. Message layouts change only with the version.
  - **The device half.** `LiveLink` listens (by default at 127.0.0.1:7811) or dials a listening tool (`adb reverse`), on a thread of its own. Each connection gets a reader thread, which reads requests and receives and assembles pushed packs, and a writer thread, through which every reply goes, so the frame never waits on the network. The app takes what came with `LiveLink::poll` once a frame (one empty channel read, no allocation, while nothing did) and applies it between frames, before loading runs: packs through `PushedPack::apply` (the server's remount), documents and shader sources through its own owners, cvars through `set_cvar`. Nothing grows without bound and nothing holds the app: pushes queue 16 deep (a full queue holds the connection's reader back, never the app, which applies at most four a frame), each connection queues 64 replies (a tool that reads none loses the rest), and eight connections are served at once. A listening device listens again if its socket fails (iOS reclaims a suspended app's listening sockets); a dialing one waits for the tool to accept however long, and a second between dials (behind `adb reverse`, adbd takes every dial at once and closes it when no tool listens on the desktop). `ReloadTracker` answers a pushed pack once the asset server has nothing loading or reloading, with every new version swapped in and its generation, and every one that failed; it also keeps every replaced asset's generation for the status (app, platform, cook, frame and frame time, the asset counters, the generations and the link's counters).
  - **Packs: only what changed.** The tool sends the new pack's header and table of contents; the connection's thread checks them with the pack reader and compares them with the version it serves (the pushed one, or the app's file, whose table of contents it reads through the app's source): an asset of the same ID, kind, size, hash and stored blocks is taken from there, and the device asks for the byte ranges it lacks, which the tool streams in order, in chunks of at most 256 KiB, checked as they come (out of order, past a range or short is a refusal). The app then serves the new version through `LivePacks`, its pieces read from memory or from the version before, and remounts it: A8's remount reloads exactly the assets whose hashes changed, behind their handles. Each asset the device lacks is a range of its own, kept in a buffer of its own that grows as its bytes come, so a later push that drops it frees it; the padding between assets, which nothing reads, is served as zeros, as the packer writes it; a push asks for at most 1 GiB. Reads of more than 256 KiB of received bytes (a pushed texture) are copied on a worker, since the asset server reads on the app's thread. Pushing a pack the app doesn't mount stores it for whatever mounts it later. A pushed pack overrides the app's own until the app restarts, so a desktop running both triggers takes the pushed version.
  - **Documents** go by the stable type name their envelope gives (`sapphire_serialize::document::type_name`, read on the connection's thread, so text without an envelope is refused there): the sandbox's `Document` maps time of day's timeline and the character light rig presets to A8's parse-and-swap, which keeps the old one on any error. **Shader sources** go by file name into the renderer's current sources and `Renderer::set_shader_sources`, all or none, and a renderer made later gets them as A8's do. **Cvars** go through the app's `CvarRegistry`, as the console sets them.
  - **The tool:** `cargo xtask link status`, `cvar <name> <value>`, `push [<file>...]` (`.spack` packs named by their stem, `.ron` documents, `.wgsl` sources; with no files, every pack of the cook the device reports, in `target/content/<cook>/`) and `watch`, which pushes the packs `cargo xtask content --cook <cook>` rewrites, the renderer's WGSL sources and the two documents as they change (A8's debounced `FileWatcher` and `sources` paths), and connects again when the device goes. `--addr <host:port>` (default 127.0.0.1:7811) or `--listen <host:port>`, which checks each connection it takes with a status request, skipping those a restarted device left in its backlog.
  - **Transports.** Plain TCP to an address. Desktop and the iOS Simulator (which shares the Mac's network stack): 127.0.0.1:7811. An iPhone over USB: `iproxy 7811 7811` forwards the Mac's port to the phone's loopback (libimobiledevice; no device here to try it). Android over USB: `adb forward tcp:7811 tcp:7811` with the app listening, or `adb reverse tcp:7811 tcp:7811` with the app dialing (`--live-link connect=127.0.0.1:7811`) and the tool listening (`--listen 127.0.0.1:7811`); Android needs the `INTERNET` permission for any socket, loopback included, which the manifest now declares (a normal permission; store builds, M4, need it for online services anyway). Wi-Fi: the app listens on the LAN (`--live-link 0.0.0.0:7811`) and the tool connects to the device's address; an iPhone may ask for local network access, whose `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` comes with device signing (M4).
  - **Security.** Anyone who can connect can replace a dev build's content, shaders and cvars: there is no authentication or encryption. So the device listens on loopback by default, which only the machine itself and USB forwards reach; any other address is an explicit opt-in, logged as a warning, for trusted networks only; and shipping builds have none of it. Frames, pushes and every pushed byte's use are bounded and hash-checked, so a malformed request can't make the device allocate unboundedly or decode unverified data. A pairing token is for later.
  - **The sandbox:** on by default in `dev` builds on every platform, in the windowed app and the offscreen modes alike; `--live-link off|<addr>|listen=<addr>|connect=<addr>` changes how. A port already taken leaves it off with a warning, and the app runs on. The overlay shows it (§5.20).
  - **Tests:** framing (round trips of every message, frames over 4 MiB refused unread, truncated and malformed frames), preambles (other versions and other protocols refused), planning and chunking (only changed assets and new ones sent, pushes over pushes, a pack the device lacks sent whole and read back on a worker, padding between assets served as zeros, out-of-order, overlong, short and unrequested chunks, invalid heads), reads that gather across pieces, and over loopback TCP: a pushed material change reloads behind the same handle as generation 1, in the same slot, with the new bytes, sending under 1 KB of the pack (its texture stays), and pushing it again sends nothing; a 1 MiB pack streams in chunks and serves a later mount; a pack the server refuses keeps the device's version, and a corrupt head is refused before anything is sent; a document whose data doesn't fit, or that doesn't parse, is refused with the old timeline kept, and a valid one replaces it; cvars set, with an unknown name and an out-of-range value refused; both sides refuse the other protocol version; a device dials a listening tool, and when each dial is closed at once dials again, each time a measured second or more after the close (CI first counted dials over a loop of sleeps, which a loaded runner stretched; the wait itself is now a channel's timeout, which a thread's park, as before, could end early). An independent review (read-only) found the dial loop's spin behind `adb reverse`, stale backlog connections under `--listen`, unbounded queues, pushed buffers outliving their assets and copies of large pushed reads on the app's thread before they were fixed.
  - **End to end, iOS Simulator** (the dev sandbox through `cargo xtask run --platform ios-sim --features dev -- --swatches`, headless, iPhone 17 Pro, iOS 26.3, the tool at 127.0.0.1:7811): editing the ramp swatch's base color and running `cargo xtask content --cook mobile` then `cargo xtask link push` sent 326 B of the 2.4 MB mobile pack (the material alone), the remount took 0.3 ms of its frame, and the material came back as generation 1, its reply after the upload; a timeline with a key's hour as text was refused (`default_day.ron: 37:22-37:23: Expected float`) and an edited exposure on the 10:00 key replaced the default day; `post.filter_strength` was set to 0.5; an edited `sky.wgsl` rebuilt the sky's program and six pipelines in 6–10 ms; undoing the edit and pushing again made generation 2. The app held 60 fps throughout (58 of 59 two-second windows at 60.0, one at 59.8). **Android emulator** (`sapphire_api36`, headless, through `adb reverse tcp:7811 tcp:7811`, the app dialing with `--live-link connect=127.0.0.1:7811` and the tool listening with `--listen`): the same material edit sent 326 B and came back as generation 1, then 2 when undone; the broken timeline was refused and the edited one replaced the default day; the cvar was set; the remount took 0.1–0.2 ms. The same on desktop (a debug build, offscreen `--bench`, hot reload off) sent 343 B of the 2.3 MB desktop pack, a broken `post.wgsl` was refused at its line, and a misspelled cvar was answered with the right name.
  - **Cost:** idle, nothing measurable. Sandbox `--bench 3000` (1920×1080, Desktop-High with TAA, Apple M5, release, `dev` builds with hot reload watching, and live link listening on 127.0.0.1:7811 on this branch) against a `dev` build of 6e5ad54, three sets of three alternating rounds on a machine loaded by four other packages' builds (load averages 12–40): every one of the eighteen runs made 407.6–407.7 allocations and 99.6–99.7 KB a frame, so the per-frame poll allocates nothing. CPU p50 in the two pairs of rounds that both ran clean (p99 under 1 ms) was 123.2 and 126.2 µs on this branch against 159.3 and 125.0 µs on the base; the other rounds swung from 124 to 484 µs in either build with outside load (p99 up to 10.5 ms). A pushed pack's remount takes 0.1–0.5 ms of its frame (iOS Simulator, Android emulator, a desktop debug build), as A8's does, and its changed assets then load as any asset does; a pushed shader edit rebuilds on the frame's thread, as hot reload's does.
- **As built (M1 X2 rehearsal, exit test 1 without a phone):** the exit run's procedure, rehearsed end to end on the desktop, the iOS Simulator and the Android emulator, is [m1-exit-run.md](m1-exit-run.md), with what a phone run needs and what only a device can prove. What the rehearsal added:
  - **Captures over the link (protocol version 2).** `RequestBody::Capture` asks for the next frame the app draws, answered with `ReplyBody::Captured(Capture)`: the frame's number, size and a PNG. The app renders that frame to a texture of its own instead of the surface (it isn't presented, so a capture costs one frame on screen), reads it back through the frame graph, swaps BGRA surfaces' channels, and encodes the PNG on a thread of its own (`sapphire-capture`), halving the image (2×2 means) until it fits a frame (`MAX_CAPTURE`, 1 KiB under `MAX_FRAME`): a 2622×1206 simulator frame came back whole, 2 MB in 0.4 to 0.6 s. Eight captures wait at most; more are refused. Captures need no device tool (`simctl`, `adb`), which an iPhone on iOS 17 or later doesn't have from the command line. A version 1 tool and a version 2 app refuse each other at the preamble, as designed.
  - **The tool:** `capture <png>`; `watch [<document>...]` also watches the named documents (X1 part 2a's `lookdev_day.ron`); and every command prints how long the device took to answer, from sending the request to its reply (`sapphire_time::Stopwatch`).
  - **`--offscreen` (sandbox, `dev` builds):** draws the scene at 60 frames a second without a window, display or focus until stopped, with hot reload and live link on, so the desktop leg runs headless and `cargo xtask link capture` shows what it draws. It is the offscreen app's third mode beside `--capture` and `--bench`, exclusive with both, and a non-`dev` build refuses it with the reason. Each frame is one `iterate` of the main callbacks, so SIGINT and SIGTERM end it as they do the window (a first cut that looped inside `init` never saw SDL's quit event and needed `kill -9`).
  - **`cargo xtask run --platform ios`** (and `bundle`): a physical iPhone. It builds `aarch64-apple-ios`, assembles the bundle as for the simulator and signs it with `--identity` and `--profile` (the profile's entitlements extracted with `security cms` and `plutil`, its application identifier checked against the bundle's, `embedded.mobileprovision` copied in), or ad hoc without them, which checks everything but the signing; `run` installs and launches through `devicectl` (`--device`), with the app's console in the terminal. No phone has run it yet.
  - **Push to visible, rehearsed** (push → reply, edit / undo, ms; the next frame shows it): desktop (offscreen, release `dev`, Desktop-High, 1280×720): cvar 15 / 9, time-of-day key 26 / 34, `post.wgsl` 2,436 / 44, `anime_character.wgsl` 1,666 / 85, character material 51 / 35, environment material 28 / 28, ramp 44 / 45. iOS Simulator (iPhone 17 Pro, iOS 26.3, Mobile-Low): 13 / 8, 19 / 50, 2,605 / 32, 2,072 / 74, 44 / 46, 42 / 68, 38 / 39. Android emulator (`sapphire_api36`, Mobile-Low, the Mac's GPU through MoltenVK): 17 / 18, 31 / 19, 3,554 / 102, 2,572 / 138, 28 / 48, 38 / 55, 49 / 41. Content adds its cook, 0.5 to 1 s; packs sent a few hundred bytes of the 11.6 MB mobile pack (3.4 KB for the ramp). On the desktop with hot reload alone, from saving to applied: documents 0.12 to 0.30 s, WGSL 0.13 to 0.14 s, content 0.66 to 0.79 s with its cook. Every edit showed in the captures (and the simulator's and emulator's own screenshots) and went again when undone.
  - **Findings, not fixed here:** a pushed shader's cold compile holds the frame for 1.7 to 3.5 s, since pipelines rebuild on the frame's thread (building them off it is M2 or M4 work, with the PSO warm-up); and the first `cargo xtask content` after editing a WGSL source or the default day takes 26 to 63 s, because `sapphire_pipeline` links `sapphire_render` and `sapphire_world`, which compile them in.
  - **On C7, X1 part 2a and post's lazy pipelines (2026-10-27).** Both days are timelines, so the sandbox took any pushed timeline as the day's: pushing `default_day.ron`, which the tool's `watch` does whenever it changes, replaced the look-dev stage's own day. The scene now knows the day it runs on (`Scene::day`), and a timeline replaces it only from that day's file, by name (`Scene::replace_day`, through which hot reload and live link both go); others are refused with the day's name and file. `cargo xtask run --platform desktop` held the cooked content locked while the app ran, so a `cargo xtask content` beside it waited until the app exited, which defeats hot reload; a `dev` build now leaves it unlocked (packs are renamed into place, which remounts already rely on). `--offscreen` prewarms post's variants as the capture and bench modes do (it is built on their stage), and pushed or reloaded screen effect presets prewarm theirs. Run again on the desktop with C7 in: the materials, the ramp, the character shader (re-anchored in C7's `character_pixel`) and the look-dev day's key all showed and went, at 2 to 97 ms a push but the character shader's cold compile (2.5 s); [m1-exit-run.md](m1-exit-run.md) has the numbers.

---

## 6. Cross-platform checklist

These concerns cut across modules. Each line names its owning module.

### 6.1 Graphics (`sapphire_rhi`, `sapphire_render`)

- **One shader source.** WGSL / WESL is translated by naga at runtime into MSL, SPIR-V or HLSL. Never fork shaders per OS. Branch on `GpuCaps` and the tier table instead.
- **Two texture cooks.** `desktop` (BCn) covers Windows, Linux and macOS; `mobile` (ASTC) covers iOS and Android. A device that is missing its expected format fails at boot with a clear message instead of silently decompressing.
- **Android:**
  - Require Vulkan 1.1 and skip GLES entirely.
  - Handle surface destroy/create on every pause.
  - **Verify** how wgpu handles swapchain pre-rotation on Android. If rotation isn't handled, the compositor rotates every frame, which costs performance.
- **Apple:** Metal only. Use Xcode's GPU frame capture and Metal System Trace in Instruments.
- **Windows:** default to Vulkan with automatic DX12 fallback (§2 note 2). Test both backends in CI.

### 6.2 Frame pacing and displays (`sapphire_platform`, `sapphire_time`, `sapphire_render`)

- **Desktop:** VRR-friendly FIFO by default, a user frame cap (30 / 60 / 120 / unlimited), and a 60 Hz sim with interpolation.
- **iOS:** ProMotion devices allow 120 Hz. Default to 60 fps on Mobile-High and 30 on Mobile-Low, and set the preferred frame-rate range so the display doesn't run at 120 Hz for a 60 fps game.
  - **Decision (M1 F7, ADR 0003; was Q13):** the engine owns the frame clock. SDL's main-callback link ran `SDL_AppIterate` at the display's full rate, and SDL's API reaches neither it nor the window link that asks for 80–120 Hz. `sapphire_platform` now drives SDL's own main-callback functions from a `CADisplayLink` of its own.
    - The link's `preferredFrameRateRange` is exactly the target rate. A ProMotion panel can drop to it, and the main thread wakes once per frame.
    - The target is the quality tier's `frame_rate`, which the app passes to `Platform::set_frame_rate` at startup and on every tier change.
    - A pure pacer (`sapphire_platform::pacing`) is fed the vsync each tick's frame will be shown at (`CADisplayLink.targetTimestamp`). It counts whole refreshes: each frame owes the target interval in refreshes and is due on the first refresh that pays it, and any overpayment carries to the next frame. So the target rate holds and is never exceeded on any panel: 60 fps takes 3, 2, 3, 2, 2 refreshes at 144 Hz and 2, 1, 2, 1 at 90 Hz. It holds back any tick that comes before its frame is due, so frames are never doubled. A late frame starts the count afresh.
    - The frame's time (`Platform::frame_delta`) is the time between the frames' vsyncs, so steady frames run whole fixed steps. In the background it is at most one target interval.
    - SDL's internals make an SDL upgrade deliberate: `sdl3-sys` is pinned exactly, and a compile-time assertion requires SDL 3.4.18.
    - Present-interval pacing was rejected. wgpu 30's Metal surface presents with `presentDrawable:`, with no minimum duration or present time. Skipping SDL's ticks would leave the panel at 120 Hz with no vsync timestamps.
    - Presentation stays FIFO: a frame shows at the first vsync after its GPU work. Timed presentation (wgpu presenting at a time, or `CAMetalDisplayLink`) is the follow-up if the device check below finds frames shown early.
  - **As built (M1 F7):** `Platform::frame_pacing` reports the source, target, display refresh, this frame's interval and the counters: frames on time, target intervals missed, frames doubled, ticks held back. They appear in the sandbox's 2-second `frame stats` log line (`pacing=…`, with `best_ms` and `worst_ms`) and in the overlay's stats panel (the Frame pacing rows under the frame-time graph).
    - On the iOS 26.3 simulator (60 Hz display), Mobile-Low held 30.0 fps with every frame interval 33.33 ms (base a560a84 ran the same tier at 60). `--tier mobile-high` held 60.0 fps at 16.67 ms.
    - A trip to the background and back resumed at once with 0 missed and 0 doubled.
    - A live `r.tier` change from 30 to 60 fps (over live link) moved the link to 60 in the same frame, and the window around it counted 0 missed and 0 doubled. The pacer doesn't measure the interval that ends a target change: it straddles both rates. Here that interval also held the tier's renderer rebuild (about 55 ms).
    - A probe that asked the link for twice the target had the pacer hold every second tick, keeping 33.33 ms intervals.
  - **Device check (open; needs a ProMotion iPhone and the user's Apple signing identity).** Run on an iPhone 15 Pro or later (8 GB, so Mobile-High). If at hand, also run on a 13 Pro or 14 Pro (6 GB, ProMotion at Mobile-Low) and a non-ProMotion iPhone (60 Hz panel at 30 fps).
    1. **Build and install.**
       - Build the bundle and the device binary:
         - `cargo xtask bundle --platform ios-sim --release --features dev` assembles `target/ios-sim/SapphireSandbox.app` with the mobile cook.
         - `cargo build -p sapphire_sandbox --release --features dev --target aarch64-apple-ios` builds the device binary.
       - Copy `target/aarch64-apple-ios/release/sapphire_sandbox` over the bundle's executable.
       - Add the provisioning profile for `dev.sapphire.sandbox` as `embedded.mobileprovision`. Then `codesign --force --sign "Apple Development: <name>" --entitlements <plist with application-identifier and team-identifier> SapphireSandbox.app`. The M4 Xcode project replaces these steps.
       - `xcrun devicectl device install app --device <udid> SapphireSandbox.app`.
    2. **Launch.** Launch with the Metal performance HUD and the stats overlay: `xcrun devicectl device process launch --device <udid> --console --terminate-existing --environment-variables '{"MTL_HUD_ENABLED":"1"}' dev.sapphire.sandbox --environment --debug-overlay=stats --frames 18000`. That is five minutes at 60 fps of the courtyard with the turntable spinning, so judder is visible.
    3. **Record.** Keep the console's `frame stats` lines. During minute two, record 60 s with `xcrun xctrace record --template 'Animation Hitches' --device <udid> --attach dev.sapphire.sandbox --time-limit 60s`. It shows the display's refresh rate and each frame's time on glass. Watch the HUD's present-interval graph.
    4. **Repeat** with `--tier mobile-low` (30 fps); with Low Power Mode on; and with three trips to the home screen and back.
    5. **Pass:**
       - The display runs at the target: 60 Hz for 60 fps, never 120; 30 or 60 Hz for 30 fps.
       - In steady log windows: `0 doubled`, `0 missed`, `best_ms` = `worst_ms` = the target interval, and `held` near 0, meaning the link honours the rate. Steady excludes the first 2 s after launch, a tier change and a return to the foreground.
       - The HUD and Instruments show present intervals of 16.7 ms (or 33.3 ms), with no alternating short and long pairs (8.3 / 25 ms, 16.7 / 50 ms) and no hitches in steady state.
       - Pacing resumes within one window after each return to the foreground.
    6. **Fail, and what follows:**
       - A 120 Hz panel at a 60 fps target: some link still asks for more. Find it in Instruments.
       - `doubled` above 0: a pacer bug.
       - Alternating present intervals while `missed` stays 0: frames are shown early. Implement timed presentation: a present gate that holds `present()` until one refresh before the frame's vsync, or wgpu presenting at a time or through `CAMetalDisplayLink`.
- **Desktop and Android (M1 F7):** unchanged. SDL's loop runs frames back to back and FIFO paces them. The same pacer measures each frame's start against the tier's rate, for the stats, and never holds a frame. The desktop frame cap and Android's limiter above will gate frames with the same pacer.
- **Android:**
  - Displays run at 60, 90 or 120 Hz. Use FIFO plus a frame limiter that divides the refresh rate evenly (for example 30 on 120 Hz → present every 4th vsync).
  - Hint the refresh rate with `ANativeWindow_setFrameRate` (API 30+).
  - Android's Swappy frame-pacing library doesn't fit wgpu's swapchain ownership, so don't plan on it.
- **Notches and safe areas:** handled by `sapphire_ui` anchors from platform insets.

### 6.3 Thermal, power and memory (`sapphire_platform`, `sapphire_render`, `sapphire_assets`)

- **Thermal input** comes from iOS `thermalState` and Android thermal headroom plus ADPF performance-hint sessions (report the target and actual work duration each frame).
- **Response ladder:**
  1. Dynamic resolution.
  2. Disable optional passes.
  3. Drop the tier.
  4. Offer a 30 fps lock.
- **iOS has no swap**, and the OS kills apps that exceed device-dependent memory limits. Check `os_proc_available_memory` at boot to pick budgets, and drop caches on `LowMemory`.
- **Android low-memory killer:** keep the background footprint small, and release GPU resources when backgrounded where practical.

### 6.4 Storage and content delivery (`sapphire_assets`, `sapphire_patch`)

- **Install model, following gacha-game practice:** ship a small app (boot pack, UI, the first zone), then download the remaining packs from a CDN in-game, with progress UI and resume.
- **Store size limits:** use Play Asset Delivery and the App Store's on-demand options only if store size limits require it. **Verify** the current limits in M4.
- **iOS:** mark downloaded packs as excluded from iCloud backup.
- **Android:** download into app-internal storage so packs can be memory-mapped.

### 6.5 Lifecycle (`sapphire_platform` → everyone)

On **background**:

- Stop submitting GPU work. This is mandatory on iOS.
- Suspend the FMOD mixer.
- Flush saves.
- Pause the simulation, using the world clock.
- Keep sockets for at most a few seconds.

On **foreground**:

- Recreate the surface.
- Resume audio.
- Show a "tap to continue" overlay if the player was in combat.

### 6.6 Input (`sapphire_input`, `game_ui`)

- **Default devices per platform:**
  - Desktop: KB/M plus gamepad, with glyphs following the last-used device.
  - Steam Deck: gamepad glyphs, and a 1280×800 layout check.
  - Mobile: touch plus optional gamepad. On-screen controls hide when a gamepad connects.
- **Text input:** SDL3 text-input events show the on-screen keyboard on mobile (for name entry and the dev console).
- **As built (M1 F4):** every device is live on every platform, through one set of bindings. `sapphire_input` reports the last-used device kind with the gamepad's glyph family (the Steam Deck counts as Xbox, whose A/B/X/Y positions it shares) and how many gamepads are connected; showing glyphs and hiding on-screen controls are `game_ui`'s (M3).
- **As built (M1 F5):** `sapphire_platform` forwards text input as `Event::Text` (UTF-8 in events of at most 32 bytes, split between characters, so events stay `Copy`), only while a window takes it: `Window::start_text_input` (which shows the on-screen keyboard on mobile; a `TextInputKind` of `Verbatim`, for names, commands and codes, turns off auto-capitalization and autocorrect, `Prose` keeps them), `stop_text_input` and `set_text_input_area` for the input method. The debug console is its first user; IME composition previews are not forwarded yet.

### 6.7 Store and platform compliance

- **iOS:**
  - No JIT.
  - App Store guideline 2.5.2 on downloaded code.
  - A privacy manifest (`PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy`) declaring required-reason APIs.
  - The ATT prompt only if you track users.
- **Android:**
  - 64-bit only.
  - 16 KB page-aligned `.so` files (use NDK r28+, plus a linker flag, §7.3).
  - Bump the target API level every year as Play requires.
- **macOS:** hardened runtime, codesigning, notarization.
- **Steam / Steam Deck:** controller support, legible text at 800p, and the Steam Runtime on Linux.

### 6.8 Accessibility (`sapphire_ui`, `sapphire_input`, `sapphire_render`, `sapphire_gameplay`)

- Subtitles with speaker names and a size setting.
- Full remapping, plus hold-to-toggle options.
- **Screen-shake and flash-intensity sliders.** Anime combat is flash-heavy, and these protect photosensitive players.
- Colorblind-safe attack telegraphs, with shape and icon as well as color.
- UI text scale.
- Adjustable auto-target strength on touch.

---

## 7. Repository layout and build

### 7.1 Layout

```
engine/
├─ Cargo.toml               # virtual manifest: [workspace.dependencies], profiles, lints
├─ rust-toolchain.toml      # pinned stable + all 7 targets
├─ .cargo/config.toml       # per-target linker flags, aliases (xtask)
├─ clippy.toml              # disallowed-methods (rule R2)
├─ deny.toml                # license policy, bans (bincode, archived crates)
├─ crates/                  # en_* engine and tool crates (flat)
├─ game/                    # game_app (bin/cdylib/staticlib), game_combat, game_characters, game_ai, game_ui
├─ platform/
│  ├─ android/              # Gradle project, GameActivity extends SDLActivity, manifest, FMOD jar
│  ├─ ios/                  # Xcode project, Info.plist, privacy manifest, entitlements, launch screen
│  ├─ macos/                # bundle Info.plist, entitlements, icons
│  ├─ windows/              # app manifest (DPI awareness), icon, installer config
│  └─ linux/                # .desktop file, Steam Runtime notes
├─ shaders/                 # WGSL / WESL sources
├─ content/                 # source assets + .meta sidecars; third_party/ with provenance (Git LFS later)
├─ xtask/                   # build automation, layer checker (layers.toml)
├─ docs/                    # this spec, ADRs (architecture decision records)
└─ .github/workflows/
```

### 7.2 Cargo profiles

```toml
[profile.dev]
opt-level = 1
debug = "line-tables-only"

[profile.dev.package."*"]
opt-level = 3                 # deps fast, your code debuggable

[profile.release]             # internal "profile" builds with Tracy
lto = "thin"
debug = "line-tables-only"

[profile.shipping]
inherits = "release"
lto = "fat"
codegen-units = 1
panic = "abort"
debug = "full"
split-debuginfo = "packed"    # PDB / dSYM / .dwp uploaded to Sentry, then stripped
```

- **Desktop dev builds** add an `sapphire_dylib` crate (`crate-type = ["dylib"]`) that re-exports the engine. `game_app` links it behind a `dynamic` feature, as in Bevy's dynamic-linking mode. This is the single biggest compile-time win.
- **Keep gameplay code in `game_app` during iteration**, because `subsecond` hot-patching only patches the top-level crate.
- **Linkers:** keep Apple's default linker (ld-prime) on macOS. On Linux, use mold or lld. Wild is Linux-only.

### 7.3 Per-target notes

| Target | How `game_app` is built | Packaging |
|---|---|---|
| macOS / Windows / Linux | `cargo build -p <app>` (bin) | `cargo xtask bundle --platform macos` gives an `.app` with `Contents/Resources/content`. Codesigning, notarization and Steam depots come in M4 |
| iOS | `cargo build -p <app> --target aarch64-apple-ios[-sim]` (bin; SDL is statically linked) | `cargo xtask bundle --platform ios-sim` gives an ad-hoc-signed `.app`. Device signing, plus an Xcode project for sentry-cocoa, the privacy manifest and store archives, come in M4 |
| Android | `cargo rustc -p <app> --lib --target aarch64-linux-android --crate-type cdylib`, with the NDK environment set by xtask | `cargo xtask bundle --platform android`: the Gradle project in `platform/android` takes the `.so` in `jniLibs/arm64-v8a`, SDL's Java glue (synced from `sdl3-src`) and cooked content in `assets/content`. `SandboxActivity extends SDLActivity`. FMOD's Java init is added in M2 |

`.cargo/config.toml` fragment for Android 16 KB pages (**Verify** against NDK r28+ defaults):

```toml
[target.aarch64-linux-android]
rustflags = ["-C", "link-arg=-Wl,-z,max-page-size=16384"]
```

### 7.4 `xtask` commands

| Command | Does |
|---|---|
| `cargo xtask check-layers` | Enforces §4.1 and rule R8 from `cargo metadata` |
| `cargo xtask cook --cook desktop\|mobile [--only <guid/zone>]` | Runs `sapphire_pipeline` with the content-hash cache |
| `cargo xtask run --platform desktop\|ios-sim\|ios\|android` | Builds, cooks, deploys and launches, with live link enabled |
| `cargo xtask package <platform> --profile shipping` | Produces store-ready artifacts and uploads symbols |
| `cargo xtask replay <file\|dir>` | Runs headless combat replays |
| `cargo xtask golden [--update] [--scene <name> \| --shard <i>/<n>]` | Runs golden-image render tests; CI splits the CPU rasterizers' scenes across runners with `--shard` |
| `cargo xtask link status\|cvar\|push\|capture\|watch` | Live link's desktop tool (§5.29): reads a running dev build's state, sets cvars, pushes packs (only what changed), documents and WGSL sources, captures the next frame the device draws as a PNG, or watches and pushes them as they change; each command says how long the device took to answer |

---

## 8. CI

| Trigger | Job | Runners |
|---|---|---|
| Every PR | fmt, clippy (with R2 lints), rustdoc (warnings denied, private items included), `check-layers`, `cargo-deny`, `cargo-nextest` | Linux |
| Every PR | Build desktop binaries | macOS, Windows, Linux |
| Every PR | Compile-only builds for iOS (device and simulator) and Android | macOS (iOS), Linux (Android) |
| Every PR | Combat replay suite | macOS, Windows, Linux |
| Every PR | Golden images: lavapipe (Vulkan) and WARP (DX12), required; Metal (paravirtual GPU), informational until stable | Linux, Windows, macOS |
| Nightly | Full cook of both `desktop` and `mobile`; package all 5 platforms | Mixed |
| Nightly | Device smoke: install, boot, load a zone, 2 minutes of scripted combat, collect frame times and memory | Self-hosted Mac mini with an iPhone and an Android phone attached |
| Weekly | Thermal soak: 30 minutes of combat on Mobile-Low devices; budget regression report | Same |

**Build caching:** `sccache` 0.18, plus `cargo-hakari` to unify dependency features across the workspace.

---

## 9. Quality gates

A change can't merge if any of these fail:

1. **Layering and license checks:** R1, R8, and `cargo-deny`.
2. **Determinism:** all combat replays reproduce their recorded checksum for the runner's architecture, on every desktop OS.
3. **Golden images:** within `nv-flip` tolerance per backend. Rendered with the sandbox's offscreen `--capture` mode (M0), so no window is needed.
4. **Allocations:** the zero-allocation assertion (R6, simulation thread inside the fixed step) during the replay suite.
5. **Budgets:** no budget in §3 regresses by more than 10% on the nightly device run without a recorded waiver.

---

## 10. Milestones

These windows are rough estimates for a small, senior team. A solo developer should expect them to stretch.

| Milestone | Window | Build | Exit test |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M0 Bring-up** | Months 0–2 | Workspace, layer rules and CI on 3 OSes. `sapphire_platform` (SDL3 main callbacks) and `sapphire_rhi` (Metal / Vulkan / DX12) on all 5 platforms. Skeletons of `sapphire_core`, `sapphire_math`, `sapphire_jobs` and `sapphire_time`. Tracy, `sapphire_crash`, and iOS Xcode / Android Gradle packaging. A glTF character with a basic two-tone character shader and hull outline. | **The same commit** runs the outlined two-tone character at target fps on Mac, Windows, Linux (including Steam Deck), an iPhone and an Android phone. Background → foreground survives on both phones, and a forced crash reaches Sentry from every platform. |
| **M1 Data path** | Months 2–6 | ECS, reflection and serialization; the `sapphire_assets` runtime. `sapphire_pipeline`: glTF / FBX, `ctt` BC and ASTC, meshopt, packs, content-hash cache, hot reload. Renderer v1: frame graph, compute skinning, forward lighting, shadows, the anime character recipe (track C), the world look (time of day, sky, fog, ambient probes, stylized environment shader) and the post stack. VRM import and anime map tools, `sapphire_input`, `sapphire_debug`, live link, golden-image tests. The plan is [m1-plan.md](m1-plan.md). | Editing a ramp, material parameter or time-of-day key shows up on desktop *and* phone without a restart. The look-dev scene meets the tier budgets on all 5 platforms. Golden images pass on 3 backends. |
| **M2 Combat slice** | Months 4–10 | `sapphire_anim` (ozz, inertialization, IK, spring bones), `sapphire_physics`, the combat core, party swap, camera director, one enemy AI, FMOD, VFX v1, touch controls, live link, and the timeline editor v1. | One character against one enemy **feels right** with a gamepad on desktop and with touch on a phone. Replays are deterministic in CI, and Mobile-Low holds 30 fps while thermally throttled. **This is the go/no-go gate.** If tuning still feels slow, take the off-ramp in the report: move gameplay and tools to Godot with gdext, and keep the Rust systems. |
| **M3 World and tools** | Months 8–18 | Zones and transitions, navigation, sequencer and dialogue, Luau, `sapphire_ui` with the HUD and menus, saves, localization, the editor document model with undo and play-in-editor, and the pipeline (PSO) inventory. | A zone with 3 encounters, a boss authored with no Rust changes, one cutscene, and a full HUD and menus in 2 languages, all on 5 platforms. |
| **M4 Ship readiness** | Month 12 onward | PSO warm-up, signed chunk manifests and `sapphire_patch`, quality tiers with dynamic resolution and thermal handling, the symbol pipeline, accessibility, and store compliance. | Zero shader hitches in a scripted playthrough. Patches download only changed chunks. Builds pass TestFlight, Play internal testing and Steam review. |

**M0 status (2026-10-24):**

- **Done and verified on 3 of the 5 platforms.** The same code renders the outlined two-tone mannequin at 60 fps on macOS (Metal), the iOS 26.3 simulator (Metal) and the Android 16 emulator (Vulkan through MoltenVK). It survives background → foreground on Android, and a forced crash leaves a valid minidump on macOS.
- **Since then (2026-10-24):** CI runs on GitHub, and Windows and Linux build and pass their tests there. Tracy is verified: a trace from the sandbox on the headless iOS Simulator records every engine zone and wgpu's. Release builds link on every Apple target (thin LTO used to drop a symbol SDL needs).
- **Deferred by decision (2026-10-24):** crash upload. Nothing needs it yet, so the Sentry backend was removed from `sapphire_crash`, which halves its dependency tree. Minidumps are still captured and kept for the next launch, and the backend comes back from git history once there is a project to send reports to.
- **Still open for M0 exit:**
  - A run on Windows and Linux hardware.
  - A physical iPhone, which needs a signing identity.
  - A physical Android phone.

**M1 exit run:** [m1-exit-run.md](m1-exit-run.md) is the procedure for exit test 1 and the phones' budget measurements: the six edits (ramp, character and environment materials, time-of-day key, cvar, WGSL), the commands per target (desktop headless, iOS Simulator, Android emulator, an Android phone through `adb reverse`, an iPhone through `cargo xtask run --platform ios` and `iproxy`), what to measure and record, and what only a device can prove. It was rehearsed on 2026-10-26 on everything but the phones (§5.29).

## 11. Open questions to resolve

| # | Question | Resolve by |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | ~~Does the SDL3 entry flow work from Rust?~~ **Answered in M0:** yes. `SDL_RunApp` runs on the iOS simulator, and an exported `SDL_main` with SDL's JNI symbols links for Android (see Android results in ADR 0002). | M0 spike |
| Q2 | ~~Android 16 KB page alignment~~ **Answered in M0:** NDK r28+ defaults already produce 16 KB-aligned `.so` files; the rustflag in `.cargo/config.toml` is belt and braces. | M0 |
| Q3 | Steam Deck: native Linux build, or the Windows build under Proton? Prefer native. | M0 |
| Q4 | Which Windows default backend, Vulkan or DX12, based on Intel / AMD / NVIDIA driver testing? | M1 |
| Q5 | wgpu on mobile and Apple: on-tile MSAA / transient attachments, timestamp queries inside passes on Apple GPUs, Android pre-rotation, Metal binary archives. **Partly answered in M0:** MSAA and depth are transient attachments, and Apple GPUs report the memory saving; the M5 has no timestamps inside passes. The iOS simulator lacks base vertex / first instance, indirect execution, cube arrays and comparison samplers, so the CPU-driven draw path avoids them (ADR 0002). **M1 plan:** mobile AA is 4× MSAA kept in tile memory through `TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT`, to be measured on Mali and Adreno; one-pass deferred stays impossible until wgpu gets input attachments (#10148). **M1 R7:** the phone tiers keep 4× MSAA as transient attachments, held there by a GPU test; the measurement on Mali, Adreno and Apple devices moves to M2. | M1 |
| Q6 | ~~Do hosted CI runners expose a GPU for Metal golden images?~~ **Answered (M1 plan):** yes. GitHub's macOS arm64 runners expose Metal as "Apple Paravirtual device", and wgpu runs its image tests there on every PR (shader validation is broken there; 3 vCPU). Goldens run per PR on lavapipe, WARP and `macos-26` Metal. | M1 |
| Q7 | Does ozz-animation-rs cover offline animation building, or is the C++ ozz tooling needed in the cooker? **Partly answered (M1 research):** it has no offline tools, so the cooker needs the C++ ozz tooling. It also needs **nightly Rust** (`portable_simd`), which conflicts with the pinned stable toolchain, so the runtime choice is open too. | Start of M2 |
| Q8 | FMOD license tier and cost; if blocked, the `kira` backend becomes primary. | M2 |
| Q9 | Device floors (A14 / Snapdragon 865 / 6 GB), measured while thermally throttled. | M2 |
| Q10 | iOS policy on downloadable scripts and content: legal and App Review guidance. | Before any live-service plan |
| Q11 | Current App Store and Play size limits, which shape the install-time vs CDN content split. | M4 |
| Q12 | ~~Android crash capture~~ **Answered in M0:** there's no workable monitor process on Android. `sapphire_crash` writes an async-signal-safe signal record and chains to `debuggerd`; symbolicated native crashes come from Sentry's Android NDK SDK in the Gradle project. | M0 |
| Q13 | ~~iOS frame pacing: engine-owned `CADisplayLink` or present-interval pacing?~~ **Answered in M1 (F7, ADR 0003):** an engine-owned `CADisplayLink` at the tier's rate drives SDL's own main-callback functions; a pure pacer holds back early ticks and measures intervals. Decided text, simulator results and the device check that remains are in §6.2. | M1 |
| Q14 | Android pre-rotation: wgpu doesn't pre-rotate, so SurfaceFlinger rotates our landscape buffer on portrait-native phones. Measure the cost on real devices before choosing whether to render pre-rotated. | M2 |

## Appendix A. Dependencies

Pin these in `[workspace.dependencies]`. Versions were checked against crates.io on 2026-10-24 during the research, except rows marked **unchecked**, which were added for this spec. Keep all `bevy_*` crates on the same 0.20.x.

| Crate | Version | Used by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sdl3-sys` | =0.7.2 (SDL 3.4.18; pinned exactly, ADR 0003) | `sapphire_platform` | SDL is zlib-licensed |
| `raw-window-handle` | 0.6 | `sapphire_platform`, `sapphire_rhi` | **unchecked** |
| `crash-handler` / `minidumper` | 0.8 / 0.11 | `sapphire_crash` | No iOS support; use sentry-cocoa there |
| `sentry` | ≥ 0.49.3 | `sapphire_crash` | Native minidump capture. Deferred: out of the tree since 2026-10-24 (M0 status) |
| `mimalloc` | latest | `sapphire_core` | |
| `bumpalo` / `bump-scope` | 3.20 / 2.4 | `sapphire_core` | std `Allocator` trait from Rust 1.100 |
| `slotmap` | 1.1 | `sapphire_core` | |
| `tracing`, `profiling`, `tracy-client`, `puffin` | –, –, 0.18, 0.20 | `sapphire_core` | No default features for `tracing` and `profiling` (no proc macros); `tracy-client` comes through `profiling` |
| `glam` | 0.33 | `sapphire_math` and up | The line `bevy_reflect` 0.20 reflects (`^0.33.2`), so math types reflect directly; moves with Bevy (M1 F1) |
| `libm`, `rand_pcg` | latest | `sapphire_math` | `libm` **unchecked** |
| `bevy_ecs`, `bevy_tasks`, `bevy_reflect` | 0.20 | `sapphire_ecs`, `sapphire_jobs`, `sapphire_reflect` | Pin per milestone |
| `serde`, `ron`, `rkyv`, `postcard` | 1, 0.12, 0.8, 1.1 | `sapphire_serialize` | **Never** `bincode` |
| `zstd`, `lz4_flex`, `memmap2`, `blake3` | 0.13 (libzstd 1.5.7), 0.14, –, 1.8 | `sapphire_assets` | M1 A4: zstd is the C library, not `ruzstd` (§5.10 gives the measurements); `lz4_flex` with its safe block codec only. `memmap2` waits for memory-mapped packs. The packer encodes with the same crates |
| `notify` | 8.2 | `sapphire_hot_reload` (dev builds, desktop), tools | M1 A8: 8.2.1, with a debounce of its own rather than `notify-debouncer-full` (§5.10). Only dev-only crates may bring it in (`xtask check-layers`) |
| `wgpu`, `naga` | 30 | `sapphire_rhi`, `sapphire_render` | |
| `wesl` | 0.6 | `sapphire_render` | `naga_oil` is archived |
| `encase`, `bytemuck` | 0.13, – | `sapphire_render` | `encase` through glam's `encase` feature; not `wgsl_bindgen` (built on archived `naga_oil`, no WESL) |
| `vrm-spec` | 0.1 | `sapphire_pipeline` | pixiv's VRM 0.x/1.0 and MToon types, Apache-2.0 (M1 VRM import) |
| `wgpu-profiler` | 0.28 | `sapphire_rhi` | Not used (M1 F5): the frame graph times its passes itself, see §5.20 |
| `dlss_wgpu` | 6.0 | `sapphire_render` (Windows / Linux) | NVIDIA + Vulkan only |

| FMOD Studio API | vendor SDK | `sapphire_audio` | Commercial license; own bindgen bindings |
| `kira` | 0.12 | `sapphire_audio` (fallback) | |

| `rerecast`, `landmass` | 0.4, 0.9 | `sapphire_nav`, `sapphire_pipeline` | Vendor both (small maintainer base) |
| `statig` | 0.4 | `sapphire_gameplay` | |
| `mlua` (feature `luau`) | 0.12 | `sapphire_script` | No `luau-jit` |
| `fluent`, `icu` | 0.17, 2.4 | `sapphire_loc` | |
| `reqwest`, `rustls`, `rustls-platform-verifier`, `ed25519-dalek` | latest | `sapphire_patch` (later) | **unchecked** |
| `jni` | latest | `sapphire_platform` (Android) | **unchecked** |

| `transform-gizmo`, `egui-snarl` | 0.11, 0.12 | `sapphire_editor` | |
| `gltf`, `ufbx` | 1.4, 0.11 | `sapphire_pipeline` | |

| `ctt`, `ktx2`, `meshopt` | 0.6, 0.5, 0.7 | `sapphire_pipeline`; `ktx2` also `sapphire_assets` | `basis-universal` is stale. ctt with only the bc7enc-rdo, ISPC (prebuilt) and astcenc encoders, and no `rayon` |
| `image`, `exr`, `rayon` | 0.25, 1.74, latest | `sapphire_pipeline` | Tools only. `image` with only its PNG, TGA and EXR decoders, and JPEG for VRM images (M1 A6); `exr` comes through it |

| `cargo-ndk`, `sccache`, `cargo-hakari`, `cargo-nextest`, `cargo-deny` | 4.1, 0.18, –, –, – | Tooling | `bacon` is AGPL (fine as a local tool, not as a dependency) |

**Keep out of the dependency tree:** `kajiya`, `rend3`, `naga_oil`, `generational-arena`, `oxidized_navigation`, `laminar`, `rrise`, `oddio`, `bincode`, `optick`, `rustybuzz`, `bevy_cobweb_ui`, `distill`, `physx-rs`, `specs`, `legion`. Encode this list as bans in `deny.toml`.
